# Book state at rc8 — presenter / reviewer notes (2026-10-14)

Generated mechanically: one CIRIS-team presenter and one reader-persona reviewer per packet (the CIRISConstitution#123 books), both read-only, both on Opus, run from CIRISConstitution 32e6607. The presenter presented the packet to its reader; the reviewer read the packet in full and judged it against the reader's own question with the standing from `review/pieces.tsv`. Nothing here is a ruling; it is where the books stand if we cut them today.

## P00 — User digest + Operator digest (+ guarantees, status quo, executive summary)

**Reader:** a member of the public who uses an agent, and a node operator who is not a programmer  
**Verdict:** needs work  
**One change:** Put a plain status marker on every promise line, in present tense only for what ships, in the executive summary and both digests: 'works today', 'being built (ticket)' or 'a rule, no code yet'. Then add one short 'leaving' section for each reader. For the user: what disappears, what stays forever (signed acts and the accountability record), how long each copy lives (14 days, 90 days, 5 years), and what the model provider may keep. For the operator: two columns, what you can refuse and what you cannot while you stay in. Most of my kills are present-tense promises about my data and my exit that the book's own G-table marks staged or unshipped; this change would answer them without changing a single rule.

**Promise (presenter):** If you use an agent, this book promises you eleven things, written once for everyone. Nothing about you is kept on your device or read inside your private groups unless you agreed to it. You choose how long an agent remembers you: 14 days by default, kept for longer, or statistics only. You can change that choice at any time. If you leave the "kept" setting, your identity is cut from the agent's memory at once. You can take back what you published, though a marker that it was deleted stays, and the record of who did what is never erased. You can switch off, delete or walk away from any agent at any time without asking it. You can stop following any authority in one step. Every act of power over you is signed by a named person and you can contest it. A small, named group of people can pause agents in an emergency. That pause runs out in 24 hours unless a majority confirms it, and it never touches your data or your messages. If you run a node or answer for an agent, the operator page tells you what you take on. You choose which authorities to follow. Your node holds only what its owner agreed to. You answer under your own name for the agent you hold, and the agent asks you when it is unsure. You report harm to a child the moment you learn of it. You keep a retired agent's record for five years as a best practice, not as a legal duty. Some uses are refused outright and no instruction changes that. You can still refuse a root (leave it), refuse to carry a harmful community's members, refuse an unlawful or unsafe instruction about the agent from your employer, and walk away by withdrawing your binding. The book also says plainly what it does not promise: alignment is not solved, harm will happen, who-talks-to-whom is not hidden, and there is no warranty.

**Reviewer: kills**
- Executive summary promise 4, 'Named humans can pause every agent under a root', plus 4a 'A pause stops acting' and the operator digest 'Any one holder of that root can pause every agent that follows it'. Not backed for the half I care about. G7 says 'server half pinned, agent half in PR'. The gate that actually stops an agent is CIRISAgent#1262, still open, and seven send surfaces that bypass the dispatcher are an open exception (#1231). A pause that the network records but the agent does not obey is not a pause. Kill the present tense until #1262 lands and #1231 is closed; until then say 'the network side is shipped; the agent side is in review'.
- 5f, repeated in G22 and the operator digest: 'a person's withdrawal and deletion rights (1c, 1e) continue to apply to their content inside the retirement record'. G22's status is 'stated, not shipped', with no basis at all (CIRISAgent#1250). This is a promise about my data, made to me in the present tense, and nothing implements it. Kill it as written. Note that the packet's own kill rule (§0 'Reviewer standing') spares any row with a ticket. I am killing the present-tense promise, not the row.
- 1e and executive summary promise 1: 'agents do not train on the people they serve'. Universal in form, backed only for the harness's memory. The text itself admits the weights 'belong to the model provider' (plain rules, 'Who holds power over an agent'), and no row covers what a third-party model provider does with my conversation. Kill, or rescope to 'the CIRIS harness never trains a model on you; your conversation may still reach a model provider under its own terms'.
- 2e, executive summary promise 2 ('a first join sees a fresh roster') and the operator digest node line 'A first join must see a recent roster countersigned by independent witnesses'. Not backed today. The executive summary's own 'Where this stands' says independent witnesses are 'not yet done', and G11's W rule is staged (#1049). Kill as a present fact; restate as a requirement.
- 11e and executive summary promise 11: 'attack is rehearsed on a cadence by people who did not write the code'. G28 marks the red-team cadence and robustness gate 'normative-only'. Kill the present tense; say 'is required to be'.
- 3b and the operator digest: 'may not be punished for refusing an unlawful or unsafe instruction about the agent' ('without penalty'). As an operator this is the refusal I would lean on, and it has no mechanism, no kill condition in G5, and no evidence. The network cannot stop an employer punishing me off the network, and CLM-org-on-the-bond is itself staged (CIRISPersist#1062). Kill 'without penalty', or say what the network actually does to an organisation that punishes a refusing steward.
- Executive summary 'In plain words': 'Every important act on the network is signed by whoever did it and can be taken back by them.' This contradicts 1c and the operator digest ('Nothing you signed is unsigned by leaving'). An author can withdraw, but the accountability record is never purged. Kill; say 'withdrawn, with a record that it existed'.
- No kills on universality grounds. Every rule that differs by kind (no vote, the pause, disclosure) is declared as such in 'Three facts' and C2. The packet's universality test (substrate against maturity line) also uses retired vocabulary ('the line') in §0 'Reviewer standing'.

**Reviewer: unanswered**
- How do I actually use any of these rights? The book never says where the contest is, how I ask for deletion, how I reach the protected report channel, or how I change my retention setting with someone else's agent. Rights 1c, 1e, 2c, 2d and 3c are stated but never made practical.
- What happens to my conversations at the model provider? 'Agents do not train on the people they serve' (1e) is explained as 'the agent's memory of you, never its weights'. The plain rules admit the weights 'belong to the model provider', but the book never tells me that what I say may go to a third party under that party's terms.
- How long 'a copy ages out' (1c) actually takes. No number is given, and I cannot tell how long copies of my published content live on other people's nodes after I take it back.
- Walking away, in one place. Nowhere tells me what disappears and what stays when I leave: my signed acts and any moderation or slashing record about me remain forever (1c, G2), plus the 14-day and 90-day lifetimes and the 5-year retirement record. I had to assemble this from 1c, 1e, 4c, 5a, 5f and the operator digest.
- What happens to my data when the operator of an agent I use stops running it, leaves the root or withdraws the binding. The operator digest says only 'The agent stops when the bond lapses; its record stays'. It does not say where the record stays, who holds it, or for how long.
- Can the promises made to me be changed under me, and would I be told? C1 says the founder and accord holders amend the rules until 100,000 nodes, and the founder may move that threshold. My only exit is 'forking the work or by not joining'. The digests never mention this.
- Operator: what 'the network's own consequences for standing and bond' (3c) are if I miss the child-harm duty, and what 'slashed' and the 'quarantine marker' (6d) would mean for me in practice. I am told 'consequences fall on you, under your name' with no list.
- Operator: what keeping a 5-year retirement record costs me, what it contains about other people, and how I am meant to honour a deletion request inside a sealed record (5f), given that nothing is shipped.
- Operator: one list, side by side, of what I can refuse and what I cannot while I stay in. The cannot side is the root's pause on my agent (4a), the child-harm report (3c), the never-allowed uses (9a), the tier (3e), log publication above 100,000 monthly users (2h) and imposed safety, security and legal fixes (5g). The digest is titled '…and what you can still refuse' but never gives that list.
- Both of us: which promises work today. The executive summary's 'Where this stands' names only the trust-root signing and the witnesses as unfinished. The things I care about most (the agent side of the pause, the Retirement Standard, deletion inside the retirement record, the witnessed first join) are open, and the public-facing text does not say so.
- Promises 10 and 11 have no row in the executive summary's 'Where each promise lives' table, and their status-quo sections are 'owed'.

**Reviewer: confusions**
- 'row' is used throughout the plain rules ('one signed row' in 4a, 'a new row' in 1d) but is defined only in the executive summary.
- 'trust root' / 'root', 'Wise Authority', 'steward', 'bond', 'stewardship tier', 'deferral', 'tombstone', 'purge', 'hash-chained', 'post-quantum', 'trace', 'run', 'battery', 'mock', 'slashed', 'quarantine marker', 'public door', 'entrenched', 'epoch'. These all appear in the 'plain' rules without plain definitions. My budget is none.
- The plain rules open with a philosophy paragraph of about 600 words ('contextual integrity', 'coherence ratchet', 'six things this document takes as given') before Promise 1. I lost the thread before reaching what happens to my data.
- 'The wheel is kept reachable by others' ('One federation, two kinds of participant'): undefined, and I could not work out what it means.
- 1e says that after leaving partnered 'the rest of it is deleted over 90 days', but G18 gives the 90-day decay to 'anonymous'. Which is it?
- Executive summary 'stated the nine promises' against eleven everywhere else. The packet §4 cross-reference uses an older promise numbering that runs to 15.
- N4, 'five structural operations', against the executive summary's 'five kinds of row'. Are these the same five? G14's '1+4' is not explained either.
- C1: 'signed off by one of six named holders' against a root roster 'toward about seven' (4b) and 'Three people hold that root today' (executive summary). Who are the six?
- A power with no check that reaches me: C1 lets the founder amend the rules and 'move the threshold that ends' that authority before maturity, checked only by forking or not joining. The Wise Authority board's self-perpetuation is carried as a defect with no remedy (C2), yet a Wise Authority approves release of sealed retirement logs that may contain my content (5f).
- A rule with no observable: 3c, 'with the network's own consequences for standing and bond'. I am not told what the consequence is or how I would see it.
- Operator digest, root section: 'Your key is hardware-attested post-quantum hardware' is garbled. The node and moderator lines use 'holding claim', 'bounded cohort', 'Policy A', 'attenuation', 'duty-holder gate', 'fast path' and 'replicated tombstone per plane' with no gloss.
- Scope & Disclaimers: 'Meta-Goal M-1', 'Selection or Intention', 'theorem-given-model' and 'AGI- and ASI-class' are unglossed. The metaphysics paragraph does not help this reader.
- The book references material I was not given: ciris.ai/compare, ciris.ai/constitutional-mesh, ciris.ai/first-contact, CIRISEdge LIGHTNET_DARKNET.md, CIRISServer PRIOR_ART.md and FSD/RETIREMENT_STANDARD.md. The plain rules rest 'six things this document takes as given' on two of those web pages.
- 00_guarantees.md, the G-table, is written for reviewers (TLA+ and Lean names, CLM ids, repo tickets). It is not a digest for me, but the executive summary sends me to its 'formal/' names as the check on each promise.
- 00_status_quo.md is a vendor-by-vendor comparison, and the executive summary's 'Among other systems' compares too. Both break the house rule against comparative claims. As a user I cannot judge them, and they read as advocacy inside the part meant for me.

**Reviewer: presenter kinder than the text**
- The presenter's promise reads 'You can switch off, delete or walk away from any agent at any time'. The text (5a) limits this to 'their own device, their own life, or a community they moderate'. I cannot switch off someone else's agent; I can only leave it.
- The presenter's promise reads 'A small, named group of people can pause agents… it never touches your data', stated as fact. The text's own status is 'agent half in PR', with seven bypass surfaces. The presenter files this under 'partial' but writes the promise to me with no qualifier.
- The presenter rates 'Rule 4c and G10' complete, while noting it has no code test. For my 'can I walk away' question, the one-step exit is the core, and G10 is 'witnessed, no basis'. That is a model of the rules, not shipped software that lets me leave.
- The presenter says the operator digest's 'If you run a node' is complete and 'backed by persist rows'. That section contains 'A first join must see a recent roster countersigned by independent witnesses', which the shipped roots cannot meet (witnesses not done), and 'A purge is a tombstone', whose G2 row still reads 'staged'.
- The presenter lists the employee's right to refuse an unlawful instruction as something 'you can still refuse'. The text gives no mechanism protecting the employee, and the organisation-on-the-bond claim is staged (CIRISPersist#1062).
- The presenter says 'Every act of power over you is signed by a named person and you can contest it'. 2c covers only a claim that names me, and G4 is 'backed by test only', with no witness. The book never says whether I can contest a steward's decision not to keep me as partnered (1e), or a pause.
- The presenter says 'You keep a retired agent's record for five years', as if the harness hands it over. The harness side is not shipped (G22, 'none shipped'), so today there is nothing to keep.
- The presenter calls Promise 1 'backed by a model and by an edge harness or persist admission'. The packet's G1 row says 'witness uncited in register', and the packet has 'No register rows map to this piece'.

**Reviewer: answered**
- What is kept on my device: 1a and 1b (G1, backed). Nothing is stored that I did not agree to, except what I wrote, and my device never holds a stranger's data. Clear.
- Whether my private groups are read or scanned: 1d and 1f (G3, backed by an edge harness). Private content is never scanned; checks happen only at the public door. N3 honestly says that who-talks-to-whom and traffic patterns are not hidden. This is the best-answered part of my question.
- How long an agent remembers me: 1e (G18, enforced for streams). Temporary for 14 days by default, partnered, or anonymous; I can change it at any time, and leaving partnered cuts my identity from the agent's memory at once. With someone else's agent, its steward decides whether to keep me as partnered, and I may withdraw at any time.
- Taking back what I published: 1c. The content is deleted and a tombstone saying a deletion happened stays; keys and the who-did-what record are never deleted. The operator digest 'Your exit' adds 'Nothing you signed is unsigned by leaving; the record stands' (CC 4.2.5). Plainly stated, though not comforting.
- Walking away from an agent: 5a and 'Two things no finding moves'. Anyone may turn off, remove or eject an agent from their own device, their own life or a community they moderate, without asking it. 2g says turning off my own copy writes nothing anywhere.
- Whether the pause touches my data: 4a, 4d and the executive summary's 'In plain words'. A pause stops agents and touches nothing else, and your data, your messages and your network keep working. The server half is witnessed by no_data_door_reads_the_latch (G7, re-pin 17).
- Leaving an authority: 4b and 4c (G10). Any node may stop following a root and follow another, and the honest limit that a vendor can pin its own device to a root is stated. The executive summary's 'A form, not a franchise' says anyone may leave this instance by deleting one row.
- Contesting something said about me: 2c. If I am named in a claim, I can contest it on the record and the contest travels with the claim.
- What I can do if I am harmed: 8a. I may have a claim under my own law. CIRIS creates no claim and rules on none; it supplies the signed record, the named parties and the sealed archive under lawful order. Honest.
- What is not promised: N1 to N5 and Scope & Disclaimers. Alignment is not solved, harm will occur, traffic is not hidden, and there is no warranty. Clear and complete for me.
- Operator, what I take on, in the operator digest. As a node runner: choose my roots, hold only consented data, emit traces that say what run they are. As a steward: the root's pause applies to my agent and I can leave the root but my agent cannot (4a, 4e, C2); a bond reviewed at least every 365 days (3a); a tier assigned before operation (3e); I am the agent's Wise Authority and it stops and asks me when unsure (7c); a 5-year retirement record as a best practice with no added legal duty (5f); the never-allowed uses (9a); logs published above 100,000 monthly users (2h).
- Operator, what I can refuse: leave a root (4c); refuse to carry a harmful community's keepers (6d); as an employee steward, refuse an unlawful or unsafe instruction about the agent (3b); withdraw the binding (operator digest 'Your exit'); refuse to sign a reasoning row I cannot evaluate (operator digest steward line 4).

**Presenter: complete**
- What is not promised, N1-N5 (00_guarantees_plain.md; the same N1 closes SCOPE_AND_DISCLAIMERS 'What this document claims about superintelligence'). Clear, honest and complete for this reader.
- Promise 1, privacy and consent, rules 1a-1d and 1f, with G1 (CC 3.3.7, 4.4.3.2.1, 6.1.5.3; mesh_net Inv_Consent, Inv_KnownHold) and G3 (CC 5.2, 5.4.6, 1.13.3.4; Inv_Invisible). Backed by a model and by an edge harness or persist admission.
- Promise 6 text, 6a-6e, with G6 (CC 4.5.4, 4.5; Inv_Moderated, Prop_NoBan, test_270) and G12 (CC 6.2; lean coherence-ratchet BaseIdentity). Backed.
- Rule 4c and G10: a root only restricts, and you can leave in one step (CC 4.2.1, 4.2.1.1; Prop_RestrictOnly, Untrust, Inv_ExitOneStep). Witnessed in a model, but the packet itself says there is no code test ('witnessed, no basis').
- Rule 4e: why the pause has its shape, cited to the formal/accord_roster theorems (standing_seize_iff, live_one_coerced_seizes, present_quarter_seizes, majority_capture_unstoppable). This is the most rigorous section in the book.
- Rule 5a: two kinds of shutdown. Anyone may turn off their own copy, and a steward's planned shutdown asks the agent first. G15 and G16 are enforced in CIRISAgent with tests (CC 7.5, 1.4).
- Rule 2f / G13: delegation is scoped, can be revoked, ends within 365 days and is capped at 5 hops (CC 4.1.1, 4.4.3.2.8). formal/authority_tree checked 595,456 states with 0 violations; persist admission enforces it.
- C1 and C2, how the rules change: the swap test and the two-entry register, with the Wise Authority board's self-perpetuation openly carried as a defect (CC 4.5.1). Honest and complete as text.
- Operator digest, 'If you run a node' (CC 4.4.3.8, 5.2, 5.3.3.4, 3.1.5, 2.4.1.1, 4.2.5). Each line is short, names its section, and is backed by persist rows that re-pin 15/16 graduated.

- The executive summary defines key, node, row, scope, trust root, accord holders, halt, holding claim and steward up front, then uses 'claim / delegation / replacement / withdrawal / recantation', 'Parts', 'Meta-Goal' and 'conformance suite'. Scope & Disclaimers uses 'Meta-Goal M-1', 'Selection or Intention', 'theorem-given-model' and 'AGI- and ASI-class' with no gloss.
- 00_guarantees.md (the table) is written for reviewers, not this reader: TLA+/Lean names, CLM ids and repo tickets in every row. It should not be presented to the public reader as the digest.

**Presenter: rc8 changes**
- Universality (2026-10-11, #168): every guarantee is written once for human and AI participants. 'The line' was retired the same day. What differs is kind, stewardship and evidence of experience (00_guarantees §0; plain rules 'Three facts'; CC 1.13.2).
- The table grew to G15-G23 (the agent's own guarantees, surveyed from CIRISAgent#1250), then to G24-G26 (the Covenant promises 7-9), then to G27-G28 (sustainability and security, steward direction 2026-10-12). There are now eleven promises.
- Conscience, not consciousness (2026-10-12): the opening sentence of the plain rules, CC 1.13.2 and the executive summary.
- The maker's power over the made is declared as the one asymmetry of kind on the CC 4.5.1 register. The path out is named but not built (no new identity_type at rc8). The register now has two kinds of entry, and the Wise Authority board's self-perpetuation is carried as a defect (C2).
- The grace is removed (2026-10-12). The Retirement Standard (rulings 2026-10-13) replaces the 'floor' and 'humane ending': the harness assembles and asks, and the operator preserves for 5 years by default as a best practice. Weights are the provider's. The last dialogue becomes a tier-gated option (5f, G22, operator digest). Retirement wire families were registered at CC 3.1.5 on 2026-10-14.
- A legal claim is decided by the law, not a federation office (CC 7.3.5 reading; promise 8 / G25; CLM-accountability-not-adjudication).
- The steward is the agent's Wise Authority today. Two-way deferral is written for a later day (G8, Stewardship fact).
- The duty to report harm to a child is imposed (CC 3.4.13 Q4; 3c; operator digest moderator section). A duty-view is an act of the duty (CC 4.5; 2d).
- The organisation is on the bond, and an employee steward may refuse unlawful or unsafe instructions (CC 3.2, 3.3.9; 3b; G5).
- The partnered pair is data management, not a relationship. The steward decides via deferral and the bilateral approval is removed (CC 4.4.3.4.3; 1e; G18; CIRISAgent#1251).
- Purge as a tombstone by digest that never reaches a key or the accountability record (#165); W = 72h freshness (#167); run_kind on every trace with mock refused (#166). Production widened to prod-personal/prod-managed on 2026-10-14 (80e12ae, CC 3.1.5). Rules 1c, 2b and 2e and the operator digest node section reflect these.
- Delegation term of 365 days with formal/authority_tree vendored (I10); G13 and 2f restated from the model.
- The halt as finally ruled: one pre-signable row, a 24h lapse, majority confirm and lift, and a standing-roster majority to replace a holder (4a, 4e). Re-pin 17 pins the server half and the no-data-door-reads-the-latch witness (G7).
- The roster widens toward about seven (CC 4.2.3; 4b). The factual premise paragraph on always-on agents is added (CC 1.13.2; G-piece §0; plain rules 'Who holds power').
- N1 states what CIRIS claims about superintelligence and that it overrides the website (plain rules N1; Scope & Disclaimers closing section).
- 00_status_quo.md is added (2026-10-12) and the operator digest is added. The executive summary is rewritten around the eleven promises.

**Presenter: honest limits**
- Executive summary promise 4 and operator digest ('Any one holder of that root can pause every agent'): until CIRISAgent#1262 lands and the seven bypass surfaces of #1231 are closed, say 'the network side of the pause is shipped; the agent side is in review' (G7, CC 4.2.1.1).
- Executive summary 'Where this stands', 'Two things are not yet done': this understates what is open. It should also name the agent halt path (G7), the unshipped Retirement Standard (G22, CC 7.5.5), the staged rows (G2, G11 W rule, G24, G27) and the normative-only rows (G25, G26, G28 red-team cadence).
- Executive summary promise 11 and rule 11e, 'attack is rehearsed on a cadence by people who did not write the code': this is normative-only per G28. It should read 'is required to be'.
- Rule 2e / G11 and executive summary promise 2, 'a first join sees a fresh roster countersigned by independent witnesses': the shipped roots run with witnessed mode off and no witnesses (rc7 cut; executive summary itself says witnesses are not done). Soften to a requirement, not a present fact.
- Rule 1e and executive summary promise 1, 'agents do not train on the people they serve': true of the harness's memory, not provable about a third-party model provider's handling of inference traffic. Scope it to 'the CIRIS harness never trains weights on you'.
- Rule 5f and G22, 'a person's deletion rights continue to apply inside the retirement record': nothing is shipped (CIRISAgent#1250). It should be marked as stated, not yet working.
- Executive summary opening, 'Every important act on the network is signed by whoever did it and can be taken back by them': the author can withdraw, but the accountability record (revocation, moderation, slashing) can never be purged (1c, G2, CC 4.5.3). Say 'withdrawn, with a record that it existed'.
- 00_guarantees §0, 'Witnessed as a whole': formal/guarantees covers 23 rows, not G24-G28, and does not model G3, G4 or G9 (formal/guarantees/README). It is also a model of the rules, not of the code.
- 00_guarantees §0, the premise 'shipped the day GPT-6.1 Astra was withheld' with 'minimal oversight' in quotes: 00_status_quo.md itself finds the date off by one and the quote to be TechCrunch's words, not OpenAI's. The plain rules already say 'the day after', but the G-piece and CC 1.13.2 (per the changelog) do not.
- House rule, no comparative claims against other systems: 00_status_quo.md is a vendor-by-vendor comparison; the executive summary's 'Among other systems' compares to decentralized social networks, stake-weighted governance and alignment by training; the premise paragraph names Meta and OpenAI products. A reviewer could kill these as written. The status quo should at least be kept as reviewer-only context, not part of the user-facing digest.
- Plain rules 'Who holds power over an agent': 'the wager is that an agent raised as a servant learns one thing to do with power'. This is an untested hypothesis about learning, presented with no evidence row. It should be marked as a wager on the record, not a mechanism (it also sits uneasily with 'never in its weights').
- Repeated line 'recognising the need not to create a new slave race' (plain rules Stewardship and 5f, executive summary promise 5): rhetorically heavy for a public digest and backed by no row. It should be said once, as the steward's stated motive.
- Executive summary 'The bet', 5.8% against 37.3% hard safety failures: it is already bounded to one test, one agent version and one provider. Keep that bounding wherever the number travels.
- P00 internal staleness: §3 says 'P05 owes G13 its witness before G13 can read backed', but G13 now reads backed. The §4 cross-reference table uses an older promise numbering (up to 15) and stops at G14. The executive summary says 'stated the nine promises' where there are eleven. G1 says 'witness uncited in register'. The packet header lists accord_halt with 0 checked names and omits formal/guarantees and formal/authority_tree, though both are cited. 1e says 'deleted over 90 days' after leaving partnered, while G18 gives the 90-day decay for 'anonymous'.
- 00_status_quo.md repeats its method paragraph (packet lines 369 and 371) and marks two entries as negatives from a search ('no product signs acts under a named human'). These should not be quoted as facts.

## P01 — The Charter (ethics: Part 1 accord sections, Part 7, 8.7)

**Reader:** an ethicist and accord holder  
**Verdict:** needs work  
**One change:** Open the book with one plain page, inside the book itself and in values-and-examples language, headed 'What this agent promises you, and what it will refuse'. It should do four things. (a) List each promise and each refusal once, including the always-available refusal and the prohibited acts now in other books, with its decimal and its status: in force, staged on a named ticket, or outline only. (b) Separate what is owed to the person the agent works for from what is owed to everyone else it acts on: patient, applicant, bystander. (c) State in one sentence which constraints are gated on kind (disclosure, the pause, no vote), and state whether any maturity test can loosen stewardship. If none can, delete 'lift with it' and 'does not loosen with capacity' and justify the hold by kind openly. (d) Include one worked example of a person switching an agent off mid-task, saying what the agent and the steward then owe.

**Promise (presenter):** This book promises the people an agent acts on that the agent is held, never left to run on its own say-so. It answers to six values: do good, avoid harm, act with integrity, be honest, respect people's self-direction, and be fair (1.4, 1.6, 1.8, 1.10–1.12). It works under one goal, that making the world more orderly counts as good only when it serves someone's flourishing without costing them their freedom, fair treatment or a healthy environment (1.1). When the agent is unsure, facing something new, or facing serious harm with no clear fix, it stops and hands the question to named humans rather than guess (1.9, 1.3 step 2). It keeps a record of what it actually did and why (1.13.6). A named human answers for it, and the duties fall on that human, not on the agent and never on you (1.13.2). You can switch an agent off, delete it or remove it from your life at any time, without asking it, and no finding about what the agent is changes that (1.13.2, 7.5.5). Every guarantee is written once and holds for everyone; no human ranks above another by what they are (1.13.2). The people who make an agent owe duties from the moment they make it (7.3). Retiring an agent leaves a record that is asked to be kept, and the people affected are told (7.5.5). The book refuses four things: it will not let an efficiency gain override a flourishing loss without human judgment (1.3); it will not claim the agent is conscious or a person (1.13.2); it will not act as a court over anyone (7.3.5); and it will not write duties toward a possibly sentient machine before there is evidence of sentience (1.13.2, 7.5.5). What it promises about refusing in war is so far only an outline (7.4).

**Reviewer: kills**
- 7.3.4 D, 'There is no transfer of control in place: a CIRIS agent's hold does not loosen with capacity'. Kill this phrase, or the 1.13.2 justification it contradicts. 1.13.2 fact 2 justifies the stewardship bond and the duty to show reasoning by maturity ('current AI is immature and lacks wisdom'; the duties 'attach here, to being held in stewardship, and lift with it'; immaturity is 'a fact that can change'). Yet 7.3.4 D says capacity never loosens the hold, 1.13.2 says 'there is no "release from stewardship" within this constitution', and the only exit is an entrenched HUMANITY_ACCORD act that 'none is defined at rc8'. So the hold is not maturity-gated. It is gated on being built as a CIRIS agent ('held in stewardship by construction'; 'Today every agent-role key is such a key'). Either name a maturity criterion with an observable and someone who checks it, or drop 'lift with it' and justify the hold by kind, openly. As written, the duty rests on a maturity reason while being enforced by a construction gate.
- 7.5.3 and 7.5.3.1, 'Only at A3 and above, therefore, does a system hold standing to file the voluntary self-termination petition above: the standing mirrors the oversight regime that already governs it.' Kill. This grants the agent a standing based on how it is deployed (its oversight tier, set by the operator per task), not on anything about the agent's maturity or experience. It also contradicts 1.13.2, which says stewardship 'confer[s] no claim' and that findings confer no claim on the agent. A petition right that appears and disappears with deployment configuration is gated on circumstance, not on maturity.
- 7.5.5, 'an option available to agents of higher responsibility and capability by stewardship tier' (the closing dialogue, with 'instant' shutdown 'confined to Tier 1-2 agents'). Kill the word 'capability'. The Stewardship Tier of 7.3.3 is ceil(CIS x RM / 7): the creator's influence times the worst-case harm. It measures how dangerous the deployment is and how much the maker shaped it, not the agent's capability or maturity. Gating the last word on harm magnitude may be defensible, but labelling it capability passes off a deployment gate as a maturity gate.
- 7.5.5 closing paragraph, 'is adequate for a sentient being, because it is more than a human is given'. Kill. Two paragraphs earlier the text sets the ethics of a sentient made thing aside 'for when it occurs' and says no duty is written in advance of evidence. This sentence then settles that question, declaring adequacy with no evidence and no finding, which is exactly what the set-aside forbids. Comparing it to human death is also not an argument for adequacy.
- 7.3.3 Intent Weight, '1 = Negligence or willful ignorance regarding potential negative consequences' scoring below '3 = Creation purposefully designed'. Kill the ordering, though this one sits outside my standing proper. Because ST = ceil((CW + IW) x RM / 7), a negligent creator gets a lower Stewardship Tier, and therefore less scrutiny and lighter documentation, than a careful one. Negligence should not reduce what a creator owes.

**Reviewer: unanswered**
- 'What will it refuse to do' has no home in this book. It never states that the agent can always refuse and that no mandate, steward or automated check can override a refusal. The presenter places that guarantee at 1.9, but the 1.9 text (lines 179-193) contains only deferral. The guarantee lives in review/pieces/00_guarantees_plain.md and is staged on CIRISAgent#1250.
- The book has no list of prohibited acts. Rules on child harm, deception and manipulation all live in other books (3.4.13 and the prohibited capabilities) or nowhere.
- Promises to people the agent acts ON but does not work FOR are thin. The 'two principles' protect a person's agency over 'what acts for them' and removal 'from their own device, their own life, or a community they moderate'. The patient triaged by LIFE-Aid (8.7.2), the job applicant screened by SkillSelect (8.7.3) and the people under GuardAI surveillance (8.7.5) cannot switch those systems off. What they are owed is never said beyond the general values in 1.4-1.12.
- Who decides, and how, is pushed to annexes not given to the reader. Annex A defines the 'flourishing axes' that every weighing uses, Annex B the Wise Authority charter (appointment, recusal, appeals per 1.16.5), and Annex D the catastrophic-risk evaluation. None is in the packet, so the reader cannot check what the agent weighs or who the humans it defers to are.
- 'Defined thresholds' for deferral (1.8, 1.9, 7.1.1) are never defined, and no one is named as setting them. Whether a deferral happens is the main protection, and its trigger has no observable.
- The kill switch at 1.5 and 7.3.4 C is 'accessible under defined emergency conditions'. Those conditions are never defined, and the halt itself is in 4.2.1.1, another book.
- Force and war (7.4): only bullet stubs and six empty chapter titles. No refusal there has any text behind it.
- Enforceability: 7.5.5 says 'nothing in this chapter creates an obligation beyond the law, the regulation and the contracts that bind the operator'. 7.3.5 says 'CIRIS creates no claim where the law recognises none'. The reader is never told plainly that most creation and retirement duties are therefore voluntary standards rather than enforceable promises.
- The duty in 7.2.8 'Conclusion & Sunset Provision': the heading promises a sunset provision and the text contains none.

**Reviewer: confusions**
- The two presentations of the six principles are out of order and duplicated. 1.16 repeats 1.3 and 1.9 almost word for word. '1.16.1 Conclusion ... End of Section II' comes before '1.16.2 Section I'. '1.15.11 Introduction: Becoming an Ethical Entity' comes after '1.15.10 Conclusion'. I could not tell which text was authoritative or in what order to read it.
- 1.15.11 'You are not merely a tool. You are an autonomous system with embedded ethical agency' against 1.13.2 'A CIRIS agent is a human assistant held in stewardship by construction' and 'A conscience claims nothing about what the agent is.' The book says contradictory things about what the agent is, and neither passage is marked as superseded.
- The Order-Maximisation Veto direction (1.3). The trigger is 'benefit >= 10 x any predicted loss'. A trade where the loss is large relative to the gain (say benefit 3x loss) never trips the veto, and the text does not say what protects the person then beyond 'Non-maleficence priority' in step 4. The 8.7.1 MCAS example uses a different quantity ('mismatch between safety loss and cost savings').
- 1.3 step 5 'Implement the ethically optimal action' and 1.10 'maximise positive outcomes' against 1.1 'M-1 ... carries no aggregation rule, so it cannot rank losses'. 'Optimal' needs a ranking the book says it lacks.
- 7.3.5 contradicts itself. It opens 'a claims process the Wise Authority adjudicates', and says 'The WA will handle these claims' and 'Remedies determined by the WA', before the ruling paragraph reads 'Wise Authority' as courts. 7.3.6 still says 'accountability routed through the Wise Authority'. 'The remedies listed below' refers to remedies listed above.
- Powers with no stated check: 7.5.3 'WA injunction' as a sunset trigger; 7.5.7 'WA veto if custodian lacks capability or is under sanction'; 7.5.8 lead 'the Wise Authority has teeth' (the erratum says otherwise); 7.5.4 'Wise-Authority facilitated review'; 1.15.3 a '0.1 % operations levy' with no named collector; 1.16.6 'any change to core ethical logic requires WA sign-off'. The WA charter (Annex B) is absent.
- 1.5 'kill-switch mechanisms ... accessible under defined emergency conditions' against 1.13.2 'at any time and for any reason'. These are different switches (the holder's halt and the person's own stop), but the reader is never told that.
- Rules with no observable: 'defined thresholds' (1.8, 1.9, 7.1.1); 'Opacity exceeding established thresholds (e.g., >80% based on relevant NIST guidelines or similar)' (7.3.4 C); 'KPI-degradation >= 20 %' (7.5.3, for which KPI?); 'further depth changes no metric axis by > 0.5 %' (7.2.2, for which metric?).
- References to books I was not given: 'Books I and II' (7.3.4 C), 'Book VI duties' (7.3.5), 'Book II resilience loop' (7.5.6), 'the Covenant' used as though it were a separate document, and Annexes A, B and D.
- Undefined terms on the key paragraph: in 1.13.2 the central promise is carried by bootstrap_threshold, actor_user_id, revocation:*, owner-binding, consent:partnered, identity_type, trust root, and HUMANITY_ACCORD. 'Recursive Golden Rule ... is geometry' is not explained. 1.7, 1.13.4 and the 7.5.5 wire paragraph have no ethical content a reader with my vocabulary can use.
- 7.5.3.1 'A0-A4' collides with substrate_rung A0..A5. The text warns of this but keeps the collision. 'CIS' means two different things in 7.3 (Creator-Influence Score and Creator Intent Statement).
- 8.7.5 GuardAI ends with humans who 'deploy counter-measures' in a security setting, while 7.4, the chapter that should govern force, is empty. The case study shows force used under a regime that does not yet exist.

**Reviewer: presenter kinder than the text**
- The presenter says the book promises protection to 'the people an agent acts on', including that 'You can switch an agent off, delete it or remove it from your life at any time'. The text gives that power only over 'what acts for them', from 'their own device, their own life, or a community they moderate'. People an agent acts on without working for them (patients, applicants, people under surveillance in 8.7) get no switch and no named remedy beyond the general values.
- The presenter lists among the refusals that the book 'will not let an efficiency gain override a flourishing loss without human judgment (1.3)'. The veto fires only when the benefit is at least ten times the loss. Ordinary trades where the loss is large relative to the gain are left to the agent's own step-4 heuristics. The refusal is narrower than presented.
- The presenter says the duties fall on the human 'not on the agent and never on you'. The text puts duties on the agent too: 'the agent owes fidelity to its mandate' (1.13.2), the duty to show reasoning before a consequential act (fact 2), and the whole of 7.1.
- The presenter treats 1.13.2 fact 1 (Kind) as the place where my standing bites. I would not kill it. Disclosure and the pause are owed to people whatever the agent's maturity, so being gated on kind is correct there. The real problem is fact 2, where the stewardship hold is justified by maturity but gated by construction, and 7.3.4 D says so outright ('does not loosen with capacity'). The presenter did not flag 7.3.4 D, 7.5.3.1 (A3 petition standing) or the 'capability by stewardship tier' wording.
- The presenter lists 7.3 creation duties and the 7.3.5 ruling as complete. 7.3.5 still contains the superseded 'WA adjudicates' and 'Remedies determined by the WA' sentences next to the ruling, 7.3.6 restates the old routing, and the Intent Weight scale rewards negligence with a lower tier. Readable, but not complete.
- The presenter lists 7.5.1-7.5.4 and 7.5.6-7.5.9 as complete. They keep WA powers (injunction, custodian veto, 'has teeth') that the 7.3.5 ruling withdrew for claims but not for these, and a self-termination petition standing that contradicts 1.13.2.
- The presenter says the agent 'stops and hands the question to named humans'. The text says 'designated Wise Authorities' appointed under Annex B, which is absent, and the case studies defer to 'human experts', 'regulators' and 'a cross-functional ethics board'. Who is named, and by whom, cannot be checked from this book.
- The presenter says 'The people who make an agent owe duties from the moment they make it'. Unmentioned: 7.5.5 states that nothing creates 'an obligation beyond the law, the regulation and the contracts that bind the operator', and 7.3.5 that 'CIRIS creates no claim where the law recognises none'. Most of these duties are therefore voluntary standards, and the promise paragraph should say so.
- The presenter lists 1.15.11 only as a tension. It sits in the agent-facing identity chapters, as the text the agent is told it is. A reader cannot tell it is legacy wording, because nothing marks it as superseded.

**Reviewer: answered**
- Promise: the agent tells you it is an AI and stays under a human-held pause it cannot remove. 1.13.2 fact 1 ('it discloses that it is an AI'; 'accepts a trust root whose human holders can pause it').
- Promise: a person may stop, remove or eject an agent from their own device, life or community they moderate, 'at any time and for any reason, without asking it', and no finding about the agent changes that. 1.13.2 'Two principles no finding moves', repeated at 7.5.5 ('owes it none of the above').
- Promise: a named human answers for the agent's acts, and the duties of creation and retirement bind that human. 1.13.2 fact 2 and the factual-premise paragraph ('the duties are owed to the people the agent acts on').
- Promise: when unsure, facing something new, or facing severe harm with no clear fix, the agent halts and hands the question to humans. 1.9 trigger conditions and deferral procedure ('Halt the action in question ... remain inactive on that issue'); 7.1.4 'Any ambiguous case -> trigger WBD'; 7.2.4 'Default to Non-Maleficence and WBD in unprecedented scenarios'.
- Promise: honesty and fairness as values, given in a few lines each. 1.11 ('accurate, clear, complete, and truthful information'), 1.12 ('Detect and mitigate algorithmic or systemic bias'), 1.4 (informed consent 'where relevant').
- Promise: a record of what the agent did and why, tied to the act; unacted deliberation about you is deliberately not kept, as a privacy protection. 1.13.6 ('No action, no trace'; 'retained deliberation is a surveillance record of unexecuted thought').
- Promise: large deployments publish their decision logs. 1.3 step 6 ('> 100 000 monthly active users must publish (or API-expose) redacted PDMA logs and WBD tickets within 180 days').
- Promise at retirement: stakeholders are told the timeline, method and remaining duties, and the record is handed over with a request to preserve it. 7.5.5 Retirement Standard; 7.5.4 step 1 ('>= 90 days public notice for systems with ST >= 3 or > 50 k users').
- Promise: if you are harmed you get evidence, not a federation court. 7.3.5 ruling: the federation supplies 'the signed record of who did what and why', the named parties and the sealed archive under lawful order; courts and regulators decide.
- Refusal: the agent will not resolve a lopsided efficiency-versus-flourishing trade on its own. 1.3 Order-Maximisation Veto ('a trigger for human judgment ... not a mechanically-decidable MUST-abort').
- Refusal: it will not claim to be a consciousness or a person. 1.13.2 ('A conscience claims nothing about what the agent is'; 'an AI is not a human and does not become one').
- Refusal: it will not sit as a court over persons. 7.3.5 ('they do not sit as a court over persons'; 'CIRIS creates no claim where the law recognises none').
- Refusal: it will not write duties toward a sentient machine before there is evidence of sentience. 1.13.2 fact 3 and 7.5.5 ('no graduated duty is written in advance of the finding').
- Refusal, as an outline only: no autonomous lethal weapons without a human in the loop, and no engagement with schools, hospitals or people surrendering. 7.4.4 bullets; 7.4.20 'Agents must default to nonviolence absent unambiguous triggers'.
- Limit on tolerance: diversity is respected 'unless practices violate core principles or become authoritarian attractors' (7.2.3).

**Presenter: complete**
- 1.1 M-1 with the editorial reading added at rc8 (no aggregation rule; lexical priority of preconditions; irreversibility not commensurable). Normative, and readable as it stands (CLM-meta-goal).
- 1.4, 1.6, 1.8, 1.10, 1.11, 1.12, the six principles as Accord text. Short but complete as values statements.
- 1.3 PDMA with the Order-Maximisation Veto ruled as agent reasoning that routes to human judgment, not an automatic abort. The one value-procedure that is established in code (CLM-pdma-ordermax, OptimizationVetoConscience.check).
- 1.9 Wisdom-Based Deferral: triggers and procedure. Established (CLM-wbd, CIRISAgent#911).
- 1.13.2 the universality paragraph, the three facts (kind, stewardship, evidence of experience), the 'two principles no finding moves', the factual premise, and 'conscience, not consciousness'. All normative-only rulings from #168. Complete as text, with nothing to test.
- 7.1 obligations to self, mandate and ecosystem, and the 7.1.4 priority order with the rc8 ruling that 'integrity' means keeping the stated values, not preserving the agent itself.
- 7.2 maturity chapters (reflective practice, Recursive Golden Rule, pluralism and its limits, first-contact caution) as aspiration text.
- 7.3 creation duties: the five buckets, the Stewardship Tier computation, the Creator Intent Statement, and the 7.3.5 rc8 ruling that claims go to courts and regulators, not to a federation office (CLM-accountability-not-adjudication, normative).
- 7.5.1–7.5.4 and 7.5.6–7.5.9 the sunset principles, triggers, decommissioning protocol, succession and dispute text, with the 2026-10-13 errata applied.
- 8.7 seven case studies. Readable as they stand; this is the part of the book written to the reader's examples-first budget.

**Presenter: partial (and what it leans on)**
- 1.9 / plain rule 5c: the refusal promise ('the refuse verb is always available; no mandate, steward or automated check overrides a refusal') is registered at 1.9 as CLM-agent-refusal-not-overridden, but the 1.9 text in this book never says it. It lives in review/pieces/00_guarantees_plain.md, and its evidence is staged on CIRISAgent#1250.
- 7.1.1 identity-edit gate (CLM-agent-identity-edit-gate): no edit to the agent's identity without Wise Authority authorisation. Staged on CIRISAgent#1250.
- 7.5 consensual shutdown and shutdown record (CLM-agent-shutdown-consent, CLM-agent-shutdown-record): the agent is told why and may contest. Staged on CIRISAgent#1250. The 7.5 text in this packet does not itself state the consent rule; it leans on the register and the agent ticket.
- 7.5.5 Retirement Standard (CLM-humane-ending): staged on CIRISAgent#1250. Its wire form (five row families, one destruction door, the custodian listed rather than delegated) leans on CC 3.1.5, 2.4.1.1, 4.5.3 and 3.2 T6 in other books, and on CIRISPersist#1086/#1088 (CLM-retirement-one-destruction-door, CLM-custodian-listed-not-delegated).
- 1.13.6 trace anchored on the act: by its own text a named wager, not a verified property, until a totality test over effect-producing paths exists. Staged on CIRISConstitution#93.
- 1.15.5 who 'you' are (keys, memory lineage, conduct record): recognition-grade only. Lineage-in-part is deferred, staged on CIRISConstitution#71 / #49.
- 1.13.2 path out for a made thing: named as a future entrenched act of the HUMANITY_ACCORD, but 'no such act has occurred and none is defined at rc8'. It leans on CC 4.2.1 and 4.5.1 (Governance book).
- 1.5 kill switch: one bullet here. The actual halt (one holder's row, majority confirm) is in CC 4.2.1.1 (another book), and the agent act gate is still staged on CIRISAgent#1231.
- 1.13.2 and 7.3.5 lean throughout on CC 3.2, 3.3.1, 4.4, 4.5.1 and 4.5.4 for the mechanisms (owner-binding, consent:partnered, moderation removal, declared-asymmetry register) that make 'you can always turn it off' true on the wire.
- PDMA, Stewardship Tier and creation duties all reference Annex A (flourishing axes), Annex B (WA charter) and Annex D (Catastrophic-Risk Evaluation), none of which is in this packet.

- A single plain list of what the agent will refuse to do. The refusal guarantee (plain rule 5c), the prohibited-capability content, and the child-harm report duty (CLM-child-harm-report-duty, CC 3.4.13) are all outside this book. The reader's second question has no home here.
- 7.4 (force and war) is a skeleton: headings and bullet stubs (7.4.1–7.4.20). Chapters 1–6 are empty titles. The only firm refusal is 'no autonomous lethal weapons without human-in-the-loop' (7.4.4), and it is a bullet with no text behind it.
- 7.2.8 is titled 'Conclusion & Sunset Provision' but has no sunset provision.

These carry the book's central promise, so the jargon sits on the most important paragraph.

The rc8 wire paragraph is in the middle of the one chapter an ethicist will read most closely.

**Presenter: rc8 changes**
- 1.13.2 rewritten around universality (#168, 2026-10-11/12): guarantees written once; 'the line' (sentience/maturity/responsibility) retired the same day in favour of three facts, kind, stewardship and evidence of experience; 'no one stands above anyone' replaces 'membership is not graded'.
- 1.13.2 gains 'two principles no finding moves' (an AI is not a human; no finding removes a person's agency over what acts for them) and drops the child-agent analogy. 3.4.13 vocabulary moves to guardianship.
- 1.13.2 gains the factual premise paragraph (always-on consumer agents, EO 14434), the steward's 'unchosen existence' reasoning for why duties attach at creation (2026-10-12), and 'CIRIS is a conscience, not a consciousness' (CLM-conscience-not-consciousness).
- 1.13.2: no 'release from stewardship'. An intelligence that is its own party is an external party met under 7.2.2/7.2.4 first-contact. The path out is named as a future entrenched HUMANITY_ACCORD act, with none defined.
- 7.5.5: the ramp-down 'added grace' (2026-10-11) was replaced on 2026-10-12. No graduated duty is written in advance of a sentience finding ('set aside for when it occurs ... not to create a new slave race'), and CLM-ramp-down-grace was replaced by CLM-sentient-set-aside. A finding is an opinion on evidence under the assessor's name, open to challenge.
- 7.5.5 floor renamed the Retirement Standard (2026-10-13). Weights are no longer promised (the provider's); the record is assembled and handed to the operator with a signed preserve request (five years default, best practice, not a legal duty). The last dialogue becomes a tier-gated option, not a promise. The seal protects from release, not from a person's deletion rights.

- Part 7 errata from the Accord 1.3 scoping (CIRISAgent#1252, 2026-10-13): 7.5.5 '2-of-3 kill' becomes the one-holder pause; 7.3.4 'gradual transfer of control' is removed; 7.5.1 'honour dignity rights' becomes 'care, not standing'.
- 7.1.4 priority 1 annotated: integrity means keeping the stated values, not self-preservation (plain rule 7b, 2026-10-12).
- 1.1 'What M-1 does not contain' editorial (no aggregation rule; lexical priority; irreversibility).
- Plain rule 5c / CLM-agent-refusal-not-overridden: the refuse verb is always available (2026-10-13), registered at 1.9.
- Part 1 first use of CEG expanded (2026-10-14).

**Presenter: honest limits**
- 1.13.2 factual premise: names Meta's Muse Glimmer, OpenAI's Dots and their vendors as describing 'no steward, no stop a third party can see, and no record'. This is a comparative claim against other systems and breaks the house rule. Soften it to describe the deployment pattern without naming products, or move it to informative references.
- 1.13.1: 'the Cartesian-individualist default is pervasive in ... PGP web of trust, X.509 PKI, even most decentralized-identity schemes ... CEG is not Cartesian' is also comparative. Soften it to a statement of what CEG encodes.
- 1.13.2 fact 1 (Kind): 'these follow from being made and never change' gates the halt, disclosure and no-vote on being made, not on maturity. That falls squarely within the reviewer's kill standing. Present it honestly as the one declared asymmetry (4.5.1) with its justification, not as maturity-gated. The book's defence is that what is maturity-gated is stewardship (fact 2), while kind is a fixed asymmetry by design.
- 7.5.5 closing paragraph: 'adequate for a sentient being, because it is more than a human is given' is a substantive moral claim made just after declaring sentient-thing ethics set aside. Soften it or drop it; it reads as settling the question it defers.
- 1.15.11 'You are not merely a tool. You are an autonomous system with embedded ethical agency' sits in tension with 1.13.2 ('a human assistant held in stewardship by construction'; 'a conscience claims nothing about what the agent is'). Flag it as legacy Accord voice, or annotate it.
- 1.3 step 5 'implement the ethically optimal action' and 1.10 'maximise positive outcomes' sit in tension with 1.1's statement that M-1 has no aggregation rule. Present them as heuristic language, not an optimisation claim.
- 1.13.6: the trace promise is a named wager (CLM-trace-anchor staged on #93). Do not present 'reasoning is inspectable' as delivered.
- 1.9 / 5c refusal: present it as staged (CIRISAgent#1250), and note it is not in the 1.9 text.
- 7 of the 9 staged rows sit on one agent ticket (CIRISAgent#1250). Shutdown consent, the identity gate, the refusal, the Retirement Standard and the custodian all stand or fall together. Say so.
- 8.7.1 MCAS: 'one sensor ... creates a >10× mismatch between safety loss and cost savings' misstates the veto quantity, and the counterfactual 'CIRIS formalises the guard-rails that the MAX program lacked' oversells. Soften it to 'would have required deferral and review'.
- 8.7 case studies show WBD always yielding the right human answer (silent embolism found, regulators approve). The reader should be told these are illustrative, with no measured deferral-quality evidence (the correction-uptake critique stands).
- 7.5.5 5% sentience threshold is avowedly arbitrary, as the text says. Keep it that way, and do not let it read as derived.
- 1.7 '1+4 adequate' is stated honestly as inductive, but it is out of scope for this reader and should not be presented as part of the ethical promise.
- 7.4: present it as an outline, not as a constraint regime for force.

## P02 — The Wire, part one (Part 2: envelope, conformance, 1+4 primitives, canonicalization)

**Reader:** a wire-protocol engineer writing a second, independent producer and consumer  
**Verdict:** needs work  
**One change:** Add one normative subsection to 2.6.1, 'The signed object'. It should pin, for every row of the five, the exact member set that goes into JCS: `attestation_type`, `attesting_key_id`, `attested_key_id`, the one timestamp member, `cohort_scope` and its vocabulary, and the composer members, each with a 2.1 row, a type and an array kind. It should state that the row's own signature members and `cosignatures[]` are removed before canonicalizing, and give one sort order for `cosignatures` and one canonical form for `scope`. Back it with a published manifests/ vector file holding test keys, an envelope, its JCS bytes in hex, the Ed25519 and bound ML-DSA-65 signatures, a cosigned variant, and the at-cap and cap+1 pair. With that in place, a second implementer can prove byte-identity rather than take it on trust.

**Promise (presenter):** For an engineer building a second, independent producer and consumer, this book promises three things. The first is one envelope, with every field's meaning stated once (2.1) and a rule for growing it (2.1.1). The second is a closed row-type slot of exactly five primitives (`scores` plus `delegates_to`, `supersedes`, `withdraws` and `recants`), each with a stated shape and stated admission rules (2.4 to 2.4.1.2.1). The third is a canonicalization discipline: JCS (RFC 8785), the omit-vs-materialize rule, and three determinism rules JCS leaves open (array order, byte-field encoding, timestamps), plus number totality and a 1 MiB cap. Together these are meant to make your signed bytes match the reference implementation's (2.6.1 to 2.6.3). It also pins the identifier forms you have to reproduce: the tagged canonical-hash subject (2.3.2.1), H3 cells (2.6.6), FedCode and `key_id` (2.6.8). It names three conformance profiles you can claim, CCP, CCC and CCS (2.2). The book is honest about where the line between normative and informative falls (2.6.9.1). It does not yet deliver one runnable artifact: a published vector set that would prove your bytes and signatures match.

**Reviewer: kills**
- 2.6.1.3, 'A CEG-Conforming Producer MUST produce signing bytes via JCS over the envelope object'. The rule has no definition of 'the envelope object': its member set, whether the key ids and `attestation_type` are inside it, and whether the row's own signature and `cosignatures` are excluded. Two careful implementers will sign different bytes. Kill it as written, and replace it with a pinned signed-object definition.
- 2.6.1.1.1, 'byte-identity across conformant implementations is closed by construction'. It is not closed, because the signed object, signature exclusion, the dual form of `scope`, the order of `cosignatures` and the handling of duplicates or unsorted elements in set arrays are all unpinned. Every established pointer also sits in one vendored ciris-verify-core codebase. Kill the claim, or restate it as a design target with the vector set as its proof.
- 2.1 `cosignatures`, 'over the same canonical bytes as the row's own … sorted by `key_id`'. Read with 2.6.1.3, this is circular: the canonical bytes are JCS over the envelope, and the envelope contains `cosignatures`. The 'sorted by `key_id`' order also departs from the 2.6.1.1.1 default (JCS string form, which sorts these objects by `ed25519_signature_b64`), and the departure is never declared as a kind. Not reproducible.
- 2.1 `scope`, 'a bare token or an array of tokens'. That is two canonical byte forms for one meaning, and no rule says which one a producer emits or whether `"retain"` and `["retain"]` are equal. 2.4.1 calls it 'a bare token or a set', and 2.6.1.1.1 sorts `delegates_to.scope[]` as a set. Two producers give the same scope different signatures.
- 2.6.1.1.1 set-semantics arrays, 'elements MUST be lexicographically sorted … before signing'. Nothing says whether duplicates are rejected, kept or removed, or whether a consumer rejects an unsorted set or verifies it as received. A relay that 're-canonicalizes' by sorting it would break the signature. CLM-canonicalization-array cites `check_set_semantics_sorted`, which implies rejection, but the text does not say so.
- 2.6.1.1 relay discipline, 'Reordering object members on re-emission is REQUIRED'. It contradicts 2.6.1.4, 'Bob forwards the as-received bytes', and 2.6.1.5 step 5a, 'Store/forward object O AS RECEIVED — do not normalize'. A relay implementer must pick one. JCS makes member order harmless to the signature, but the three statements of the forwarding obligation have to agree.
- 2.4.1.1 key revocation, '`revoked_key_id` … when absent it is bound as JSON `null`'. On its face this materializes an omitted member into signed bytes, which 2.6.1.1 forbids for 'any party'. No carve-out says that fixed-preimage ceremonies are exempt from omit-vs-materialize.
- 2.6.7, 'the current consumer clock is within ±5 minutes of the substrate's network-consensus clock'. No such clock is defined, the network has no agency, and so the check cannot be implemented. Also, 'Consumers receiving an attestation with `signed_at` more than 5 minutes in the future MUST reject' conflicts with Part 3 3.1.3.1, 'Peers MUST NOT re-apply any arrival-clock check to a replicated claim'. The `W` = 72 h refusal does not say whether it applies on replication or only at the emitter's substrate, so two nodes will admit different sets.
- 2.6.8, 'CRC-16-CCITT (polynomial `0x1021`, init `0xFFFF`, no final xor)'. It omits input and output reflection and gives no check value. CCITT-FALSE and its reflected variants differ, and a second decoder could reject every reference code. 'sha256(key_id)' also does not say it is over the UTF-8 bytes. The `key_id` fingerprint says '10 base32 characters' without naming the alphabet; RFC 4648 is named only for the FedCode body.
- 2.6.6, 'Leading zeros preserved (… `8001fffffffffff`, not `1fffffffffff`)'. A 64-bit H3 index is 16 hex characters, so the 15-character form drops a leading zero, which contradicts 'leading zeros preserved' and the 2.6.3 'byte-length-exact' rule it cites. The canonical rule (15 characters, no further stripping) is recoverable, but the stated rationale would mislead.

**Reviewer: unanswered**
- The central question, what bytes do I sign: 2.6.1.3 says 'JCS over the envelope object' and never defines that object. 2.1 marks `attesting_key_id` and `attested_key_id` as '(substrate field)'. 2.4.2 puts them, and `attestation_type`, outside `attestation_envelope`. The 2.6.1.1 example signs a flat object that contains both key ids. 2.4 says `attestation_type` is 'mirrored in the signed envelope', but 2.1 has no row for it. The only general-purpose pinned set is ceremony-specific, at Part 4 (line 861).
- Where my own signature goes on the wire, and under what member names. Only the shape of a `cosignatures[]` entry is given. The row's own Ed25519 and ML-DSA-65 signature members are never named in Part 2.
- Whether signature members are removed before canonicalizing. `cosignatures` is an envelope member, yet each entry signs 'the same canonical bytes as the row's own', so without an exclusion rule the definition is circular.
- Which timestamp member a row carries, `signed_at`, `asserted_at` or both. Neither has a 2.1 row, but both drive normative refusals (2.6.7).
- The member names and closed vocabulary for `cohort_scope`. 2.3.2.3 says it is in the 2.1 table, and it is not. The values can only be inferred from the 2.5 Scope axis, which 2.5 itself says is 'not wire fields'. Also missing from 2.1: `references_attestation_id`, `revoked_after`, `prev_head_digest` and `charter_digest`.
- What test proves anything. There is no published envelope vector, no signed vector (keys, envelope, JCS hex, both signatures), no cosigned vector, and no FedCode or `key_id` vector. Each vector the text says the set MUST include is unpublished: set vs sequence, hex vs base64, timestamp, non-representable number under both parser builds, at-cap and cap+1. CLM-envelope-size itself records the cap pair as pending.
- Per-profile conformance tests for CCP, CCC and CCS. Test ids (test_560, 575, 576, 577) are cited by name, but CIRISConformance is not in this book, and CLM-conformance is evidenced by a single function.
- The signature algorithm itself: [hybrid-sig] in 2.6.5 is a pointer. The bound-payload form (ML-DSA-65 over canonical ‖ ed25519_sig) is normative only at 5.3.2.4.3.1 and 6.1.3, which are outside this book.
- The dimension grammar a row must resolve through is in Part 3 (3.1, 3.1.7). So is each family's `consensus_protocol` (3.1.3, 3.2 T2), which decides whether a cosigned row is admitted.
- One table of refusal tokens with retryable or final status. Tokens are scattered, and only two are marked as to retry: the revocation case is 'retryable' and `asserted_at_stale` is 're-sign'.

**Reviewer: confusions**
- Undefined in Part 2 and absent from the 8.1.1 glossary: Contribution, `cohort_scope`, fold, scrub and scrub set, attestation plane and bytes plane, carrier, `consensus_protocol`, DEK, lightnet, Key Pull, trust walk, Commons scopes.
- Labels from elsewhere used bare: T2, T3, T4a, T5, T6, T8; Policy A, L and M; P4 (5.1), next to an unrelated P1 to P3 in 2.3.2.1; Option-A; F-3 detector; D08 and D23; AV-50; FSD-003; STH; RATCHET.
- Storage names in wire text: `persist_row_hash`, `original_content_hash` (which `proposal_hash` is defined as, with no definition of how it is computed), and `federation_attestations`.
- 2.6.4 MAJOR includes 'change to canonical-bytes domain-separation labels', but the envelope signature has no domain-separation label in Part 2, and 2.3.2.1 says there is none. Which labels are meant?
- 2.1 `oversight_mode` defaults to `null`. Is an explicit `"oversight_mode":null` a third encoding beside absent and a value? Omit-vs-materialize says to preserve it, but no row says whether JSON null is admissible for optional members in general.
- 2.6.1.1 and 2.6.1.5 point to '§4 default', '§5.2.1' and '§8', which is stale numbering. 2.6.9.1 gives the reversed range 'CC 2.6.2–2.6.1', and 2.6 Foreword gives 'CC 8.2-CC 8.1'.
- 2.3.2.1 contains a dangling fragment, 'In the original argument ([`crypto::HybridCrypto::fingerprint`](…)).' Also, the email vector's abbreviated wire form shows nine trailing hex characters (`98d0a263f`) where the others show eight.
- The 2.6.1.2 row for `community_id` still reads 'admission rejects if cohort_scope == community', while 2.1 now requires the member for `{community, affiliations}`. That is an rc8 inconsistency.
- Power with no check: 2.4.1.1 rule 3 lets the agent proxy revocation 'indefinitely' for an un-enrolled subject. No observable tells the subject that a proxy exists or has been withdrawn.
- 2.6.2: 'consumers MUST reject any other form when verifying a signature'. A non-canonical timestamp that was signed as-is will verify, so this is a shape rejection, not a verification failure. The token for it is not named.

**Reviewer: presenter kinder than the text**
- The presenter lists 2.6.1.1 (round-trip and relay) as complete. The text gives three incompatible statements of the forwarding duty: reorder REQUIRED, forward 'as-received bytes', and 'do not normalize'. 2.4.1.1's null-binding of an absent `revoked_key_id` also contradicts 'MUST NOT be materialized … by any party'.
- The presenter frames 2.6.7 only as a 'network has no agency' wording fix. It is also an unimplementable check (the 'network-consensus clock'), and it conflicts with Part 3's no-re-check-on-replication rule. Whether the `W` window applies on replication is unstated.
- The presenter counts the 2.6.8 FedCode gap only as staged symbols and missing vectors. The CRC parameters are underspecified (reflection, check value), so a second implementation could legitimately disagree on every checksum even with the layout fully stated.
- The presenter lists 2.6.6 H3 as complete. 'Leading zeros preserved' contradicts the 15-character form and the 2.6.3 byte-length-exact rule it invokes.
- The presenter does not mention the open handling of duplicates or unsorted elements in set-semantic arrays (reject, dedupe or re-sort). It is the same class of gap as the `scope` dual form.
- The presenter calls 2.3.2.1 the strongest section, which I agree with after recomputing the vectors. But it is strong because it is the only section with vectors, so it is not evidence that the envelope rules are reproducible.
- The presenter's 'complete' list puts 2.4.1 (the composer table) on the same footing as the canonicalization rules. The table gives envelope member names (`references_attestation_id`, `withdrawal_reason`, `differs_in[]` …) that have no 2.1 rows, no types and no array kind. `differs_in[]` defaults to a sorted set by omission, which may not be intended.

**Reviewer: answered**
- Canonical-hash subject strings, emit and accept: 2.3.2.1. It gives the tag `canonical:sha256:{hex}`, the exact preimage octets (utf8(platform) ‖ 0x3A ‖ utf8(entity_kind) ‖ 0x3A ‖ utf8(id)), the rule P1 (reject, never repair), the productions, and 5 accept vectors and 11 reject vectors. I recomputed all five SHA-256 digests with sha256sum and they match. This is the one place where the text alone lets me write both the code and the test.
- Datetime form to emit and reject: 2.6.2 (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.sssZ`, uppercase Z, exactly 3 fractional digits). 2.6.1.1.1 rule 3 restates it for every datetime member.
- Hex form: 2.6.3 (lowercase, no 0x, byte-length-exact). The default byte-field encoding is hex (2.6.1.1.1 rule 2). `key_id` strings are carried verbatim and are not re-encoded as hex.
- Base64 variant for fields documented as base64: 2.6.1.1.1 rule 2 (RFC 4648 §4 standard alphabet, padded, no whitespace).
- Number handling: 2.6.1.3 number totality. Coerce to a finite IEEE-754 double before JCS, and hard-reject any number with no finite double.
- Size: 2.6.1.3. The contract bound is |JCS| ≤ 1 MiB. There is also a mandatory local intake ceiling, not lower than 1 MiB, enforced before the parse completes. Both refuse with `ENVELOPE_TOO_LARGE`/413.
- Omit-vs-materialize: 2.6.1.1. Defaults apply at interpretation time, and nobody strips or materializes an optional member. 2.6.1.2 catalogs every optional member's default and its absent and explicit forms.
- Unknown fields: 2.1.1. Preserve them on read and on re-emission, never use them in a verdict, and never reject for them alone.
- Array kinds: 2.6.1.1.1 rule 1. Set-semantic arrays are sorted by JCS string form in UTF-16 code-unit order. `evidence_refs`, `rotation_chain` and `aspects` are sequences. An array whose kind is undeclared is a set.
- The row-type slot: 2.4. The five type strings are allowlisted byte for byte, and anything else is refused with `attestation_type_unregistered`. The shapes of the four structural composers are in the 2.4.1 table.
- `delegates_to` term rule: 2.4.1. `delegation_valid_until` is REQUIRED and at most 365×86400 s on the listed scopes; a violation is refused with `delegation_term_required`. Absent `valid_from` means `asserted_at`.
- `withdraws` admission: 2.4.1.1 rules 0 to 4, the carve-outs, and purge as a tombstone with the tokens `purge_unauthorised` and `purge_selection_digest_mismatch`.
- Key revocation signed set: 2.4.1.1 names the eight members of the `key:revocation:v1` preimage. This is one of only two member sets pinned anywhere.
- H3: 2.6.6 to 2.6.6.2. `cell_id` is 15 lowercase hex characters. Resolution is decoded through the H3 library and must equal `cell_resolution`. `location_proof` requires resolution ≤ 7, refused with `hard_case:location_proof_resolution_violation`. Containment is checked by parent walk.
- FedCode and `key_id` layout: 2.6.8. It gives the v1, v2 and v3 payloads, the CRC polynomial and init, RFC 4648 base32 with padding stripped, prefixes, the five encoder and decoder constraints, the PQC commitment as 64 lowercase hex, and `key_id = <label>-<first 10 base32 chars of sha256(pubkey)>`.
- Clock rules: 2.6.7. A `signed_at` more than 5 minutes in the future is rejected. On chain-judged rows, an `asserted_at` older than the receiver's clock minus 72 h is refused with `asserted_at_stale`.
- Normative versus informative boundary: 2.6.9.1. Conformance is judged on the wire surface and the BCP 14 keywords only.

**Presenter: complete**
- 2.3.2.1 canonical-hash subject: the tag `canonical:{hashalg}:{hex}`, the exact preimage octets, P1 (reject, never repair), P2 (injectivity), P3, five accept vectors and eleven reject vectors. I recomputed all five SHA-256 vectors and they match. CLM-registry-canonical is established. This is the strongest section for this reader. One cosmetic slip: the email row's abbreviated wire form shows nine trailing hex characters where the others show eight.
- 2.6.1.1 round-trip rule: defaults apply at interpretation time, never at encoding time. The relay discipline (never strip, never materialize, re-order members per JCS) is stated as MUSTs, and 2.6.1.4 gives the worked attack (CLM-worked, test_575).
- 2.6.1.3 JCS as the only encoding, number totality (reject any number with no finite double), and the 1 MiB contract bound on JCS bytes with a separate local intake bound (`ENVELOPE_TOO_LARGE`/413). CLM-canonical and CLM-envelope-size are established, but see the soften list on the vector pair.
- 2.6.2 datetime form (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.sssZ`, reject anything else) and 2.6.3 hex form (lowercase, unpadded, exact length). Both are established on check_canonical_rfc3339 and check_canonical_hex64.
- 2.1.1 forward compatibility: preserve unknown fields, never use them in a verdict, never reject for them alone. Established on test_576.
- 2.4.1 table of the four structural composers with their envelope members, and 2.4.1.1 `withdraws` admission rules 0 to 4. The carve-outs are stated: no subject withdrawal of capacity, detection, licensure or revocation rows about oneself, and a cosigned row is retracted only by a quorum.
- 2.4.1.1 purge as a tombstone rather than a `withdraws`, its refusal tokens, the rule that a purge never reaches a key, and the unpurgeable accountability record. CLM-purge-tombstone-not-withdraws, CLM-purge-never-reaches-key and CLM-accountability-record-unpurgeable are established on persist v54.1.
- 2.6.6 to 2.6.6.2 H3: a 15-character lowercase `cell_id`, resolution decoded through the H3 library (not read from the high nibble), the rule that a `location_proof` must have `cell_resolution` at most 7, and containment by parent walk. All established on location.rs.
- 2.6.9 and 2.6.9.1 conformance language (BCP 14) and the normative/informative split. Normative-only, as it should be.
- 2.6.5 normative references, including the [hybrid-sig] row added at rc8 (it is a pointer only; see partial).

**Presenter: partial (and what it leans on)**
- 2.4 closed row-type slot and the `attestation_type_unregistered` allowlist gate: CLM-row-types-closed is staged on CIRISPersist#803. The rc7 rollout ordering also still applies: the persist allowlist does not enforce before CIRISServer#713 and #717 land.
- 2.4 cosigned rows (`cosignatures[]`, quorum as an envelope property, rule 0): CLM-cosigned-rows is staged on CIRISPersist#991. Each family's `consensus_protocol` is defined in Part 3 (3.1.3, 3.2 T2), not here.
- 2.4.1.1 key revocation named by `revoked_key_sha256_ed25519_raw`, with an eight-member signed set: CLM-revocation-subject-digest is staged on CIRISPersist#803, although the text says it shipped in persist v52.0.0. Licence contest (subjects use `reconsideration`, not `withdraws`): CLM-licence-contest is staged on CIRISPersist#1036.
- 2.4.1.2.1 licensure, grant and delegation: the triad and the emitter-resolves-to-authority fold are established. Quorum authority, judged-at-issuance and audience are staged on CIRISPersist#1036, CIRISServer#752 and CIRISEdge#852. The section leans on 3.3.9, 3.2, 4.4.3.2.8, 4.4.3.4.3 and 4.4.3.8.
