- 2.6.7 admission freshness window W = 72 h (`asserted_at_stale`): CLM-admission-freshness-window is staged on CIRISPersist#1049, which also did not ship in v54.1.0.
- 2.6.8 FedCode v2/v3: the wire layout and constraints are fully stated, but CLM-fedcode-kind-tagged is staged on CIRISVerify#297 (the encoder and decoder symbols are not yet published). The owned-node and PQC-commitment rows are established.
- 2.3.1 rule that subject-naming dimensions must carry `subject_key_ids`: CLM-subject_kind-subject-2 is staged on CIRISServer#536, and the substrate rule is only a MAY.
- 2.4.2 `scores`: CLM-silo-projection-rebuildable is staged on CIRISPersist#988 and anchored at 2.4.2, but nothing in 2.4.2's text states the rule. The register row points at text that is not in this book.
- 2.2 conformance profiles: the floors lean on 4.4.2 (default aggregation), 4.4.3.8 (Policy A), 4.2.2 (hardware class), 5.3.1 and 5.3.2 (substrate) and 3.4 (reserved prefixes). CLM-conformance is evidenced by one function (conformance.rs#require_op), not by a test per profile.
- 2.6.5 [hybrid-sig]: the bound-hybrid signature (Ed25519 over the canonical bytes, then ML-DSA-65 over the canonical bytes followed by the Ed25519 signature) is only a pointer row here. It is normative at 5.3.2.4.3.1 and 6.1.3, which are outside this book.
- 2.1 trust-root and halt fields (`attached_head_digest`, `attach_window_secs`, `witness_*`, `halt_fuse_secs`, `recovery_commitments`): their semantics depend on 3.2 (T2, T3, T4a, T6, T8), 4.2.1.1 and 4.2.6.
- Most canonicalization rows that count as established cite CIRISServer/src/ciris-verify-core and CIRISVerify/src/ciris-verify-core, which are the same vendored code. There is one implementation family, so independent byte-identity has not yet been shown. This reader's implementation would be the first independent one.

**Presenter: missing**
- The general signed member set. 2.6.1.3 says to sign 'JCS over the envelope object', but Part 2 never says which object that is. 2.4.2 nests `attestation_envelope` with `attestation_type`, `attesting_key_id` and `attested_key_id` outside it. The 2.6.1.1 example signs a flat object that includes both key ids. 2.4 says `attestation_type` is 'mirrored in the signed envelope', but 2.1 has no row for it. The only fully pinned member set is a ceremony-specific one at 4.4.3.4.3.1. A second implementer cannot reproduce signing bytes from this book alone. This is the envelope rule most exposed to a kill.
- An exclusion rule for signature members. Each `cosignatures[]` entry is a hybrid signature 'over the same canonical bytes as the row's own', yet `cosignatures` is listed as an envelope member, and JCS over the envelope would include it. Nothing in Part 2 says that `cosignatures` or the row's own signature is removed before canonicalizing.
- A `cohort_scope` row. 2.3.2.3 says the envelope-level fields are 'exactly those in the CC 2.1 table (`cohort_scope`, ...)', but 2.1 has no `cohort_scope` row and its closed vocabulary is never given in Part 2. The values can only be pieced together from 2.5's Scope axis.
- Which timestamp member the envelope carries. `signed_at` (the 2.6.7 future-skew rejection) and `asserted_at` (the W window, judged-at-issuance) are both used normatively, and neither has a 2.1 row.
- A published cross-implementation vector set. 2.6.1.1.1, the base64 pin, number totality and the size cap each say the vector set MUST include a specific case (set vs sequence array, hex vs base64, timestamp, non-representable number under both parser builds, at-cap and cap+1). The repository's manifests/ holds only namespace vectors. CLM-envelope-size itself records the freeze-gate vector pair as pending.
- One complete signed test vector: test keys, an envelope, its JCS bytes, and the Ed25519 and ML-DSA-65 signatures.
- FedCode and `key_id` test vectors. No example code string appears, and the base32 alphabet for the 10-character `key_id` fingerprint is not named (RFC 4648 is implied by the lowercasing but not stated).
- A consolidated table of refusal tokens with retryable or final status. Tokens are scattered across sections: attestation_type_unregistered, delegation_term_required, licensure_delegator_not_authority, purge_unauthorised, purge_selection_digest_mismatch, ENVELOPE_TOO_LARGE, asserted_at_stale, and hard_case:location_proof_resolution_violation.
- A per-profile conformance suite: what a CCP, CCC or CCS must pass, by test id.
- A rule for `scope` as 'a bare token or an array'. These are two canonical byte forms for one meaning, and Part 2 does not say whether a single-element array and a bare token are interchangeable or which one a producer must emit.
- A rule reconciling `cosignatures` order (sorted by `key_id`, per 2.1) with the 2.6.1.1.1 two-kind taxonomy. That taxonomy's default sorts by JCS string form, which for these objects sorts by `ed25519_signature_b64`, not by `key_id`.

**Presenter: jargon**
- The 8.1.1 glossary defines CEG, CEWP, FedCode and steward but none of these terms used in Part 2: Contribution (2.1 onward), `cohort_scope` (2.1, 2.3.3), fold (2.4, 2.4.1.2.1), scrub and scrub set (2.1, 2.4), attestation plane and bytes plane (2.4, 2.4.1.1), carrier (2.4), `consensus_protocol` (2.1, 2.4), DEK (2.1, 2.4.1.2.1), lightnet (2.6.8), Key Pull (2.6.8), trust walk (2.4.1.2.1), Commons scopes (2.6.1.3).
- Labels from other parts or repositories used without expansion: T2, T3, T4a, T5, T6 and T8 (Part 3 trust-root clauses, 2.1); Policy A, L and M (2.1, 2.2, 2.3.3); P4 (5.1, in 2.1 `history_on_join`) alongside an unrelated P1 to P3 in 2.3.2.1; Option-A (2.1); F-3 detector and 'encyclical-sourced translations' (2.1, after the table); D08 and D23 (CIRISAgent design ids, 2.1 and 2.3.4); AV-50 (2.6.1.3); FSD-003 (2.3.2.1, 2.6.8); STH (2.6.7); RATCHET (2.1 `context`); Recursive Golden Rule (2.4.1.3).
- Storage-internal names leak into wire text without definition: `persist_row_hash`, `original_content_hash` and `federation_attestations` (2.6.1.3, 2.4.1.1, 2.4.2). Redaction-scheme terms are used bare: BBS+, JMSW, SD-JWT, mDoc (2.6.1.3).
- Stale and broken pointers: 2.6.1.1 refers to '§4 default', and 2.6.1.5 to '§5.2.1', '§4' and '§8', all from older numbering. 2.6.9.1 has the reversed range 'CC 2.6.2–2.6.1'. The 2.6 Foreword (placed after 2.5) gives the reversed range 'CC 8.2-CC 8.1'.
- Bookkeeping note: 8.6 itself says that checking terminology against the 8.1.1 glossary is not implemented, so nothing mechanical will catch these gaps.

**Presenter: rc8 changes**
- 2.2: CEG is now expanded at first use. 2.6.5 gains the [hybrid-sig] row, which closes a reference that had dangled since 2.2 first cited it (2026-10-14).
- 2.1 `community_id`: now REQUIRED iff `cohort_scope` is community or affiliations, and a hierarchy edge resolves its `role_term` through it (erratum 2026-10-09, CIRISPersist#1048, still staged). The 2.6.1.2 catalog row was not updated and still reads 'admission rejects if cohort_scope == community'. That is an rc8 inconsistency.
- 2.4.1 `delegates_to` term (#163): `delegation_valid_until` is REQUIRED, at most 365 days, on edges scoped sub_delegation, license, grant, moderate, takedown, review or slash, and on edges in an affiliation hierarchy. Violations are refused with `delegation_term_required`. An absent `delegation_valid_from` means `asserted_at`, and an absent `valid_until` on a term-free edge means open-ended (reading 2026-10-09). The formal authority_tree invariant I10 is vendored.
- 2.4.1.1 (#164): the subject-authority carve-out now also covers `licensure:*` and `revocation:*` rows about oneself; the contest path is `reconsideration:{grounds}`.
- 2.4.1.1 (#165, steward ruling 2026-10-09): a purge is a tombstone by digest authorised by a cosigned `takedown_notice` with basis steward_purge. A purge never reaches a key, the accountability record is unpurgeable, derived rows follow the reason, and two refusal tokens are added. Graduated at re-pin 15 on persist c03aae6.
- 2.4 and 2.4.1.2.1 (#161, #162): an organisation is an affiliation. `licensure:{community_key_id}` is a cosigned row whose protocol source is the authority named in the dimension. Authority is judged at issuance on the signed `asserted_at`, and a licence has no public default.
- 2.6.7 (#167): admission freshness window W = 72 h on chain-judged rows, with refusal `asserted_at_stale` (CIRISPersist#1049, still staged).
- Register: pointers follow persist's rename to `resolve_withdraws_admission_rule_at` (re-pin 15). The 2026-10-14 threat-model rows at CC 1.13.3.3 do not touch Part 2's text.

**Presenter: honest limits**
- 2.6.1.1.1: 'byte-identity across conformant implementations is closed by construction.' Soften to 'is the design target'. The signed member set, the exclusion of signature members, the dual form of `scope` and the order of `cosignatures` are not pinned in Part 2. The required vector set is not published, and every established pointer sits in one shared ciris-verify-core codebase.
- 2.6.1.3 and CLM-envelope-size: the row is marked established, but its own summary says the freeze-gate vector pair (at cap, and cap plus one byte) is pending. Say so wherever the cap is cited.
- 2.6.4 versioning: the text says SemVer 'binds strictly' once 1.0 is published, and this is 1.0-rc8. A second implementer should be told that wire-breaking change is still permitted.
- 2.6.7: 'the substrate's network-consensus clock' implies a clock owned by the network. The network has no agency and no such clock is defined. Restate as the consumer's own clock against a known-good source.
- 2.4.1.3: 'no prior identity system (PGP, SPKI/SDSI, W3C VC) typed epistemic-error-admission as a wire primitive' is a comparative priority claim, which the house rules exclude. Restate as what CEG types and why.
- 2.3.5: 'A single shape carries thirteen distinct content cases uniformly'. Only five cases are named and none is enumerated or tested. Drop the count or point to the list.
- 2.4.1.2.1: 'The professional analogue is exact' and 'the domain this federation licenses' overstate the analogy and give the federation agency. Soften to 'illustrative'.
- 2.6.6.1: 'H3 resolution 7 hexagons average ~5 km² edge-length' mixes area and edge length. Resolution 7 averages about 5.16 km² in area, with an edge of about 1.2 km.
- 2.6.1.1.1: 'raw key material — public keys … MUST be lowercase hex … never base64 in canonical bytes' conflicts on its face with the `pubkey_ed25519_base64` and `pubkey_ml_dsa_65_base64` members that 2.1 `recovery_commitments` hashes through JCS. The explicitly-base64 exception covers this, but the wording should say so.
- 2.4.1.1: 'Rule (3) is the elegant answer'. Drop 'elegant', and say plainly that rule 3 depends on the agent's honesty as proxy.
- 2.1 `occurrence_*` and 2.6.8 FedCode are honestly marked self-asserted or unsigned. Keep that wording as it is.

## P03 — The Dictionary (3.1, 3.3, 3.4, 3.5 + the generated registry)

**Reader:** a schema and registry engineer  
**Verdict:** needs work  
**One change:** Make the manifest the complete answer and have the build fail when it is not. Every vocab segment should carry an explicit open:true or open:false with no implicit default, and its values parsed correctly (the '/' and ',' truncation fixed). Every family whose prose states an emitter rule, a 'reserved', or a '-only' should carry a reserved_rule with a non-null cc_ref, starting with R2's own objection, quarantine and wa_adjudication, plus regime, mesh_config, identity:canonical_binding, the substrate-only hard_case kinds and the capacity consent gate. The generator should refuse to build when the prose says 'closed' or names an emitter and the row says otherwise. With that in place, a registry engineer could answer which rows exist, who owns each, and what is closed from namespace_registry.json alone, as R1 promises.

**Promise (presenter):** This book says it is the one place where every dimension family on the wire is catalogued, with an owning component, a polarity class and, where one exists, a reservation (emitter rule). It also says the machine-readable registry is generated from the prose and is the count of record (CC 3.1, 3.1.7 R1). Specifically: a family is registered only if it has a CC 3.1 row (R1). A family with no row is refused loudly as namespace_family_unregistered and is never admitted under a default authority (R2). Segments are compared byte for byte, each segment's case class is carried as data, and nothing is case-folded (R3). The declared polarity is a rule enforced at admission (R4). x_private: never federates (R2). Reserved families are gated at the substrate and at every consumer (CC 3.4, 3.4.7). Relations between rows resolve deterministically when two writes race (CC 3.5.1). For a schema or registry engineer, the book promises that the manifest (_meta, families[].segments[], reserved_rule, polarity_classes) answers three questions without reading prose: which rows exist, who owns each, and which vocabularies are closed. That third promise is the one the manifest only partly keeps today.

**Reviewer: kills**
- 3.1.9.2 slashing:{outcome}: kill the phrase "the substrate's coined `upheld` / `suspension` / `expertise_fraud` admit as open `vocab`". This is the punitive-outcome family, with boolean-via-score polarity, and its outcome vocabulary is open with no values in the manifest. Any coined outcome token admits. The family should be closed at proven_rogue | not_proven, plus whatever is ratified.
- 3.1.9.2 objection:{state} / quarantine:{state}, 3.1.9.4 wa_adjudication:{state}: kill "state lifecycle per the minting cut" and "emitter/composition elaboration rides #67". R2 (3.1.7) names these three families as its motivating failures, yet the manifest still has {state} with no values, no open marker and reserved:false, even though the prose says 'slash-duty-holder-only' and 'WA-quorum-emitted'. These families need closed state sets and machine emitter rules, or R2 has not closed its own examples.
- 3.1.9.2 regime:{artifact}:{version} and mesh_config:{key}: kill "reserved — substrate-steward-emitted, the emitter rule pinned at mint per R2(a); registered to end the ProducerSteward default". The manifest row is reserved:false, and {artifact} is open:true with values ['manifest']. The row says the ProducerSteward default is ended, and the manifest shows it still applies.
- 3.1.9.4 hard_case:{kind}: kill "**Open vocabulary**" as applied to the kinds that 3.4.2 and 3.4.4 reserve to substrate_persist. An open, unreserved {kind} cannot carry those reservations, so anyone can emit hard_case:family_membership_change:v1. The reserved kinds must become closed leaves with a rule. The same row also contradicts itself: it says 'open' and also 'New {kind} values land via the CC 4.5.1 amendment process'.
- 3.3.4 ConsensusProtocol: kill "**`ConsensusProtocol` — open vocabulary**", including `custom:{family_specific_id}` and `weighted:{rubric}`. A substrate evaluates this value at admission (3.1.3.2, 3.3.4 ceremony step 2). An open protocol token means two substrates can return different verdicts on the same row. The set should be closed at founder_only | unanimous | majority | quorum:{m}/{n}.
- 3.4.12 capacity_assurance {domain}: kill "`... digital_identity, ... }  // open per-domain vocabulary`". Admission checks 'scope ⊆ attested_incapacitated_domains' and 'scope ∩ PROTECTED_NON_TRANSFERABLE == ∅'. If the domain vocabulary is open and the protected set is not in the manifest, a novel domain token can route around the apophatic floor. At minimum the protected domains should be closed and machine-readable.
- 3.1.4 / 3.4.3 Edge system:*: this family has no owner row. The prose registers a substrate_edge-reserved system:*, but the manifest has only persist's system:* with rule 'substrate-self-report'. Either register Edge's row with its own rule or strike the claim.
- 3.1.5 retirement:{state}:v1: kill nothing in the prose, but the manifest row as generated (reserved_rule {cc_ref: null, rule: 'reserved (see table)'}, {state} unmarked) leaves a 'closed lifecycle' open by the R3 default. It needs closing in the manifest.
- 3.3.14 identity:canonical_binding: kill "**1+4 preserved** — `identity:canonical_binding:{H}` is a reserved `scores` dimension". The manifest says reserved:false and carries no rule, yet admitting this row widens withdraws authority. Under R1 it is a gated family with no rule the generator can see.

**Reviewer: unanswered**
- Closed versus open, where the prose closes a set and the manifest does not. retirement:{state}:v1 is called a 'closed lifecycle' (3.1.5), but its {state} segment has no values and no open:false, so by R3's default it reads as open. goal:{scale} is ruled to 'stand unchanged at seven tokens' with federation forbidden (3.1.9.7), but the manifest has open:true with values ['self'].
- Correct enumerations: the generator truncates lists separated by '/' or ','. In the manifest, moderation:{allegation_type} = ['rogue_vote'] (prose lists 5), regime:{artifact} = ['manifest'] (prose lists 4), detection:distributive:access:{resource_type} = ['compute'] (prose lists 5), goal:{scale} = ['self'] (prose lists 7). partner_role:{role} and revocation:{entity_type} enumerate values in prose (3.1.1) but carry none in the manifest. A consumer reading segments[].values gets wrong canonical sets.
- Emitter rules a vendor can read from the manifest alone. 21 rows say only 'reserved (see table)', 6 of them with cc_ref null (the five retirement rows and trust:{job}:{version}). Families whose prose states an emitter rule but whose manifest row is reserved:false: quarantine:{state} ('slash-duty-holder-only', 3.1.9.2), wa_adjudication:{state} ('WA-quorum-emitted', 3.1.9.4), regime:{artifact}:{version} ('reserved — substrate-steward-emitted', 3.1.9.2), mesh_config:{key} ('trust-root-emitted', 3.1.9.2), identity:canonical_binding ('reserved dimension', 3.3.14), watchlist:{id} (moderate-holder, 3.1.9.4). Each of these falls to the ProducerSteward default that R2 exists to prevent.
- The capacity:* reserved_rule carries only 'no-self-emit'. The consent-before-scoring admission gate (3.4.5, 3.1.4) is missing from the machine rule on all seven capacity rows.
- Reserved hard_case kinds: 3.4.2 and 3.4.4 restrict roughly 14 kinds (family_membership_change, identity_occurrence_added, lineage_equivocation, recipient_excluded, …) to substrate_persist. The scored dimension is hard_case:{kind} (3.4.2 note), and the manifest row is reserved:false with an unmarked {kind}, so no machine rule protects those kinds.
- Who owns Edge's system:*: 3.1.4 and 3.4.3 register it for substrate_edge, but the manifest has a single system:* row owned by persist, with no separate rule for substrate_edge.
- A registry of subject_kind discriminators: the CC 3.3 kinds (takedown_notice, key_grant, location_proof, family, consent_record, identity_occurrence, organization, org_membership, partner_record, settlement, consent_replication) have no manifest, no owner and no closed/open marker.
- A provisional-versus-ratified status per family. R2(a) defines provisional registration, and accord:human_dignity is provisional (3.4.1), but the manifest has no status key.
- A previous-owner field for the CIRISServer#537 fold, which R1 promises. accord:* (the halt family) is still owned by CIRISRegistry, the repo being folded.
- A join from family to claim id: claims.tsv and the registry meet only by CC section.
- consent_role values (3.4.7.2): BaseRole, Peer, AuthorizedReview and SelfConscience are named but never enumerated, and nothing says whether the set is closed.
- identity_type role vocabulary (3.4.7.1): 'open role vocabulary', and every reserved gate keys on it. The book never lists the role tokens the gates recognise.

**Reviewer: confusions**
- 3.3.4's membership-change ceremony ('Proposer emits a new `family` Contribution superseding the current family Contribution ... with the new membership list') and the Carol example ('Bob proposes a supersedes Contribution adding ...; founder_only admits on Bob's signature alone') contradict 3.1.3.2: 'A supersede never adds a member ... a roster-growing supersede is refused', and every addition needs the joiner's acceptance.
- 3.4.10 says the `witness` identity_type is the emitter role for transparency_log:cosigned:* 'and for nothing else in this section'. 3.4.11 and 3.4.12 give the age_assurance and capacity_assurance emitter rule as `witness ∈ attesting_key.identity_type`. The 3.4.10 table itself says 'witness' in those families means 'not the subject'. That gives two readings of the same gate.
- 3.4.5 finding (4) says 'identity_type is self-asserted, set-valued, and optional at the read surface', while 3.4.7 says every reserved-prefix admission reads identity_type. I cannot tell whether reservations rest on a conferred field or a self-asserted one.
- 3.4.11 contradicts the registry it claims to match. Its construction block shows `age_self_declared:{band}:v1` with band ∈ {minor, adult} (example `age_self_declared:adult:v1`), while 3.1.2 and the manifest register `age_self_declared:band:{band}:{version}` with five bands. It then says rungs 'MAY further qualify `{level}` with a `{verifier_key}` / `{credential_class}`', which the closed {level} segment refuses. 3.3.12 repeats the 'MAY further qualify' phrase and gives age_assurance polarity as 'enumerated', while 3.1.2 and the manifest say 'signed'. Under R4, polarity is an admission rule, so this disagreement is not cosmetic.
- 3.3.7 calls consent:replication 'An open-vocabulary member of the `consent:*` family', while 3.1.5 and 3.3.1 say the consent leaf set is closed and grows only by catalogue entry.
- 3.5.1 rule 3 breaks ties on 'substrate-assigned key (Persist's `federation_attestations.attestation_id`)'. If the id is substrate-assigned, two substrates can pick different winners. The tie-break is deterministic only if attestation_id is derived from content, and CC 2.x, which would say so, is not in this book. The field `signed_at` is also never defined here (the envelope fields cited elsewhere are asserted_at and claimed_at).
- 3.4.1 versus 3.1.1: the reserved-leaves table says 'constitutional: 2-of-3 accord-holder multi-sig', while the 3.1.1 row for accord:lifecycle:confirmed describes 'a strict majority's confirmation of a one-holder `constitutional` halt'. The 3.4.1 table also omits accord:lifecycle:confirmed, giving 6 rows against 7 leaves in the manifest.
- Ledger unit case: 3.1.3 and 3.3.10.1 give the wire example `ledger:head:usd:v1`, while the match vectors use `ledger:head:USD:v1`. The {unit} segment is class external with pattern null, so both spellings pass, and one ledger can be spelled two ways, against R3's one-wire-string rule.
- 3.1.4 capacity:relay_delivery is owned by Edge (transport-delivery) under the capacity: stem, which LensCore otherwise owns. The ownership split is argued in prose, but the manifest's rule for it leaves out the consent gate the prose says binds it.
- 3.4.3 'cross-attested by all stewards in the steward-triple' is never defined in this book and reads as pre-rc7 roster language.
- The 3.1.9.4 table is broken: the watchlist:{id} row appears twice, the hard_case:admin_action paragraph splits the table, and observation:reachability has four columns in a three-column table. The generator happened to parse it, but a human reader cannot see where the Reserved column went.
- The Q1 text in 3.4.13 is garbled at the 'Verified-rung preference' insertion: an open parenthesis is never closed, and a dangling 'Q4/Q7) for safeguarding-sensitive actions' line is left in the middle of the list.
- 3.4.8 says landing the detection:* wildcard gate is 'tracked at CIRISPersist#365 ... not a claim that the gate already ships', but CLM-detector-only is established. I cannot tell which is current.
- Terms this book uses without defining them: CEG (first use in 3.1), M-1 (3.1.5), T2–T8 (CC 3.2, not in this book), WBD, Counter-RII, F-3, N6, AV-17/AV-42, ProducerSteward fallback, Frickerian, 1+4 lockdown, OneOfSix/StewardTriple (3.3.13). Version-lineage tags in headings ('CEG 0.6 addition', '1.0-RC4, ratifies Accord §RC') are noise for an implementer.

**Reviewer: presenter kinder than the text**
- The presenter said every row carries owning_component and owning_repo, 'so the who-owns-each question has a machine answer for all 174 rows'. That holds only for rows that exist. Edge's system:* has no row, the substrate-reserved hard_case kinds have no row, and none of the CC 3.3 subject_kinds is registered. accord:*, the halt family, is owned by the repo being folded away.
- The presenter listed per-family emitter rules as machine-readable reserved_rule text and named six cc_ref:null rows as the exception. The text is worse. 21 rows say only 'reserved (see table)', and seven families whose prose states an emitter rule (quarantine, wa_adjudication, regime, mesh_config, identity:canonical_binding, watchlist, the reserved hard_case kinds) are reserved:false in the manifest. R2's own three motivating families are among them.
- The presenter said 'the capacity:* no-self-emit gate (CC 3.4.5)' is machine-readable without noting that the consent-before-scoring admission gate, which is the ratified half of 3.4.5, is absent from every capacity:* reserved_rule.
- The presenter framed closed versus open as a missing-marker count (76 unmarked). The real defect is different: R3 gives unmarked segments a default (open), and the generator writes wrong enumerations. goal:{scale} = ['self'] open:true, against a ruled seven-token set. moderation, regime and detection:distributive are each truncated to their first value. Missing markers are a gap; wrong values are an error.
- The presenter said 'Relations between rows resolve deterministically when two writes race (CC 3.5.1)' and listed 3.5.1 under complete. Rule 3's tie-break on a 'substrate-assigned key' is deterministic only if that id is derived from content, which this book does not establish.
- The presenter did not mention that 3.3.4 (a family grows by supersede, and founder_only admits on one signature) contradicts 3.1.3.2 (a supersede never adds a member, and the joiner must accept). It also missed that 3.4.11's construction block and its age_self_declared shape contradict the registry row that 3.4.11 says it matches.
- The presenter listed membership:{stage} and custody:{kind} as complete specs and did not flag that 3.3.4's ConsensusProtocol, the predicate those ceremonies are judged under, is an open vocabulary including custom:{id}.

**Reviewer: answered**
- Which rows exist: _meta.n_families = 174, 78 rows reserved:true, no duplicate prefixes. Registration is defined as a catalogue row that the generator can see (3.1.7 R1). An unregistered family is refused as namespace_family_unregistered (R2(b)). x_private: never federates, and its literal is carried as _meta.private_use_prefix (R2).
- Who owns each row, in the mechanical sense: every family carries owning_component and owning_repo. Per-component counts are in _meta.per_component (accord-agent 34, node 48, persist 22, registry 21, attestation 21, lens 15, anti-sybil 6, transport-delivery 6, cirisbench 1). 3.1.7 R1 says how ownership changes on a fold ('a fold keeps the row and moves the owner').
- How the matcher works: 3.1.7 R3 gives byte-exact, full-match comparison; five segment classes carried as data (_meta.case_rule); the trailing :vN rule with an exempt list; variadic '*'; multi-segment placeholders; the rule that a malformed form never falls into open vocabulary; reserved_stems. The one matcher is replayed through manifests/namespace_match_vectors.json. This is specified precisely enough to implement.
- The default for closed versus open: 3.1.7 R3 says 'an enumeration is closed only where the row says so', so an unmarked vocab segment is open by rule, and a value outside a closed set is refused as namespace_vocab_value_unregistered. That gives every segment a default answer, even where the manifest carries no marker.
- Closed sets that are marked open:false in the manifest and enumerated: hardware_custody {platform} (3.1.2), takedown_notice {basis} (3.1.1/3.3.2, 11 values), key_grant {axis} (3.1.3), lineage_witness {stage}, membership {stage} (3.1.3.2), custody {kind} (3.1.3.3), consent:state {stance}, prohibited {category} (3.1.5.4, 22 values), archive_release {basis}, the age_assurance and capacity_assurance level and band segments. accord:* and consent:{kind} carry leaves_closed:true.
- Polarity as data: _meta.polarity_classes treats point sets as points and intervals as ranges (3.1.7 R4).
- Precedence between relations: 3.5 lists eight relations, four of them structural. 3.5.1 orders recants > withdraws > supersedes, then signed_at, then the smallest id, and dedups on (references_attestation_id, attestation_type, attesting_key_id).
- Fully specified families with refusal tokens and stated residuals: session:{kind} (3.1.3.1), membership:{stage} (3.1.3.2), custody:{kind} (3.1.3.3), key_grant (3.3.2), the LegalBasis, WrapAlgorithm and GrantScope wire strings (3.3.2).

**Presenter: complete**

The rules are written precisely enough to implement. - CC 3.1 and 3.1.7 say 'the family count is generated, not asserted' and that the recorded source hash pins prose and manifest together. Today they do not match: _meta.source_sha256 is 29e34de8…, while constitution/part_3_the_namespace.md hashes to bae735a0…. The manifest was last regenerated at 8f80685, and the Part 3 run_kind edit in 80e12ae came after it. That edit is probably prose-only, so registry_sha256 (which excludes source_sha256) may not move, but the claim should be softened until the generator is re-run.

That contradicts CC 4.2.1.1 as ruled at rc7 (#146: one holder's pre-signable row fires; a strict majority resumes). The table also omits accord:lifecycle:confirmed (6 rows, against 7 leaves in CC 3.1.1 and in the manifest). Both should be fixed or flagged before a reviewer reads the halt rule from this book.
- CC 3.3.4 HUMANITY_ACCORD worked example and CC 3.4.1: 'three accord-holder triple' and 'quorum:2/3' are written as the instance, while rc8 CC 4.2.3 widens the roster toward about seven seats. These need to read as the current instance only (the CC 3.4.1 'roster is an instance parameter' paragraph covers this, but the example and the table do not).
- CC 3.1.7 R1/R2, 'a producer MUST NOT ship a family this Part does not register', and the implied promise that the registry is self-sufficient. For closed-versus-open and for emitter rules on six reserved families (five retirement rows and trust:{job}:{version}, all with cc_ref null), the manifest still defers to prose, so a vendor reading only the manifest gets less than R1 says it gets.
- CC 3.1.7 R4, 'a substrate MUST refuse… score_outside_declared_polarity': the rule is normative, but its evidence row CLM-polarity-admission is staged on CIRISPersist#1023. It should be presented as ruled, not as shipped.
- CC 3.1.6 sole-evidence screen for ratchet:flag:* and detection:*: stated as enforcement ('Critical enforcement'), but there is no evidence row at rc8 per the CHANGELOG, and R14 acknowledges that the signed default in 4.4.2 has no bounded-influence rule.
- CLM-registry-of-record (CC 3.1.7) is 'established' with evidence impl:@manifests/namespace_registry.json, so the registry is cited as evidence for itself. That shows the file exists, not that consumers read it.
- Twenty of the 136 rows in this book are staged, across nineteen open tickets. The book should be presented as roughly 82% evidenced, not as a finished dictionary.

## P04 — The Constitution (power: 1.2, 3.2, 4.2, 4.3, 4.5.1)

**Reader:** a governance and legal reviewer, or a regulator  
**Verdict:** needs work  
**One change:** Add one table near the front of the book, with one row per power. Columns: power; holder (as a role noun, defined once, with the CIRIS instance's current occupants given separately); the act it takes and its threshold; the check that ends or limits it; who may exit it and how; and the evidence (claim id and status, model invariant). Rows: halt, confirm, resume, roster change, mesh configuration, emergency relief, root conferral, pipeline blessing, trust-root admission, T7 recovery, key reclaim, guardianship, Wise Authority adjudication, pre-maturity amendment, the 1-of-6 veto, and the prefix gate. Every 'nobody' or 'outside this book' cell would then show for itself, without the default-versus-privilege argument getting in the way. Building the table would also force the 2-of-3, 1-of-6 and halt-scope contradictions (4.2.1.1 vs T3, T8 and 4.2.1.3) to be resolved, because a single cell cannot hold both answers.

**Promise (presenter):** This book tells a governance or legal reviewer, in the text's own words, where power sits in the CIRIS mesh and what limits each power. The powers are: the halt (one accord holder may pause every AI agent that has accepted the root; 4.2.1.1); resumption and roster change (a strict majority of the standing roster; 4.2.1.3, 4.2.6); trust-root founding and admission (a founder quorum, entrenched, which nodes never join; 3.2 "Infrastructure does not vote", T7); mesh configuration (the trust root, which may relieve and may never expand; 4.2.1 rules 1 to 4); stewardship of node and agent keys (one owner per key, reclaimed only on a Wise Authority finding; 3.2 single-owner invariant); guardianship of minors (an adult only; 3.2 guardianship rule); Wise Authority adjudication (4.3); and amendment (4.5.1, with the founder deciding until the mesh reaches maturity). For each power the book says how it ends or is checked, and it keeps a register of the asymmetries that remain (4.5.1). The way out is the same for everyone. A node deletes one acceptance row and every power the root holds over it ends (3.2 T3). Leaving takes effect from that point forward only: you cannot undo a halt that has already fired (4.2.1). Any widening of the accord's reach must wait 72 hours so that people can leave first (4.2.1). The book states plainly that an agent has no exit and that the Wise Authority board has neither a bound nor an exit (4.5.1 register).

**Reviewer: kills**
- 4.2.1 'Blessing a build pipeline' vs 4.2.1 'pre-committed powers only, never act-then-ratify'. The pipeline-blessing power was added to the entrenched enumeration with the note: 'It records the mechanism already in production (CIRISServer /v1/accord/ci-key; persist is_infra_attest_effective)'. That is act-then-ratify, which the same section forbids. No 72 h severance window is recorded for this widening of the accord's reach. Kill 'It records the mechanism already in production ... and changes no signed bytes' as a justification. Either record the severance window that ran, or carry the widening on the 4.5.1 register.
- 3.2 T3, T8 and 4.2.1.3 vs 4.2.1.1, on what a halt reaches. T3 makes 'no halt latched' a leg of every candidate root, and says a failed root means 'every downstream gate (serve, agent capability, manifest serving, vouching, score validity) fails closed'. T8 lists 'the halt latched (→ NotRooted)', and 4.2.1.3 says 'a halted node is not serving'. 4.2.1.1 says the halt 'does not stop a node ... serving ... continue exactly as before, because none of them reads the latch'. These two accounts of the scope of the most important power in the book contradict each other, and CLM-nsproc-halt-latched-trust is established on persist trust_root_valid. Kill 'a halted node is not serving', or restrict the T3 and T8 halt leg explicitly to agent capability.
- 4.5.1 'Read as defaults, not privileges ... founder amendment authority under the maturity gate' (with 'accord-holder permanence' and the '1-of-6 sign-off and its veto'). This is a power with no paired check. One person, or any one holder, amends every surface, including the entrenched 4.2 surface and the maturity gate itself. 4.2.3 says this is checked 'by nothing mechanized before' maturity. It is kept off the register because it can be exited 'by not using that instance', and that exit is not a check on the power. The register's own four-cell test (bounded, revocable exit, scope-isolated) would mark it a defect, because the bound can be moved by the holder.
- 4.2.6 'Why this restores the accord and cannot seize it' / 'it grants no path to weaken or seize the halt-authority beyond these stated residuals'. The Lean model carries this claim at the roster layer only. Every rule in 4.2.6 was itself changed three times in October and 'ratified by the founder under the maturity gate', so the amendment plane is a fourth path to weaken the halt, and no model covers it. Either name it as a fourth residual or kill 'cannot seize it'.
- 3.2 T7 'so it cannot be a seizure vector'. The same paragraph concedes that a conferring body able to retire and confer founders 'can replace a stalled roster seat by seat'. For ciris-canonical the conferring body (the accord) and the founders (the accord holders) are the same three keys, so the only check on the founders' recovery power is the founders. TrustRootVerdict.tla models recovery but not this residual. Narrow it to 'cannot be a seizure vector for anyone but the conferring body'.
- 4.2.3 'any one of whom may fire the halt, two of whom resume it', its table row '1 / 2-of-3 / majority', and 'The 2-of-3 multi-sig verifier at CC 4.2.1.1 is the quorum:2/3 enforcement'. These conflict with 4.2.1.1, which says the halt is one holder's signature and that every threshold is 'M of N over the standing roster, never a fixed 2-of-3'. Since 4.2.3 also says the roster is widening toward seven, a reader cannot tell which threshold binds. Kill the fixed 2-of-3 wording.
- 4.2.6 lame-duck rule: 'a valid accord_participation from them fires'. Firing no longer rides a participation. The same section also says 'A server-issued freshness nonce stops a replayed signature' and says the nonce is minted by 'the proposer ... never a server'. 'Steward PQC publication: ... the (key-independent, H6) steward-backstop PQC halves' names a body the section removes. These are stale descriptions of who holds power. Kill all three.
- 4.5.1 step 5 '1-of-6 accord-holder OR steward sign-off' and 4.5.1.2 'NOT the 2-of-3 from CC 4.5.1 step 5'. The step names a veto held by six parties who do not exist under the current roster, and the cross-reference gives the wrong count. A veto whose holders cannot be identified is a power with no identifiable check.
- 4.2.5 'First-mover is not structural'. No model or register row carries this claim. The canonical root ships pinned on every conformant deployment (3.2 'default trust anchor'), two of three holders share a household (4.2.3), and the roster has not yet been widened. Present it as a claim, or drop it.

**Reviewer: unanswered**
- Who seats the Wise Authority, and what checks that body. 4.3 defers appointment, rotation, recusal and appeals to CC 1.16.5 and Annex B. The 4.5.1 register declares the board's self-perpetuation unbounded with no exit. Yet a Wise Authority finding is the only gate on key reclaim and seizure reversal (3.2). The book names a power held by a body it cannot identify and itself calls a defect.
- How an ordinary community member leaves. The member's own exit row is in CC 3.1.3.2, which is not in this book. 3.2 only says that a geographic member's location_proof stays after leaving ('a one-way disclosure').
- Who applies the prefix gate in 1.2, and what checks them. T2 says a failing prefix 'gets renamed', but no body is named to decide a failure or order a rename, and CLM-admission is staged.
- Who may amend before maturity, and what checks them. 4.5.1 says 'the founder, and ... any accord-holder' but never says whether one holder acting alone may amend, how an amendment is recorded, or what checks it. 4.2.3 concedes it is checked 'by nothing mechanized before' maturity. Nothing says who measures the >=100,000-node threshold, and the founder may move it.
- Who the six in '1-of-6 accord-holder OR steward sign-off' are (4.5.1 step 5). The roster has three holders, and the regional stewards were removed (4.2.6). Under 4.5.1.3 this step also governs reclaiming a squatted name.
- What check the minor has on the guardian. A guardian's withdrawal leaves the minor 'steward-less and MUST NOT operate' (3.2), so one adult's act silences the minor, and no check on that act appears. Adjudicated incapacity of an adult (CC 3.4.12) is cited, but who adjudicates is not in the book.
- Accord-holder replacement runs through 'the out-of-band CIRIS L3C process at FEDERATION_ANNOUNCEMENT.md §4' (4.2.3), which is outside the book. 4.2.3 calls it 'not a constitutional requirement'.
- Whether the agent can ever leave. 4.5.1 points to 'an act of the HUMANITY_ACCORD under first contact', which it also says is 'not defined at rc8'.
- Who 'the founder' and 'the steward' are as role nouns. Ratifications are by 'the founder', and the 4.2.6 amendments were made 'on the steward's objection'. The book never says whether these are one person, or whether that person is also holder 1 and part of the two-holder household named in 4.2.3.

**Reviewer: confusions**
- 'Founder' means two things: a community role (3.2) and 'the founder', the single maintainer with amendment authority (4.5.1, the 4.2.6 ratifications). It is never defined once as a role noun.
- 'Steward' has four meanings: the key steward (3.2), 'steward ruling' (the maintainer's decisions), the removed regional stewards (4.2.6), and the steward who signs off in 4.5.1 step 5. A legal reader cannot tell which steward holds which power.
- The 'CC 1 reading rule' (slot, default, privilege) decides which powers appear on the asymmetry register, but the rule itself is in Part 1 and not in this book. The distinction between default and privilege is doing the main work of the book's argument, and this reader cannot check it.
- 4.5.1 steps 1 to 4 cite NodeCore P5, P8, P10 and P11. None is defined in the book, so the mature-federation check on amendment cannot be read here.
- WBD, PDMA, CCC and CCS are used without being expanded, and WA is used before it is expanded in places.
- Outside references are used as explanation and never defined for this reader: TUF, Certificate Transparency, Sigstore, Keybase and Secure Scuttlebutt (T4a, T6), KERI pre-rotation, Casper FFG inactivity leak, Enoch Arden, Delaware/BFT. Wire field names such as cohort_subkind, delegates_to, revoked_after and asserted_at stand in where role nouns are needed. This exceeds my jargon budget.
- 3.2 opens with about 150 lines of community DEK, archive_mode and civic or emergency composition tables (AMBER alert, FOIA, ballots) before any power is stated. The ballot row ('vote tallies are consumer-side composition') quietly says who counts a civic vote, and then never pairs that with a check.
- The packet header lists 'formal/accord_halt — 0 checked names', but AccordHalt_row.cfg and AccordHaltFuse.cfg check Inv_LoneBounded, Inv_MajorityCanResume, Inv_ResumeNeedsHonest, Inv_FireEnabled, Prop_ConfirmedPersists and others. As rendered, the halt's checks look unmodelled. This is a gap in the packet tooling.
- 4.2.1 rule 3 says emergency relief is 'not renewable back-to-back by the same holder'. It is not clear whether a second holder can renew, which would let relief run indefinitely by rotating holders, and no check is stated for that case.
- The ciris-canonical founders are the accord holders, and the T7 conferring body for ciris-canonical is the accord. It is unclear whether any check on this trust root's admission power exists outside the three people who hold it.

**Reviewer: presenter kinder than the text**
- The presenter lists 'zero data disruption by construction' as complete and qualifies it only by noting the server scan. The text itself contradicts the claim: T3, T8 (halt latched → NotRooted, which fails 'serve' closed) and 4.2.1.3 ('a halted node is not serving'). That is a conflict inside the text about how far the halt reaches, not only a question of evidence.
- The presenter puts 4.2.1 under 'complete', including the 72 h widening rule. The presenter does not mention that pipeline blessing was added by ratifying a mechanism 'already in production', which the same section's 'never act-then-ratify' rule forbids.
- The presenter says the register 'separates declared privileges from entrenched structure'. Kinder than the text: that separation also moves founder amendment authority, accord-holder permanence and the 1-of-6 veto off the register altogether by calling them 'defaults'. The presenter's honest_limits come close (on entrenchment resting on one person's discipline), but the presenter does not say that the definition is what keeps these powers from being checked.
- The presenter says 'The way out is the same for everyone', and then lists the agent and the party deferring to the Wise Authority as exceptions. The text shows more exceptions than the presenter names. A minor cannot leave a guardian, and a guardian's withdrawal silences the minor. A geographic member's location disclosure survives leaving. An operator's exit does not reach a halt already fired.
- The presenter says 4.2.6 'Recovery keys ... never help a seizure' and that three residuals are named. Accurate for the roster layer. But the text's 'cannot seize it' conclusion leaves out the amendment plane, which 4.2.3 itself concedes, and the presenter does not draw that link.
- The presenter puts 4.2.3's 2-of-3 wording and 4.2.6's stale sentences under honest_limits, which treats them as tone. For a reader whose whole question is who holds the power, they are wrong statements of thresholds, so I treat them as kills.
- The presenter counts 3.2 stewardship and reclaim as complete. The text makes the Wise Authority the only check on reclaim and seizure reversal, and the book declares that body a defect and cannot say who seats it. The presenter notes this in honest_limits, but it means the stewardship entry is not complete as a pairing of power and check.

**Reviewer: answered**
- Who may halt, and what checks the halt: any one accord holder fires a halt by signing one row. The halt lapses after a 24 h fuse unless a strict majority of the standing roster confirms it, and a strict majority may lift it at any time (4.2.1.1 'The fuse', the signatures-per-kind table). The power and its check sit side by side in the text. formal/accord_halt/AccordHaltFuse.cfg checks Inv_LoneBounded and Inv_MajorityCanResume, but the packet header leaves them out (see confusions).
- Resumption: only accord:lifecycle:active can resume, cosigned by a strict majority of the standing roster. It names the single halt it ends (resumes_halt_id), can be verified offline, and no Annex F operator tier can resume a constitutional halt (4.2.1.3).
- Roster change: decided by a strict majority of the standing roster within a 72 h participation window. Capture is resisted by a minority, cannot be stopped by a majority, and the roster freezes once more than half are lost. All three residuals are named (4.2.6). The Lean theorems the header lists (minority_capture_resisted, majority_capture_unstoppable, recovery_keys_never_help_seizure) back this claim at the roster layer.
- How the accord's power is limited: its powers are a closed list (halt, notify, drill, resumption, root conferral, pipeline blessing). Its silence confers no authority, it cannot reach the consent or licensure planes, and it is stated to reach the amendment plane (4.2.1, 4.2.3).
- Mesh configuration: held by the trust root on the delegation plane. Checks: relieve-never-expand, a closed key registry, threshold-1 emergency relief capped at a 72 h TTL, and durable settings only by the root's own quorum (4.2.1 rules 1 to 4).
- Who founds and admits for a trust root: founders by quorum:M/N, entrenched, with M >= 2 and N >= M+1. A node-bearing key can never be a founder (3.2 'Infrastructure does not vote'). The stalled state and its one bounded recovery row are in 3.2 T7.
- How an operator or node leaves: deleting one acceptance row ends every power the root holds (3.2 T3). Exit takes effect from that point forward only, judged against the halt's asserted_at (4.2.1 'Reach is consent-scoped'). Any widening of reach waits out a 72 h severance window (4.2.1).
- Who owns a key and how ownership is reclaimed: one owner per key, transfer is explicit, and reclaiming takes a four-step ceremony on a Wise Authority finding of abandonment (90-day floor) or seizure, passing through an unowned state (3.2 single-owner invariant, recovery gate, abandonment predicate).
- Guardianship: an adult can never be stewarded, a minor's guardian must be an adult, and the guardian's own signature is the guardian's consent (3.2 guardianship rule, user-target admission rule).
- Wise Authority liveness and denominators: entrenched or roster-changing decisions count against the full seated roster, and deferral-answering counts against the live set (4.3).
- Group bans: no power exists to ban a room mesh-wide, and the text says why (3.2 'Forbidding a room'; formal/mesh_net Prop_NoBan).
- Where a party has no exit: the 4.5.1 register states that the agent has no exit and that a party deferring to the Wise Authority has no exit (both rows).

**Presenter: complete**
- 4.2.1 Authority scope. The accord's powers are a closed list (halt, notify, drill, resumption, root conferral, pipeline blessing), and they cannot reach the consent or licensure planes. Reach depends on consent: it is resolved against the trust edges that existed at the halt's asserted_at, so leaving takes effect from that point forward only. Widening the accord's reach waits out a 72 h severance window. The text says outright that the accord can reach the amendment plane rather than denying it. Mesh-config rules 1 to 4: relieve-never-expand, a closed key registry, emergency relief limited to 72 h, and durable settings only by quorum.
- 4.2.1.1 The halt. It is one holder's row, can be signed in advance, fires on receipt and latches. It pauses agent acts only, with zero data disruption by construction. The 24 h fuse runs from the moment each node receives the halt, and the halt stands longer only if a standing-roster majority confirms it. A majority may lift an unconfirmed halt at any time. The table of signatures per kind is the single source. Evidence: formal/accord_halt/AccordHalt.tla and AccordHaltFuse.tla close with no violations (AccordHalt_row.cfg at 19,408 states; AccordHaltFuse at 319,090 states after the resume change). CLM-halt-authority graduated at re-pin 17 on CIRISServer c70f96bb. The server halves of CLM-halt-one-row landed, with witness rows showing that no data path reads the latch.
- 4.2.1.3 Resumption. It needs a strict majority of the standing roster, and resumes_halt_id ties it to the one halt it ends. It can be verified offline. No operator tier in Annex F can resume a constitutional halt. Register: CLM-lifecycle is established.
- 4.2.6 Roster change. It is counted against the standing roster, so seizure needs floor(N/2)+1 coerced holders and censorship gains the adversary nothing. A named holder keeps fire authority through a lame-duck period. Recovery keys bind key material and never help a seizure. Three residuals are named: duress, a coerced majority, and a frozen roster once more than half are lost. Evidence: formal/accord_roster/AccordRoster.lean has 17 checked theorems, including minority_capture_resisted, majority_capture_unstoppable and recovery_keys_never_help_seizure, with no sorry, and it runs in CI.
- 3.2 Trust-root founding and the no-vote rule. Infrastructure communities use quorum:M/N over founders, entrenched, with M >= 2 and N >= M+1. A node-bearing key can never be a founder. A consumer can always re-root, and trust is a separate relation from membership. Register: CLM-infrastructure-does-not-vote, CLM-canonical-founders-accord, CLM-liveness-margin and CLM-canonical-community-row are all established on CIRISPersist symbols.
- 3.2 T1 to T4a and T7. Leaving is one row (T3). Liveness is a signal and never a validity gate (T4). Attaching requires a fresh head, but staying attached does not (T4a). A stalled root stays valid but cannot admit anyone. Recovery adds a human founder and nothing more: no member can be removed and M cannot be changed (T7). The residual power of the conferring body to replace founders seat by seat is named. Register: CLM-reclaim-authority-fails-closed, CLM-attach-freshness and CLM-witnessed-lineage are established.
- 3.2 Stewardship and guardianship. Steward binding is grounded in custody. A capability grant is not stewardship. Each key has one owner. Reclaiming a key goes through four steps on a Wise Authority finding of abandonment (90-day floor) or seizure, and passes through an unowned state on the way. An adult can never be stewarded, and a minor's guardian must be an adult. Register: the CLM-nsproc-* ownership rows and CLM-steward-binding-by-custody are established on persist admission.rs.
- 4.5.1 The register of declared asymmetries. It separates declared privileges from entrenched structure. The Wise Authority board row is marked as a defect (no bound, no exit). The maker's-power row states that the agent has no exit. The swap test's blind spot is stated (a rule that is formally universal can still gate access through hardware).
- 4.2.2 and 4.2.2.1 Hardware classes. Any class not in the table counts 0.0. The class is corroborated by the verified attestation chain, not by the string the holder asserts. All four platform chains are established in CIRISVerify.

**Presenter: partial (and what it leans on)**
- 4.2.1.1 The halt does not yet reach agents through a verified path. The agent act gate (the check that 'an agent checks the latch before every effectful act' depends on) is staged on CIRISAgent#1231 (PR #1262). CLM-accord-reach is staged on CIRISPersist#803. For a regulator this is the main open item: the brake is established in verify and server, but the agent side is not.

CLM-standing-verdict is staged on CIRISConstitution#127. formal/trust_root/TrustRootVerdict.tla closes at 22,152,480 states, but it checks only (i), (ii), (iv), (vi), (viii), T7 and recovery; (iii), (v) and (vii) are stated as holding by construction. The text does not say what happens to the community's quorum:2/3 when the accord roster widens toward seven (4.2.3, rc8). Holders qualify as founders directly, so the founder count may change while M stays fixed at 2.

**Presenter: honest limits**
- 4.2.1.1, 'An agent checks the latch before every effectful act'. Soften to 'is specified to check'. The agent act gate is staged on CIRISAgent#1231, and the rc7 cut note says the kill switch does not yet reach agents through a verified path.

Present it as 'by construction, now enforced by a mutant-verified scan', not as something that was never in doubt.
- 4.2.3: inconsistencies left from rc7. The section says 'two of whom resume it', its table says '2-of-3', and it says 'The 2-of-3 multi-sig verifier at CC 4.2.1.1 is the quorum:2/3 enforcement'. But 4.2.1.1 now fires on one holder, and 4.2.1.1 and 4.2.6 say every threshold is M of N, 'never a fixed 2-of-3'. With the roster widening toward seven, this text will mislead a reviewer.
- 4.2.6, the lame-duck rule says 'a valid accord_participation from them fires'. Firing no longer rides a participation, because a fire is one invoke row. The same section says 'A server-issued freshness nonce stops a replayed signature' and also that the proposer mints the nonce, 'never a server'. Its 'Steward PQC publication' sentence still names the 'steward-backstop' that the section removes. These three are stale sentences.
- 4.5.1 step 5 is '1-of-6 accord-holder OR steward sign-off'. Six assumes three regional stewards who no longer exist (4.2.6, removed in rc7/#139). 4.5.1.2 says 'NOT the 2-of-3 from CC 4.5.1 step 5', but step 5 is 1-of-6. 4.5.1.2's fixed '2-of-3 HUMANITY_ACCORD signatures' also conflicts with 'M of N over the standing roster'.
- 4.5.1 and 4.5.1.2, entrenchment. Before maturity, every entrenched amendment to 4.2 in rc7 and rc8 was 'ratified by the founder under the maturity gate'. In practice the entrenchment rests on one person's discipline until a threshold of >=100,000 nodes, which that same person may move. Present it that way, and do not lead with 'entrenched'.
- 4.2.5, 'no federation-internal protocol path to that signature exists' and 'First-mover is not structural'. Pair these with the household-correlation fact in 4.2.3 (two of three holders share a household, so the resumption majority can be assembled at one address) and with the fact that the roster has not yet been widened.
- 3.2 T6, 'two witnessed heads are equivocation'. This is true only once witnessed mode is on. The shipped roots run with it off for an indefinite period, so founder equivocation is undetectable on the wire today.

It does not model the shipped mode (witnessed off) or the five review corners it says must be encoded first.
- 3.2 T7 recovery 'cannot be a seizure vector'. The same paragraph concedes that a conferring body able both to retire and to confer founders can replace a stalled roster seat by seat. Say 'cannot be a seizure vector for anyone but the conferring body'.

## P05 — The Reader's Policies (4.1 anti-patterns, 4.4 composition)

**Reader:** an authorization and graph reviewer writing a consumer  
**Verdict:** needs work  
**One change:** Add one normative 'Consumer verdict contract' at the head of 4.4.3, and make every policy in the library fill in the same row. That row would say: the input trust set and exactly which edge extends it (resolving the item-3 edge to node→root trust:accepts:v1 per CC 3.2 T3, and saying whether Policy A counts delegates' attestations); the aggregator (resolving Policy A against 4.4.2); the threshold default; the outcome set (trust / refuse / degrade / abstain-on-empty); and the refusal conditions, including depth cap, lapsed term, withdraws and the concentration cap with root_trust defined. Any policy that cannot fill in the refusal column (today B, C, D and F Layer 3) is marked non-normative until it can.

**Promise (presenter):** You are writing the consumer. This book says the substrate only carries signed edges and you compute every verdict. It gives you a conformance floor and a library of named policies to compute them with. The floor is Policy A (4.4.3.8): trust what your pinned set signed, aggregate mean(score × confidence) per (dimension, attested key), and apply your own threshold. Policy A sits on a pluggable trust graph. The self-root is the floor, a root is a self-delegating charter, grants under it can only narrow (attenuation-bound), deleting one edge un-trusts a root, and federated capability fails closed when no external root is present (4.4.3.8 items 1-8). Above that floor, 4.4.2 gives default aggregators per polarity column. 4.1 tells you which shapes to refuse or downweight: delegation depth capped at 5 by default, cycles refused, a concentration cap of 0.5 × root_trust, and the withdraws:recants ratio watch. The membership resolvers (Policy M 4.4.3.2, Policy L 4.4.3.4) tell you who is inside a cohort. The enforced-admission rule (4.4.3.4.3) says when a delegated moderate, takedown, review, license or grant act is refused rather than merely unweighted. The book's answer to "whom do I trust" is: whoever your pins reach through attenuating, term-bound, depth-capped delegates_to chains, never whoever registered. Its answer to "what is the refusal path" is answered well at the admission door. At the composition layer the answer is uneven, and this presentation says where.

**Reviewer: kills**
- 4.4.3.12 Policy B: 'Consumer trusts an attestation if `attesting_key_id` has been vouched for by the pinned trust set. Adds one hop of indirection.' This is a transitive trust grant with no refusal path. It does not name which edge counts as 'vouched' (a positive score, delegates_to, or trust:confers:v1), whether the vouch must carry a scope, or whether term, withdraws or 4.1.1's concentration cap apply. As written, any positive emission from a pin admits a stranger. Kill it until it names the vouching edge and the conditions under which the vouch is refused.
- 4.4.3.13 Policy C: 'transitive-trust propagation across the full attestation graph, weighted by canonical-bootstrap distance with confidence decay per hop.' It gives no seed set, no decay function, no damping, no convergence bound and no threshold, and it never says when a key is NOT trusted. Propagation over 'the full attestation graph' also appears to ignore the 4.1.1 five-hop cap. A policy whose only exit is 'less weight' has no refusal path. Kill it.
- 4.4.3.7 Policy F Layer 3: 'a non-canonical `agent_files:*` attestation accumulates NodeCore P4 votes. Consumer policy may elevate trust once an accumulated-weight threshold is reached.' It gives no threshold, no voter-eligibility rule and no Sybil or concentration bound, and it does not say who may vote or when elevation is refused. Layer 1 protects the install endpoint, but Layer 3 as a composition policy has no refusal path. Kill Layer 3, or bind it to Policy A's pinned voters.
- 4.4.3.9 Policy D: 'measured by `affected_population_estimate` in the attestation `context`, weighted inversely (smaller = more vulnerable, more weight).' The tie-break input is self-declared and attacker-controlled, and nothing refuses or caps an implausible estimate. A tie-break that the contesting party can set is a win path, not a policy. Kill it as written, or require the estimate to come from a non-party attester under the trust set.

**Reviewer: unanswered**
- Does Policy A trust only keys IN the pin set, or also keys the pins reach by delegation? 4.4.3.8 opens with 'attesting_key_id is in the consumer's pinned trust set' (direct, zero hops). Items 1-8 then describe a graph whose 'validity flows from a shared root'. The text never says whether an attestation signed by a root's delegate counts under Policy A, or whether that is Policy B. This is the core of 'whom do I trust', and it is left open.
- The verdict contract for Policy A: 'Consumer threshold determines verdict' (4.4.3.8). There is no default threshold, no abstain or unknown outcome, and no stated behaviour when the trusted-attester set for a (dimension, key) tuple is empty. An empty set gives a mean over nothing.
- Which aggregator applies: Policy A says 'mean of score × confidence' for every tuple, while 4.4.2 says boolean-via-score is Min, positive-only is Max, enumerated is Most-recent and detectors use Median. The text never says whether 4.4.2 overrides Policy A or Policy A overrides 4.4.2.
- A refusal path for the transitive policies, B (4.4.3.12) and C (4.4.3.13). Neither has a seed set, decay, damping, convergence bound or threshold, and neither says how the 4.1.1 depth cap or the 0.5 × root_trust concentration cap applies.
- What '0.5 × root_trust' means. root_trust is not defined anywhere in the book, so the concentration cap in 4.1.1 cannot be computed.
- What 'treated as attestation:self_verify only' means operationally (4.1.1). Should I drop the attestation, keep it at self-attested weight, or emit something? For a consumer, 'degrade to a mechanism prefix' is not a defined action.
- Who owns the depth cap at admission. 4.4.3.4.3 says the chain is 'depth-capped per CC 4.1.1', but 4.1.1 makes the cap 'Consumer policy MUST ... (configurable)'. An admission-time refusal cannot depend on each consumer's own setting. The substrate-versus-consumer split of 4.1.1's MUSTs is never stated once.
- Whether term expiry is part of the enforced-admission test. 4.4.3.4.3 says 'live delegates_to chain', and 'live' is never defined there. 4.4.3.2.8 refuses perpetual edges (delegation_term_required), but the admission test does not say that a lapsed delegation_valid_until on any hop refuses the act.
- The window and minimum sample for 4.1.4. 'Rolling window' has no length, and a new attester's 1:0 or 0:0 ratio is undefined.
- The negative path in Policy I (4.4.3.6). ladder_verdict uses 'any positive attestation' per rung and never consults negatives, withdraws or expiry. It does not say whether a rung with a conflicting negative is refused.
- A guard on Policy D (4.4.3.9). affected_population_estimate is self-declared in context, and no safeguard is given against an attester claiming a tiny cohort to win ties.
- The proxy rule in resolve_consent (4.4.3.5.5). It accepts any 'attesting_key_id ∈ delegates_to(s).proxies' with no scope check (consent_revocation), no depth cap and no term check. The book's own enforced-admission discipline is not applied on this read path.
- A single consumer-side table of refusal outcomes: refuse at admission, degrade, downweight, emit hard_case, or defer. Today the answers are scattered across 4.1.1, 4.1.3, 4.4.3.1.1, 4.4.3.4.1, 4.4.3.4.3, 4.4.3.6 and 4.4.3.8 item 5.

**Reviewer: confusions**
- The direction and label of the acceptance edge contradict another part of the text. 4.4.3.8 item 3 says 'A consumer trusts one by hanging `delegates_to(user → root, [infra:attest, infra:serve])` off the base'. That is a capability-bearing user→root edge with no trust:* job label. CC 3.2 T3/T4a (and the 4.1.1 row in this very book) say the lever is a node→root `trust:accepts:v1` edge that 'confers nothing on the root', MUST carry attached_head_digest, and that 'A new row with no `trust:{job}` label gives no acceptance'. An implementer following 4.4.3.8 literally writes an edge that does not attach. Worse, if the root ever delegates a capability back to that user, it closes a two-key capability cycle that 4.1.1 refuses.
- The self-root has two different forms. 4.4.3.8 item 1 says it is 'a plain `attestation(user → user)`'. 4.1.1 says the charter or self-root form is `delegates_to(A → A)`.
- Option A forward secrecy contradicts itself. 4.4.3.2.2 says 'The CC 4.4.3.4.5 Option A discipline applies identically: on member removal the substrate rotates the community DEK'. 4.4.3.4.5, defining Option A, says 'No DEK rotation; no re-encryption of historical content.'
- The resolvers walk 'supersedes chain → latest non-superseded' (resolve_community 4.4.3.2.4, resolve_family 4.4.3.4.4, resolve_self 4.4.3.4.6). The admission text two paragraphs earlier says membership is a new roster version chained by prev_head_digest, 'never a single-signer `supersedes`' (rc7 erratum). For a graph reviewer, the resolver and the admission gate walk different graphs.
- admit_family_change (4.4.3.4.2) is broken pseudo-code. Every match arm `return`s before the acceptance loop runs, and `admit` is never initialised. As written, 'nobody joins without their own consent' is unreachable.
- 4.4.3.2.5 worked example, step 4: 'No key_grant cascade (community is unencrypted)'. This contradicts the MANDATORY community DEK in 4.4.3.2.1. Step 2 uses `supersedes` plus member signatures, which the erratum retired, and omits the joiner's acceptance.
- 4.4.3.2.6 'open → consensus_protocol = "open:self_admit"' is not one of the six canonical kinds. It also conflicts with the `public` subkind path in 4.4.3.2.3.
- 4.4.2 lists `attestation:l*` as a Min-aggregated hard constraint, while 4.1.3 and 4.4.3.6 say attestation:l{N}:* is rejected at admission. One of these is stale.
- There are stale cross-references that I cannot navigate: §8.1.13.1, §8.1.13.2, §8.1.12.3, §8.1.12.3.1, §8.1.12.4, §8.1.12.7, §8.1.11.4, §5.6.8.8.1, §5.6.8.10, §0.8.2 and §4.2.2.3, plus anchors such as #814-policy-d..., #83-frickerian..., and #81132-community-admission....
- Undefined in this book: root_trust (4.1.1), 'live' chain (4.4.3.4.3), '1+4', G3, AV-17, R1/Q1, T1/T3/T4a/T6/T7, NodeCore P4/P8, LensCore, CIRIS L3C, HITL/HOTL/HOOTL, acceptance_admits, and 'explicitly invited' in Policy H's community_feed (4.4.3.3).
- Policy H is labelled '(LIVE)' but has no register row. Policy G's '~20 substrate stories' and Policy C's 'less common in practice' are frequency claims with no source.
- 4.4.3.2.8 is mostly regulatory configuration vocabulary with no enforcing row for most fields. It sits in a composition-policy book and buries the one part a graph reviewer needs, the 365-day term on role, issuance and moderation edges.

**Reviewer: presenter kinder than the text**
- The presenter's answer to 'whom do I trust' ('whoever your pins reach through attenuating, term-bound, depth-capped delegates_to chains') is kinder than the text. Policy A's operative sentence is 'attesting_key_id is in the consumer's pinned trust set'. The book never states that pins extend through delegates_to chains for scoring purposes, and the term bound is staged (CLM-delegation-term) and is not part of Policy A.
- The presenter calls 4.4.3.8 'the strongest section for this reader' and does not flag that its item 3 edge (delegates_to user→root with infra scopes, no trust:accepts:v1 label, no attached_head_digest) contradicts both 4.1.1 in this packet and CC 3.2 T3/T4a. An implementer following the strongest section writes an edge that does not attach.
- The presenter missed the Policy A versus 4.4.2 aggregation conflict. Policy A says mean for everything, while 4.4.2 gives Min, Max, Most-recent and Median by polarity.
- The presenter missed the Option A contradiction. 4.4.3.2.2 says Option A rotates the DEK, and 4.4.3.4.5 says Option A does not rotate.
- The presenter calls the enforced-admission rule 'the clearest refusal path in the book'. It is, but the presenter does not note that the rule is 'depth-capped per CC 4.1.1', where the cap is a configurable consumer-policy MUST, so an admission-time refusal depends on an unnamed party's setting. 'Live' is also undefined, and lapsed terms are not stated as refusing.
- The presenter missed that resolve_consent (4.4.3.5.5) admits proxies via 'delegates_to(s).proxies' with no scope, depth or term check. That is a revocation path that bypasses the book's own enforced-admission discipline.
- The presenter treats 4.1.1's depth cap as a concrete refusal ('counts as a refusal of transitive trust'). The text only says 'treated as attestation:self_verify only', which is not a defined consumer action. The 0.5 × root_trust cap rests on an undefined quantity.
- The presenter puts Policy F Layer 3 and Policy D under partial or missing rather than recognising them as kill candidates under this reader's standing. Both are composition policies with no refusal path.

**Reviewer: answered**
- Whom do I trust at the floor: 4.4.3.8. A conforming consumer MUST implement Policy A (4.4 preamble), which trusts an attestation if attesting_key_id is in the pinned trust set. The recommended default pin is ciris-canonical founders plus humanity-accord, resolved live, and the text calls it 'never *the* root'.
- How a root becomes trustworthy: 4.4.3.8 items 2, 5 and 8. The self-charter is the capability ceiling. The root validity minimum is [infra:serve, infra:attest]. Federation fails closed with no external root (item 5, an explicit refusal path). Out-of-band fingerprint comparison is a first-class attach step (item 8).
- How I un-trust: 4.4.3.8 item 4. Deleting one edge revokes roles, score validity, vouching and kill-switch authority, and the self-root keeps running. This is a clean exit path.
- Delegation-chain refusal: 4.1.1. Depth is capped at 5 by default with a 16-hop substrate clamp, and longer chains get no transitive trust. Cycles are refused at emission. The text says exactly what is NOT a cycle: a self-edge, and a trust:accepts:v1 edge in either arrival order.
- Refusal path for scoped acts: 4.4.3.4.3, the enforced-admission rule. A moderate, takedown, review, license or grant act is admitted only if the signer is the delegator or sits on a live, scope-matching, depth-capped, revocable delegates_to chain. 'Reject otherwise.' An issuance whose delegator lacks the underlying authority is refused at admission, and a grant on possession alone is refused.
- Node and agent separation: 4.4.3.4.3 Conformance. A verifier MUST reject any agency:* scope on a delegate whose identity_type contains node. The test is set membership, so it is wire-checkable.
- Refused wire shapes: 4.1.3 (attestation:l{N}:* is rejected at admission, restated at 4.4.3.6) and 4.4.3.4.1 (a consumer MUST reject a v1 wrap on a self/family grant).
- Quorum counting: 4.4.3.4.2.1. M in quorum:M/N is an absolute count, separated from the T7 liveness margin.
- Sub-quorum: 4.4.3.1.1 gives three explicit paths, each with a named hard_case, and 'no implicit fallback'. That is a real refusal or deferral path, though staged (CIRISServer#536).
- Revocation read path: 4.4.3.5.4 and 4.4.3.5.5. Any one subject's admitted withdraws evicts the item. Reads are latest-wins with revoke as terminal, and the four outcomes include 'unspecified'.
- Attester-behaviour downweighting: 4.1.4. A withdraws:recants ratio above 5:1 SHOULD be downweighted.
- Signing bytes for the Self-at-login edges: 4.4.3.4.3.1 gives exact JCS member sets plus the omit and identifier-verbatim rules, and is implementable as written.

**Presenter: complete**
- 4.1.1 Delegation laundering. This is concrete and testable. The default traversal cap is 5 hops; a longer chain degrades to attestation:self_verify, which counts as a refusal of transitive trust. The shipped clamp is 16. Cycles are refused at emission. The rc8 readings state precisely what is not a cycle: a self-edge is the charter form, and a trust:accepts:v1 edge is outside the capability graph. Rows CLM-anti-pattern-delegation, CLM-self-edge-not-cycle and CLM-delegation-depth-default are established on persist admission.rs and topology.rs; the first two graduated at re-pin 15.
- 4.1.3 Already-rejected wire additions. Each row pairs a rejected form with its correct expression. Two are load-bearing for the reader. First, attestation:l{N}:* is refused at admission (restated in 4.4.3.6). Second, observation is the zero state and never a capability (infra:observe has no scope, deliberately).
- 4.4.3.8 Policy A and the pluggable trust root. This is the strongest section for this reader. It covers the self-root base, the charter as capability ceiling, a root validity minimum of [infra:serve, infra:attest], un-trust as one deletable edge, the fail-closed federation floor (item 5), the two conferral planes (ceremony for identity, delegation for reserved-prefix roles, item 6), and the recovery and independence audit (item 7). Item 8 is honest that genesis is tamper-evident, not self-verifying. CLM-trust-root is established on persist trust_root.rs (check_trust_charter_admission, trust_root_valid, ConferralPlane), with a residual staged:CIRISServer#536 pointer.
- 4.4.3.4.3 The enforced-admission rule for scoped delegation. This is the clearest refusal path in the book. A moderate, takedown, review, license or grant act is admitted only if the signer is the delegator, or sits on a live delegates_to chain that carries the matching scope and is depth-capped and revocable. Anything else is refused at admission, not merely unweighted. An issuance whose delegator does not itself hold the underlying authority is refused. A node-typed delegate presenting any agency:* scope is rejected (the infra:* / agency:* split). Rows: CLM-issuance-scope (established on persist check_licensure_delegator_is_authority), and CLM-delegation-attenuation, -cascade, -sub-delegation-requirement and -determinism (established on the vendored authority-tree model, invariants I1, I3, I7 and I9).
- 4.4.3.4.3.1 Signing member sets. The exact JCS member sets are given for partnership_grant/accept:v1, delegates_to and the transport_destination binding, with the omit rule and identifier-verbatim rules. This is implementable as written. CLM-canonicalization-signing is established on Verify self_at_login.rs.
- 4.4.3.4.2.1 quorum:M/N reads M as an absolute count. The section separates this from the T7 liveness margin, which is a clean pin. Note that it is not enough on its own, because the surrounding pseudo-code is broken (see honest_limits).
- 4.4.3.5.4 and 4.4.3.5.5 Multi-subject revocation and the consent read path. Any one subject's admitted withdraws evicts, there is no new primitive, and the read path is latest-wins with revoke as terminal. CLM-multi-subject is established on server resolve_withdraws_admission_rule_at.
- 4.4.3.2.1 and 4.4.3.2.2 The community tiers and the mandatory DEK cascade, with v2 hybrid wrap required, the holder-inspectability principle, and rotation on removal. CLM-community-three is established on server community_dek.rs.
- 4.1.4 withdraws arbitrage. It names the countermeasure (a per-attester withdraws:recants ratio, default 5:1) and the norm level is honest: SHOULD downweight. CLM-withdraws-arbitrage is established on server withdraws_arbitrage.rs#enforce.

**Presenter: partial (and what it leans on)**
- 4.4 as a whole leans on one symbol. CLM-composition-policies is established on a single CIRISServer compose_policy.rs#Composer::compose for thirteen named policies. Policies B (4.4.3.12), C (4.4.3.13), D (4.4.3.9), F (4.4.3.7), G (4.4.3.11) and H (4.4.3.3) have no row of their own, so no claim says which of them that symbol actually implements.
- 4.4.3.8 recommended default pin (ciris-canonical plus humanity-accord, bootstrapped from GET /v1/trust-root/bundle): CLM-default-pin-community is staged on CIRISServer#537.
- 4.4.3.1.1 sub-quorum fallback, 4.4.3.2.4.1 deterministic resolution, 4.4.3.6 Policy I and 4.4.3.10 Policy J are all staged on the single ticket CIRISServer#536 (5 rows). The sub-quorum section is a refusal path: its three paths are explicit, with no implicit fallback. It has no evidence yet.
- 4.4.3.2.8 the term (#163): every role, issuance or moderation edge carries delegation_valid_until of at most 365 days, and anything else is refused delegation_term_required. CLM-delegation-term is staged on CIRISServer#752 and CIRISEdge#852. Its only evidence today is the formal authority-tree I10 (595,456 states, 0 violations). It is modelled, not shipped.
- 4.4.2 aggregation defaults: CLM-nsproc-aggregation-policy is staged on CIRISPersist#803. The `signed` default is a plain mean, which the threat model now names as acknowledged risk R14 (8.6 table, rc8).
