- Four of the delegation rows (attenuation, cascade, sub-delegation-requirement, determinism) rest on the formal model alone, test:@formal/authority_tree I1, I3, I7 and I9, with no impl: pointer. The packet still says 'Formal witnesses: none'.
- 4.4.3.2.3 and 4.4.3.4.2 lean on CC 3.1.3.2 (acceptance_admits, #133) and CC 3.2 T6 and T7 for roster versioning and liveness. A consumer cannot implement admission from this book alone.
- 4.4.3.2.8 affiliation config: CLM-affiliation-config-record (persist check_community_record) and CLM-affiliations (conformance tests 262 and 263) are established for the record's shape. Most fields (lawful_access transparency_mode, compartments with per-member exclusion, archive_custody, member_attributes gating) have no row naming what enforces them.

**Presenter: missing**
- A refusal path for the transitive policies. Policy B (4.4.3.12) is two sentences and Policy C (4.4.3.13) is one paragraph. Neither gives a hop decay function, damping, seed set, convergence bound or threshold, and neither says how 4.1.1's depth cap and 0.5 × root_trust cap compose with it. This reader would kill these two as written.
- Negative evidence in the ladder. Policy I's ladder_verdict (4.4.3.6) takes 'any positive attestation' per rung. It never consults negatives, withdraws or expiry, which contradicts 4.4.2, where boolean-via-score dimensions aggregate by Min (any negative trumps). There is no stated refusal when a rung carries a conflicting negative.
- A threshold or verdict contract for Policy A. 4.4.3.8 says 'consumer threshold determines verdict' and gives no default, no abstain or unknown outcome, and no behaviour on an empty trusted-attester set.
- A guard on Policy D. The tie-break weights affected_population_estimate inversely (smaller counts as more vulnerable). That value is self-declared in context, so an attester can claim a tiny cohort to win ties, and no structural safeguard is named, unlike the one 4.4.1 names for testimonial_witness.
- Bounded influence for the signed mean (R14). The book now admits the gap. It does not say what a consumer SHOULD do meanwhile (for example a per-attester cap or trimmed mean as a local override), and 4.4.2's 'override via consumer policy' is the only exit.
- The window and threshold for 4.1.4. The 'rolling window' has no length, and the 5:1 trigger has no minimum sample size, so a new attester's 1:0 ratio is undefined.
- A single consumer-side table of refusals (refuse at admission / degrade to self_verify / downweight / emit hard_case / defer). The refusal paths exist but are spread across 4.1.1, 4.1.3, 4.4.3.1.1, 4.4.3.4.3, 4.4.3.4.1 (reject v1 wrap), 4.4.3.6 (reject l{N}) and 4.4.3.8 item 5. This reader's question is answered piecemeal.
- How the resolvers (resolve_community, resolve_family, resolve_self) interact with the 4.1.1 depth cap and with lapsed terms (#163) when a role edge expires between two reads.
- Which of 4.1.1's MUSTs bind the consumer and which bind the substrate. The depth cap is 'consumer policy MUST', cycle detection is 'substrate MUST', and the aggregate-weight cap is 'consumer policy SHOULD'. A consumer that also runs a substrate needs this split stated once.

**Presenter: jargon**
- Stale section numbers break navigation for an implementer: §8.1.13.1, §8.1.13.2, §8.1.12.3, §8.1.12.3.1, §8.1.12.4, §8.1.12.7, §8.1.11.4, §5.6.8.8.1, §5.6.8.10, §0.8.2 and §4.2.2.3 in the 4.4.3.2-4.4.3.4 pseudo-code. - 'Cartesian-individualist defaults', 'Ubuntu-primary discipline' (4.1, 4.1.2) and 'Frickerian' (4.4.1) are philosophical framing. They exceed an implementer's budget without changing what the consumer computes. 4.4.1 does give concrete rules, which saves it.
- NodeCore P4 votes, P8 quorum, LensCore and 'CIRIS L3C' (4.1.3, 4.4.3.7, 4.4.3.10) are named components and processes that are never defined here.

- G3 (4.4.3.1 'Closes G3'), AV-17 (4.4.3.4.3), R1/Q1 merge (4.4.3.2.4.1) and T1/T3/T4a/T6/T7 (CC 3.2 labels) are internal register labels that an outside implementer cannot resolve from this book.
- HITL/HOTL/HOOTL (4.1.5) are not expanded.
- The 4.4.3.2.8 regulatory vocabulary (42 CFR 2, ITAR/EAR, NAGPRA, FISA/NSL, USA-FREEDOM, BAA, OIOS, P&I Convention) is far outside a graph reviewer's budget, and it is the bulk of the section.

**Presenter: rc8 changes**
- 4.1.1: a new row says what is not a cycle. A self-edge is the charter form, a trust:accepts:v1 acceptance edge is outside the capability graph in either arrival order, and a capability delegates_to closing the same pair is still refused (readings 2026-10-09, CIRISPersist#1031). The 16-hop ceiling is stated as a clamp that does not raise the default of 5. CLM-anti-pattern-delegation and CLM-self-edge-not-cycle graduate at re-pin 15 (persist c03aae6).
- 4.4.3.2.8 hierarchy: authority decays (#163). Every role, issuance or moderation edge carries a term with a constitutional ceiling of 365 days; role_term is a charter parameter; a perpetual edge is refused delegation_term_required; a lapsed moderate edge is a CC 4.5.4 lapse. CLM-delegation-term is staged on Server#752 and Edge#852. The vendored authority-tree model gains invariant I10.
- The authority tree is vendored to formal/authority_tree (2026-10-12) with nine invariants plus I10. Four register rows were minted on it (attenuation, cascade, sub-delegation, determinism) and four were pointed at it.
- 4.4.3.4.3 enforced-admission is extended to license and grant (#161-#164). Authority is judged at issuance, not at receipt; there is no public default; anyone may be an authority under their own key; a subject cannot withdraw a licensure row about itself, and the contest is reconsideration.
- 4.4.3.4.3 step 1: the partnered pair is data management, not a relationship the agent may refuse (#168, 2026-10-11). For a user who is not the steward, the decision is the steward's, reached through Wisdom-Based Deferral. CLM-consent-stream-is-data-choice is staged on CIRISAgent#1251.
- 4.4.1: CLM-no-worse-for-what-you-are is added (normative, #168 council). It gathers the Frickerian weighting, the justice:* tie-break and detection:distributive:* into one rule.
- 4.4.2 is not edited, but the rc8 threat-model pass (CC 1.13.3.3, ERC-8004 adversary cases) names its `signed` plain-mean default as acknowledged risk R14 in the CC 8.6 table, as an open rc9 question.
- 4.4.3.2.8: the organisation is an affiliation (#161). This does not change P05's text directly but changes what the hierarchy carries (roles become delegates_to edges, partners become licensure:{community_key_id} rows).

**Presenter: honest limits**
- 4.4 / CLM-composition-policies: I would not present thirteen policies as 'established' on one Composer::compose symbol. Say instead that Policy A, the trust root and the scoped-delegation admission are evidenced. B, C, D, G and H are named patterns with no row, and B and C are under-specified.
- 4.4.2: soften 'opinionated defaults ... the CCC minimum' wherever it reads as robust. The signed default is a plain mean inside the trust set with no bounded influence (R14). Its safety rests on trust-set admission alone.
- 4.4.3.2.5 worked example: retract or fix it before presenting. Step 4 says 'community is unencrypted; No key_grant cascade', which contradicts 4.4.3.2.1's mandatory community DEK. Step 2 proposes membership via `supersedes` plus member signatures, which contradicts the rc7 erratum in 4.4.3.2.3 (a new roster version chained by prev_head_digest, never a single-signer supersedes) and skips the joiner's own acceptance.
- 4.4.3.2.4 resolve_community, 4.4.3.4.4 resolve_family and 4.4.3.4.6 resolve_self still walk the 'supersedes chain → latest non-superseded'. That is the model the rc7 erratum retired for rosters. A graph reviewer will find these resolvers inconsistent with the admission text two paragraphs earlier.
- 4.4.3.4.2 admit_family_change: the match arms `return` the protocol verdict, so the acceptance loop is unreachable on the paths shown, and `admit` is never initialised. The pseudo-code as written does not enforce 'nobody joins without their own consent'. Present it as illustrative until it is fixed.
- 4.4.3.2.6: 'open → consensus_protocol = open:self_admit' is not one of the six canonical kinds. It also conflicts with 4.4.3.2.3's `public` subkind (a standing founder proposal plus the joiner's acceptance, still under consensus_protocol). Soften it to the public-subkind path.
- 4.4.2 lists `attestation:l*` under boolean-via-score Min, while 4.1.3 and 4.4.3.6 say attestation:l{N}:* is refused at admission. One of the two is stale.
- 4.4.3.1 'Closes G3' and 'Recursion safety ... always true at federation genesis' are design assertions resting on a staged row (CLM-sub-quorum, Server#536). Say 'is designed to close'.
- 4.4.3.3 Policy H is titled '(LIVE)' but has no register row. Drop the label or cite evidence.
- 4.4.3.7 Policy F Layer 1 (a registry-steward-triple key, the registry.ciris-services-1.ai install endpoint) and 4.4.3.10 Layer 3 ('the only path is the operator-set trust list') read like a fixed authority roster. This sits uneasily with 4.4.3.8's 'shipped default root, never the root' and with the anyone-may-assert rule. Frame both as the default consumer pin, replaceable like any other root.
- 4.4.3.13 'less common in practice' and 4.4.3.11 '~20 substrate stories' are informal frequency claims with no source. Cut them or cite a source.
- 4.4.3.5.4: 'a multi-party contract, any one signatory revokes → evicted' is correct for the wire, but a careful presenter says plainly that this removes the artifact from propagation, not from any holder who already decrypted it (Option A, 4.4.3.4.5).
- The four authority-tree rows (CLM-delegation-attenuation, -cascade, -sub-delegation-requirement, -determinism) and CLM-delegation-term are 'established' or evidenced by a model-checked formal model. Say 'model-checked', not 'shipped'.

## P06 — Governance discipline (4.5: moderation, redress, child safety, takedown, purge)

**Reader:** a trust-and-safety lead with a lawyer beside them  
**Verdict:** needs work  
**One change:** Add one normative table to 4.5 with these columns: remedy | who may exercise it (named role, and whether that role is the poster, the subject, a moderator, a steward, the operator or the trust root) | against what (bytes, row, licence, purge, or another moderation act) | how it is contested | who rules on the contest | deadline. Then make every line of the 4.5.5 target-to-duty-holder list agree with the table. In particular, add the content's poster and signed subject as admissible filers of reconsideration (and of a defined counter-notice with a restore deadline) against a takedown or moderation act. Delete 'or an immediate-removal basis from any claimant'. Bring the 2.4.1.1 purge-reach limits and a named purge-contest reviewer into the table.

**Promise (presenter):** For a trust-and-safety lead and their counsel, this book says who may remove what, who may contest it, and that every remedy is a signed act by a named person rather than a platform's say-so. Moderation is a duty a person holds (moderate, takedown, review, slash), either personally or handed down through a revocable, narrowing chain that starts with an accountable human (4.5.5). No group runs without a named moderator (4.5.4). Removal for the urgent legal bases (terrorist content, CSAM, court orders) skips the vote and the counter-notice but still needs a duty-holder (4.5.3 step 1). The person a row is about can always appeal by reconsideration and can see every licence about them (4.5.5 Subject standing). A report nobody defends is removed by default, and keeping or viewing reported harm is always on the record under a name (4.5.13). CSAM scanning is opt-in for each group, split across three parties, and switching it off leaves a trace (4.5.7). A trust-root purge is a separate, cosigned, whole-or-nothing remedy that never reaches a key or the accountability record (4.5.3 step 2, #165). The book promises structure, not outcomes: it does not promise that a community will choose well, only that it cannot choose in silence (4.5.13, "structure, not behavior").

**Reviewer: kills**
- 4.5.3 step 2: "(or an immediate-removal basis from any claimant)". This remedy names a party, 'any claimant', that step 1 of the same section forbids: 'never the duty-holder gate ... refused like any other unauthorised action'. A remedy cannot both belong to anyone and be refused to anyone without the duty. Delete the parenthesis.
- 4.5.3 step 5: "route through the standard CC 4.5.1 amendment path on counter-notice via `reconsideration:new_evidence`". The party a counter-notice exists for is the poster whose content was removed. That person cannot run a constitutional amendment, and under 4.5.5 a reconsideration of a prior action is admitted only from review-duty holders. As written, the poster cannot exercise the counter-notice remedy. Replace it with a defined counter-notice row the poster may file, a named reviewer, and a restore-by deadline.
- 4.5.5 Subject standing: "The **subject of the target attestation** holds standing to file `reconsideration:{grounds}`". The same section's resolution list then says "`reconsideration:{grounds}` against a prior action → `is_named_moderator(·, C, review)`". So the admission rule admits only review-holders for a reconsideration of a takedown or moderation act. Unless the subject is added to that line, the subject's appeal of a takedown cannot pass the gate that admits it. The standing holds only for licences, where the line was patched (#164).
- 4.5.13 Protective default: "Content survives **only** by an affirmative, on-record **keep-decision**: either **(A)** a steward's unilateral *approve-to-keep* signature, **or** **(B)** a live-majority *vote-to-keep*". The text defines only one required role, the moderator, and names the moderator as 'the fast-path decision-maker'. Yet the moderator alone cannot keep. In a 'moderator only' community the stated unilateral power ('either resolves an action by a single signature') cannot be used for a keep. Add the moderator to (A), or say outright that a keep needs a steward or a vote.
- 4.5.3 step 3: "operator MUST file the NCMEC CyberTipline report" and "`CourtOrder` — operator follows the court's stated timeline". These place obligations on a party that, under step 1 and the 4.5.7 table, cannot file the notice that removes the content ('Operator ... Cannot: turn it on ... act without the authority's opt-in'). The operator cannot carry out a court order the book puts on it. Either give the operator a stated node-local remedy (stop serving, quarantine) or move the obligation to the duty-holder.
- 4.5.3 step 4: "Reviewers MAY file a `reconsideration:procedural_error`". 'Reviewers' is not a defined party. If it means review-duty holders, the affected user has no standing here. If it means anyone, it contradicts 4.5.5. As it stands, nobody identifiable can exercise this remedy.

**Reviewer: unanswered**
- Whether the person who posted or uploaded removed content can appeal. 4.5.5 Subject standing names 'the subject of the target attestation', which means the person the content is about, not its author. The target-to-duty-holder list then admits 'reconsideration:{grounds} against a prior action' only from review-duty holders. A poster who is not the subject has no stated route, and even the subject's route through the admission rule is unclear.
- A counter-notice the poster can actually use, with a restoration deadline. 4.5.3 step 5 sends Dmca512, DsaArticle16, CommunityStandards and OsaIllegalContent counter-notices through 'the standard CC 4.5.1 amendment path'. No restoration timeline is given, no reviewer is named, and no restore action is defined.
- Who may contest a trust-root purge, and who rules on that contest. 4.5.3 calls the purge 'cosigned by the trust root' and says it 'reaches any plane'. Nothing in 4.5 gives the affected party a contest route or names a reviewer other than the trust root itself. The packet's own row text in 3.3.2 says a reconsideration may target the notice, but not who rules on it.
- Limits on what a purge can reach. The limits are that a purge never reaches keys or the accountability record (revocation:*, moderation:*, slashing:*). They appear only in 2.4.1.1 (part_2:287). 4.5.3 says the opposite in passing ('reaches any plane').
- What the operator can do when it is served with a court order or bound by a 2258A reporting duty but holds no takedown duty over the community. Step 3 places 'MUST file' and 'follows the court's stated timeline' on 'the operator'. Step 1 refuses any notice from a key that does not hold the duty. The book never says whether an operator may stop serving bytes on its own node.
- A remedy against a harmful row (for example a harassing or defamatory score) as opposed to bytes. A duty-holder's takedown 'reaches the bytes plane only'. The only remedies left are reconsideration or a trust-root purge.
- Whether a moderator alone may keep reported content. 4.5.13 resolution 1 lets a moderator act unilaterally, but both the protective default and the outcome table list only a steward approval or a majority vote as ways to keep. A community whose founder chose 'moderator only' therefore has no single-signature way to keep.
- What a poster can do after a false or mass report against a community whose moderator is absent. An undefended report is removed after 48 h, and no penalty or restoration path for the reporter's abuse is given.
- What a user can do after a false perceptual-hash match. That basis has no counter-notice, the text says 'no human eyes on it, ever', and a CyberTipline report is mandatory. The only route is a procedural_error reconsideration filed by unnamed 'reviewers'.
- Who 'live members' are for the community vote, and how membership is proved. 4.5.13 points to 4.2.6, which is in another book.
- Whether an auto-promoted moderator may decline. 4.5.4 rule 2 grants the duty 'automatically'. Rule 3 mentions 'none consenting' but gives no way to consent or refuse.
- How the duty to report harm to a child (3.4.13 Q4, CLM-child-harm-report-duty) applies. A child-safety reader would expect it beside the 4.5.13 duty-view paragraph, and it is not mentioned.
- A statement of reasons to the affected user, or internal complaint handling (DSA Articles 17 and 20). 4.5.2.2 maps consent and erasure articles only.
- Which law sets the hash-retention window ('federal-legal retention window', 4.5.3 step 3), and how long it is.

**Reviewer: confusions**
- 4.5.5: the table header says 'the three duties' but the table has four rows; slash is the fourth. 'slashing:{outcome}' and 'quarantine:{state}' are never defined in this book, so I cannot tell what a slash does or to whom.
- 4.5.13 outcome ladder: 'live-majority flagged-but-retained' is a third outcome the vote mechanism never defines. The vote is described as keep or remove.
- 4.5.3 step 1: 'the per-basis dispatch below is the duty-holding operator's'. This merges two parties that 4.5.7 keeps apart (operator and authority).
- 4.5.3 and 4.5.5: 'escalate to the HUMANITY_ACCORD triple per CC 4.2.1 invocation procedures'. This points at a remedy in another book without saying who may invoke it or what it does to a takedown.
- 4.5.3 step 2: an immediate-removal basis 'destroys on receipt', but the hard-delete rule is written for steward_purge with a 72 h dwell. I could not tell which clock governs a CSAM removal on a peer's node, or whether the bytes go at once or after W.
- 4.5.4 merit auto-promotion 'emits exactly this appointment shape ... the community's authority auto-grants'. Whose key signs the grant when the authority set is the absent party is not stated.
- 4.5.13: 'steward' carries two meanings. The term pin admits this, but uses of the word before the pin had already confused me (for example 'steward-bound root' in 4.5.5 against 'any steward ... resolves the action').
- Jargon outside my budget sits in the middle of the remedy text: bytes plane, tombstone, holding claim, re-acknowledge none, descent, forever-memory floor, T4, 1+4, delegates_to, withdraws, scores, attesting_key_id, band-blind, fire-floor-of-1, live set L, Enoch-Arden return, proof-of-presence over the proposal nonce.
- 4.5.2, 4.5.8, 4.5.9, 4.5.10, 4.5.11 and 4.5.12 are off my question (identity_type sets, H3 cells, hash-DB landscape, the Magnifica Humanitas bootstrap, family DEKs) and make up roughly half the packet.
- 4.5.3 'TVEC mandates a 1-hour removal obligation'. TVEC is a content category, not a law. Counsel needs the source regulation named.

**Reviewer: presenter kinder than the text**
- Purge reach. The presenter says a purge 'never reaches a key or the accountability record (4.5.3 step 2, #165)'. 4.5.3 step 2 says a steward_purge notice 'reaches any plane'. That limit is stated only at 2.4.1.1 (part_2_the_grammar.md:287), in another book. As far as this book's text goes, the opposite is true.
- Subject standing. The presenter says 'The person a row is about can always appeal by reconsideration'. The text gives that standing, but the admission list in the same section admits reconsideration of a prior action only from review-holders. 'Always' is not supported, and the presenter does not raise the conflict, even though it is the core of my question.
- Purge contest. The presenter lists steward purge authorisation as established with 'contest by reconsideration'. 4.5 has no purge contest route at all, and the presenter does not flag that the trust root would be judging its own act.
- Duty-holder gate. The presenter lists the 'never waived' duty-holder gate under complete. The text still contains the 'from any claimant' phrase in step 2. The presenter does note this as a missing item, but the complete row reads cleaner than the text is.
- Duty-view. The presenter presents 'keeping or viewing reported harm is always on the record under a name' as delivered. The no-human-eyes promise is normative-only (CLM-duty-view), and 'every viewing is ... by a named identity' covers only content that was reported and kept behind the gate.
- Moderator power. The presenter says 'No group runs without a named moderator', which implies that the moderator governs. The presenter does not mention that the moderator alone cannot keep reported content under the protective default.
- CSAM. The presenter says 'switching it off leaves a trace', which is true. The presenter does not mention that a false match leaves the user with no counter-notice and no named reviewer, while the text guarantees no human looks at it.

**Reviewer: answered**
- Who may remove content by takedown, and against what. 4.5.3 step 1 and 4.5.5 cover this. The person filing must hold the takedown duty over the content's community. They can hold it in their own right ((a): as the content's signed subject or as a named moderator) or through a delegation chain ((b): at most 5 links, every link carrying the takedown scope, with an accountable human at the root). The urgent legal bases skip the vote and the counter-notice but still require that duty. The target is the content's bytes, identified by their hash. A takedown filed by someone without the duty is refused.
- Who may act for a group in general. 4.5.5 'Authoritative action' and the table of duties answer this. moderate issues moderation events, takedown issues takedown notices, review issues reconsideration rulings, and slash issues slashing verdicts. Any of these may be delegated, a delegation may only narrow, and it can be revoked at any link (4.5.5 deputization). A revocation stops future acts but does not undo acts already signed (#162).
- Who may label content without authority. 4.5.5 'Open labeling': anyone may file a score or report, and each reader decides whether to filter on it. That labels the content but does not take it down.
- Who may open a moderation question. 4.5.13: anyone, through a report. The question then has a 48 h window. In that window a moderator or steward may decide it alone with one signature. If neither acts, the community's live members decide by majority vote.
- What happens to a report nobody defends. 4.5.13 'Protective default': it is removed. Content survives only if (A) a steward signs to keep it or (B) a live-majority votes to keep it, and either way the keepers are named on the record.
- Who can view reported content that was kept behind a warning. 4.5.13 'Infohazard consent gate': each viewer signs a consent record before seeing it.
- Who may switch on CSAM or other watchlist scanning, and for which groups. 4.5.7: only a group's moderate/takedown holder, one group at a time. A global scan is non-conformant. Turning it on and every match are logged. Turning off a CSAM list cannot be done silently. The operator supplies the hash list but cannot enable it, and the node runs the match but cannot choose to.
- What a group with no moderator can do. 4.5.4 rules 1 to 3 and the 4.5.13 'No-moderator recovery': automatic promotion by track record; failing that, a 48 h recovery with a 24 h open candidacy; failing that, the group stops operating at moderated capability.
- Who may appeal a licence about them, and to whom. 4.5.5 'Subject standing' and the last line of the target-to-duty-holder list: the person the licence names may seek reconsideration from the licensing authority's set, or from anyone holding a review chain from it. They cannot withdraw the licence themselves, and they can see every licence about them.
- Who reviews a mistaken fast-path removal. 4.5.3 step 4 says it is logged and that 'reviewers' may file reconsideration:procedural_error. Who 'reviewers' are is unclear (see confusions).

**Presenter: complete**
- 4.5.5 enforced-admission rule: (a) as-self or (b) delegated chain, every edge in scope, depth at most 5, steward-bound root, no payload principal field. Subject authority is resolved from the content's signed establishing attestation, never the action payload, and fails closed when that attestation is not held. CLM-takedown-moderation established (CIRISServer moderation.rs#admit_moderation_action); CLM-slash-duty-scope and CLM-scope-attenuation established in persist. Who-against-whom is stated per action in the target-to-duty-holder resolution list.
- 4.5.5 deputization and attenuation: a sub-delegation may narrow but never widen, can be revoked at any link, and judges authority at the act's signed instant (#162). Sub-scope matching is directional and an unknown caveat fails closed.
- 4.5.4 existence gate (at admission and again at every federation apply step) plus deterministic merit auto-promotion and fail-secure. CLM-registry-named established (test_271_moderator_existence_gate plus server named.rs); CLM-promotion-cohort-standing established.
- 4.5.3 one tombstone row for takedown and purge, plus purge mechanics: whole-or-nothing (purge_retraction_inadmissible), 72 h dwell timed by the node clock (purge_not_settled), tombstoned records refused at every door (purge_tombstoned), withheld blobs, projections removed. The six purge-hardening rows plus CLM-one-tombstone-row and CLM-purge-whole-or-not graduated at re-pins 15 and 16 (persist c03aae6, ca6bdf60).
- 4.5.7 watchlist: opt-in per group, a global scan config is non-conformant, a three-party separation-of-powers table, every enable and every match logged as hard_case, and disabling a CSAM list cannot be silent. CLM-registry-watchlist established (server watchlist.rs#enable_watchlist).
- 4.5.3 per-basis dispatch for the immediate-removal bases (TvecTerrorist, GifctCip, NcmecCsam/PerceptualHashCsam, CourtOrder). CLM-takedown established (server takedown_handler.rs#process_takedown_admission).
- 4.5.12.2 envelope family_id: CLM-envelope-multi established.

**Presenter: partial (and what it leans on)**

**Presenter: missing**

The book never says whether the author or uploader of content that was taken down has standing to contest it. There is no restoration timeline (for example the DMCA 512(g) 10-14 business-day window) and no named reviewer.

Under 4.5.3 step 1 the operator cannot file, yet 4.5.3 step 3 puts the reporting and coordination obligations on 'the operator'. The book does not say whether the operator may stop serving on its own device.
- A remedy against a harmful row as opposed to bytes. A duty-holder's takedown 'reaches the bytes plane only' (4.5.3 step 2). For harassment or a defamatory attestation that is itself a scores row, the only paths shown are reconsideration or a trust-root purge.
- Who may keep reported content. 4.5.13 resolution 1 lets a moderator or steward act, but the protective default lists only (A) steward approve-to-keep and (B) live-majority vote-to-keep. The book is silent on whether a moderator alone may keep.
- Abuse of reports. Anyone may propose (4.5.13), and an undefended report is removed after 48 h. Nothing addresses mass false reporting against an absent-moderator community, or what the author can do afterwards.

- A single table of remedy, who may exercise it, against what, and how to contest it. The answer to the reader's question is spread across 4.5.3, 4.5.5, 4.5.7 and 4.5.13, and parts of it sit in 3.3.2, 3.1.9.2 and 3.4.13.
- The legal retention window for NCMEC hash and metadata (4.5.3 step 3, 'federal-legal retention window') is not named.

**Presenter: rc8 changes**

The fast path waives quorum and counter-notice, never the duty-holder gate. The CC comment on #1007 that said 'from any claimant' was corrected, but step 2 still carries that phrase.
- 4.5.3 step 2 (#165, 2026-10-09): one replicating tombstone row for duty-holder takedown and trust-root steward_purge, which differ only in admission check and reach. steward_purge hard-deletes after W = 72 h. A purge is whole or not at all. A purge never reaches a key or the accountability record (moderation:*, slashing:*, revocation:*, key:revocation). The notice became a registered family takedown_notice:{basis}:v1 (staged on CIRISPersist#803).

- 4.5.5 Subject standing (#164): a subject cannot withdraw a licensure:* or revocation:* row about itself, contests by reconsideration reviewed by the authority set plus review-scoped chains, and can see every licence about them. Target-to-duty-holder list gains the licensure reconsideration line.
- 4.5.5 deputization (#162): authority judged at the act's signed instant, so a withdrawal stops future issuance but does not reclassify licences already issued. Related #163: moderation-scope delegates_to must carry a term (365-day ceiling), and a lapsed moderate edge is a 4.5.4 lapse (this lives in other sections, and 4.5.4 text does not yet mention the term).
- 4.5.13 duty-view paragraph (#168, 2026-10-11): examining reported material to act on it is an act of the duty recorded as moderation:*, not a keep or a view. The paragraph sets out minimise, restrict, record, report, and puts the organisation on the bond with the duty-holder as its agent. CLM-duty-view normative-only.
- #167 admission freshness window W = 72 h (asserted_at_stale) applies to chain-judged rows, including moderation actions (stated elsewhere, not in 4.5).

Also 4.5.5 'cf. Bluesky labelers + Ozone' and 'SOTA-aligned'. Remove the comparisons and state the invariant alone.
- 4.5.13 'Passive survival is impossible' and 'surviving harmful content is always attributable' hold only for content that has been reported, within a community that is federating. Soften to 'reported content'.
- 4.5.13 duty-view 'no human eyes on it, ever' and 'what good-faith protection in every jurisdiction turns on' are a normative-only promise and a legal generalisation. Soften to 'designed so that...' and 'commonly turns on'. Counsel should not read 'provider protection the law offers' as an opinion that the protection applies.
- 4.5.5 'The CC 4.5.5 gate has no remaining self-declaration spoof' and 'fully substrate-enforceable' are absolute. They are evidenced by one server function, and the notice row they act on is staged (CIRISPersist#803). Soften to 'no known'.
- 4.5.3 step 2: a 'reachable holder MUST destroy the bytes on receipt' is a duty no one can verify, because the text itself says no acknowledgement plane exists. State it as a duty, not an outcome.
- 4.5.3: 'TVEC mandates a 1-hour removal obligation'. TVEC is a content category; the one-hour rule comes from a specific regulation (the EU terrorist-content online regulation). Name the source or soften.
- 4.5.2.2 GDPR Art 17/20 rows ('composes trivially', 'observability primitive for Article 17 compliance') sit beside 4.5.9.3 'permanent' historical claims and 4.5.12.1 departed members keeping keys. Present the table as informational wire mapping, not as a compliance claim.
- 4.5.5 and 4.5.3 'takedown-isn't-a-coup ... a state actor ... fails the enforced-admission gate' holds only while the state actor holds no duty-holder or trust-root key. Say that.
- 4.5.4 'every peer auto-promotes the same member' assumes the same moderation_track_record view on every peer and a signer for the appointment. Neither is shown.
- 4.5.5 'no media/chat feature ships until this ... is solved and working' is a release-process promise that is not evidenced in this book.

## P07 — The Wire, part two, and the Threat Model (Part 5 + 1.13.3 adversary model)

**Reader:** a cryptographer and red-team lead  
**Verdict:** needs work  
**One change:** Rewrite 1.13.3.3 as a conditioned table. Give each row the configuration it holds under (at-rest encryption on or off, witnessed mode on or off with quorum, accountable vs best-effort stream, cover discipline present or absent) and its shipped default today. Add rows for a compromised or wrong trust anchor (build channel, pin, accord holder), a malicious forwarder or relay or L1 parent, and the loss of one half of each hybrid. Then move every traffic-level claim in 5.4.3 and 5.4.5 under the 1.13.3.1 non-goal, or bound it to a cover discipline that is actually in the book. Today a red-teamer has to reconstruct the true threat model from the staging tokens and 'MAY default off' clauses scattered through Part 5.

**Promise (presenter):** This book tells a cryptographer what CEG protects, with what, and against whom, and it says up front where the protection stops. In this spec "privacy" means exactly two things: content-holding confidentiality and cohort-scoped visibility (1.13.3). It does not mean metadata privacy, communication-graph privacy, traffic-analysis resistance or unobservability against a global passive adversary, and it says that in 1.13.3.1. Six things carry the trust. (1) Hybrid primitives with both halves verified: AES-256-GCM content, an X25519+ML-KEM-768 DEK wrap under one wire identifier, and Ed25519+ML-DSA-65 bound signatures checked at every federation admission gate with no classical-only window (5.1, 5.3.2.4.3.1). (2) MLS TreeKEM epoch keying, with one per-stream DEK derived from the exporter, coalesced removal rotation that bounds a removed member's exposure to at most T = 2 s, and forward-only secrecy by default (5.1). (3) Structural invisibility: holding claims for self, family and community content never leave the cohort, and group destinations are never announced, directed or broadcast (5.2, 5.4.6). (4) Per-stream RFC 6962 logs with an optional witness cosign checked by a consistency proof, which makes a producer or founder who shows different histories detectable (5.3.1, 5.3.1.1, 5.3.3.3). (5) A trust root that is tamper-evident but not self-authenticating, so it is only as good as an out-of-band pinned bundle_fingerprint (5.3.4). (6) An adversary table that gives, for each of nine classes, what is addressed and what is not (1.13.3.3).

**Reviewer: kills**
- 1.13.3.3, row 'Compromised substrate node': kill 'cannot decrypt self/family content (no DEK)'. 5.2 says at-rest encryption 'MAY default off as a v1 migration posture', and its stated purpose is defence 'against local-disk forensics, the host operator, or the cloud-substrate operator'. With it off, the substrate node holding a cohort's bytes holds plaintext. The adversary model does not cover the configuration the text permits by default. The same fix applies to 1.13.3.2 'End-to-end content confidentiality is additionally provided by the per-epoch DEK'. Replace with 'cannot discover or fetch it (5.2 layer 1); cannot decrypt it where at-rest encryption is on (5.2 layer 2)'.
- 5.4.3: kill 'so an on-path observer cannot distinguish a large publication from idle substrate maintenance'. This is a traffic-analysis claim, which 1.13.3.1 disclaims at base CEG/RET, and it rests on a Poisson cover discipline that is not in the book. It is also contradicted by the wire format the same section pins: symbol_envelope carries record_id and symbol_index in cleartext, so an on-path observer can group envelopes by record and count symbols. Nothing specifies what record_id and index sequence cover envelopes carry, or how cover reproduces that structure. 5.4.6 (#132) itself concedes cover framing 'is not a claim against that inference'.
- 5.4.2: kill 'a holder lacking both K_symbol and record_id' and 'cover and real envelopes are observationally identical'. record_id is cleartext in every envelope, so no holder ever lacks it, and confidentiality rests on K_symbol alone. Observational identity of real and cover traffic is asserted without specifying how cover is generated. Keep the per-envelope ciphertext-indistinguishability claim. Drop the traffic-level identity claim, or move it under the 1.13.3.1 non-goal.
- 5.4.5: kill 'an observer of the chain learns neither the true non-federation activity rate nor which leaves are real'. Committing to 'count mod target_rate' with back-pressure leaks: under sustained above-target load the backlog and latency grow observably. 'count mod target_rate' is not a defined commitment scheme. The claim 'indistinguishable ... under the IND of HMAC-SHA3' should be PRF security. The cover leaf is keyed with 'witness_signing_key', which reuses a signing key as a MAC key with no key-separation argument. Rate hiding is again a traffic-analysis claim that 1.13.3.1 disclaims.
- 1.13.3.3, row 'Equivocating founder': kill the unqualified '**mitigated** — witnessed lineage heads'. The shipped roots run witness_quorum: 0 with an empty directory (5.3.4 'Shipped charter defaults'), and CLM-lineage-head-cosign is staged on CIRISServer#693. Today the only mitigation is the out-of-band pin at attach. The row must say 'when witnessed mode is on; off at the 0.5.219 mint'.
- 1.13.3.3, row 'Equivocating producer': kill the unqualified '**mitigated**' with the 'NOT addressed' column empty. Per 5.3.3.3 the witness cosign is OPTIONAL, best-effort streams are 'producer-signed root only', and the accountable tier is 'impl-pending the streaming substrate AND STH consistency-proof enforcement'. A producer on a best-effort stream can show different chunk-K to different viewers who never compare roots.

**Reviewer: unanswered**
- What happens when the trust anchor is wrong. Everything roots in a bundle_fingerprint 'baked into conformant builds' (5.3.4). The book has no adversary row for a compromised build or distribution channel, a wrong pin, or a compromised accord holder. Recovery (T4 revocation) lives in CC 3.2, which is outside this book. With witness_quorum: 0 shipped, the attach-time pin is the only defence against a founder fork.
- What happens when one half of a hybrid falls. The AND-verify rule settles this for signatures. For the KEM, the X25519+ML-KEM-768 combiner (the HKDF-SHA256 construction, and X-Wing in 5.4.4) is never stated as a combiner with an IND-CCA argument when one component is broken. The book has no primitives-and-assumptions table at all (IND-CCA2, SUF-CMA of the bound signature, PRF security of HMAC-SHA3, SHA-256 collision resistance).
- What happens when at-rest encryption is off. 5.2 says it 'MAY default off as a v1 migration posture'. The adversary table never conditions on this (see kills).
- What happens when the witness set is empty. With witness_quorum: 0, 'two heads that each reach the charter's witness_quorum' is vacuously true of any two heads, so the equivocation definition degenerates. The text does not say how 5.3.1 behaves at quorum 0.
- The cover discipline that the traffic-hiding claims in 5.4.3 and 5.4.5 rest on: the 'companion Poisson-cover emission discipline' and the 'companion per-community archive-policy section' appear nowhere in this book or in part_5 (grep finds only the one mention). The ~10–60 KB/s/peer budget is not derived.
- Composed-protocol security. formal/mesh_net checks abstract invariants (Inv_Invisible, Inv_KnownHold and others), not cryptographic games. The 'by reduction to XChaCha20-Poly1305 IND-CCA + HKDF-SHA3' in 5.4.2 and 5.4.3 is neither written nor cited. The book has no adversarial-run results.
- Side channels and implementation attacks: timing of the ML-KEM and ML-DSA code, RNG failure (the XChaCha20 nonce source in 5.4.2 is never stated), occurrence key-storage compromise, and fault attacks on deterministic ML-DSA. Only the render tier (5.3.2.6) treats implementation risk.
- Downgrade surfaces: the accept-only alias flag (5.1), CEG-Version MINOR mismatch being 'compatible (clients MAY warn)' (5.3.6), and CIR1/CIR2 negotiation (E1) are not analysed as downgrade paths in 1.13.3.
- Adversary rows for a malicious forwarder or relay (distinct from 'compromised substrate node', even though #132 gives forwarders a sanctioned role), for a misbehaving privacy-witness operator beyond 'slashing', and for the phone-class L1-relay parent, whose 'relay-blinded path' (5.4.6) is unspecified, so what it learns about group lookups is unbounded.
- The E1 transit KEX binding (#171) is a single sentence here. Its labels, KDF layout and KATs are 'Verify's' and live at 3.3.6.2, so the hop KEX is not byte-pinned in this book.

**Reviewer: confusions**
- 5.3.1: the STH cosign canonical preimage ('ciris.sth_cosign.v1', tree_size, root_hash, signed_at) has no log_id, and the request body has no log field. 5.3.3.3 and 5.3.3.7 reuse 'the CC 5.3.1 path verbatim' for per-stream logs. So which log a cosignature attests is not bound in the signed bytes, and 5.3.1.1's 'prior STH it cosigned ... against this log' cannot be keyed per log from this request. The lineage-head preimage binds lineage_key_id, which makes the omission conspicuous. This is a cross-log replay or confusion surface I could not resolve from the text.
- 5.1 contradicts itself on what the DEK is. The #140 paragraph says 'the group's MLS exporter secret for that epoch is the epoch DEK ... there is no second key'. The #169 paragraph says the DEK is HKDF(MLS-Exporter(...)) and 'the exporter secret itself is never wrapped'. Strike the first sentence or annotate it as superseded.
- Key separation on the MLS exporter is not argued. 5.1 derives through RFC 9420 MLS-Exporter. 5.4.1 forbids MLS-Exporter and runs a bare HKDF-Expand over the raw exporter_secret. 5.4.2 reuses the label 'ciris-edge/scope-privacy/symbol/v1' both to derive K_symbol and as the per-symbol info prefix, under SHA-256 at one layer and SHA3-256 at the other. Three access patterns to one secret, with no statement of why their outputs are independent.
- 5.4.4 MLS Welcome: the inviter's ML-DSA-65 signature covers only x25519_ephemeral_pub || len || mlkem768_ct. It has no domain label, no group id, no epoch and no invitee key id, so a signed encapsulation can be replayed in another context (for example an old Welcome). It is also ML-DSA-only, while every other authenticity surface in the book is the bound hybrid. Why?
- 5.4.3 justifies HKDF-SHA3-256 for the per-symbol KDF as 'harvest-now-decrypt-later (HNDL) discipline'. A hash-function choice inside a KDF has no bearing on HNDL. The HNDL exposure is the KEM. Either the rationale is wrong or a property is missing.
- Byte order: epoch is u64 big-endian in the 5.1 DEK info, the 5.3.3.1 nonce and the 5.3.3.2.3 header, but u64 little-endian (along with K) in the 5.3.3.6 receipt_signing_bytes, with no reason given.
- 5.4.3 says '1.4 KB, one MTU' without naming the link. The Reticulum path the book otherwise uses has a much smaller MTU (low confidence; check against Edge).
- 'Witness' carries three meanings (STH cosigner 5.3.1, privacy witness 5.4.5, WholenessWitness 6.1.1). The 5.4.5 box warns about this, but 1.13.3.3 uses the bare word.
- Anchors that do not resolve: §8.1.12.4, §5.6.8.4, §5.6.8.8.2, §7.7, §10.1.4 (5.2 pseudocode), §10.5.8.2, 'tracked §N.3', 'CEG §11', '§19.3', '§19.4', and many legacy fragment links (#1053-…, #10582-…). 5.3.6 says 'currently 1.0-rc27'.
- Undefined in this book: RET (used in 1.13.3 before expansion), L1 peer, Leviculum, LensCore knob, CEWP/cewp, D2–D6, E1–E4, V1–V3, P4, AV-59/60/61, F-AV-FRONTRUN. 'Option A' points to 4.5.12.1, which is outside the book.
- PCS: 1.13.3.1 says it is not provided for streams, while 5.1 says it is 'AVAILABLE ... OPTIONAL, operator-enabled'. Reconcile as: forward-only by default, PCS only where an operator mandates self-updates.
- Register vs CHANGELOG: re-pin 17 says 'CIRISServer#536 ... leave the staging tokens', yet claims.tsv still has 21 rows staged on #536, CLM-epoch-keying and CLM-multi-steward among them. I cannot tell which is true.

**Reviewer: presenter kinder than the text**
- The presenter counts 1.13.3.1 as complete and calls the adversary table one of six things that 'carry the trust'. The table is where the book overclaims most. Two 'mitigated' rows and one 'cannot decrypt' cell are true only under configurations the book says are off or optional today. The presenter lists these under partial and honest_limits but still offers the table as a pillar.
- The presenter lists 5.3.1/5.3.1.1 cosign as complete. It missed that the STH cosign preimage and request carry no log_id, even though 5.3.3.3 reuses the path verbatim for per-stream logs.
- The presenter treats the 5.4.2/5.4.3 indistinguishability claims as needing only a softer verb ('rests on'). The text is worse: record_id and symbol_index are cleartext, so 'a holder lacking ... record_id' is vacuous, and real/cover identity at the traffic level is unsupported by anything specified.
- The presenter's 5.4.5 note concerns 'IND' vs PRF wording only. It did not flag the witness_signing_key being reused as an HMAC key, or that 'count mod target_rate' with back-pressure leaks rate under sustained load.
- The presenter counts 5.1 per-stream DEK derivation as complete. It did not flag the surviving #140 sentence 'the group's MLS exporter secret for that epoch is the epoch DEK', which contradicts the #169 derivation two paragraphs later.
- The presenter flags 5.4.4 for missing domain separation but presents it as a question a cryptographer 'will ask'. I read it as a replay surface (no group, epoch or invitee binding) and a departure from the book's own hybrid-signature discipline (ML-DSA-only).
- On the trust anchor, the presenter's 'missing' list is right, but its promise that the book 'says up front where the protection stops' is kinder than the text. For the root itself it says where the protection starts (the pin) and never where it stops.

**Reviewer: answered**
- What 'privacy' means and where it stops: 1.13.3 limits privacy to two things, content-holding confidentiality and cohort-scoped visibility. 1.13.3.1 disclaims relationship existence, the communication graph, traffic analysis, unobservability against a global passive adversary (GPA) and stream post-compromise security, and names the field or mechanism that leaks each one (family_id on the envelope, announce/path-request, the STH cadence at T=2 s, cascade timing). This is the most useful paragraph in the book for my question.
- What carries content confidentiality: AES-256-GCM content, the DEK wrapped under X25519+ML-KEM-768 with wrap_algorithm v2 mandatory and v1 rejected, a single wire identifier compared as exact bytes with normalisation forbidden (5.1), and transit E1 as a hybrid wrap under the end-to-end (E2E) DEK, with classical CIR1 rejected (5.3.3.5).
- Why signatures are trusted: 5.3.2.4.3.1 requires both halves (Ed25519 over JCS, then ML-DSA-65 over JCS || ed25519_sig) at every federation admission gate, the trace store included. There is no toggle and no window. It also states the right reason content addressing does not stop a CRQC-era forger: the forger hashes their own forgery.
- Nonce safety for chunks (5.3.3.1): the STREAM layout, a byte-exact HKDF info with an argument that it parses unambiguously, epoch reset that is safe because the key changes, a single-sender-per-(stream_id, epoch) invariant enforced by giving each sender its own stream_id, and last_flag for truncation and append resistance. The sound parts are fully stated.
- Forward secrecy on removal (5.1): removals are coalesced, which bounds exposure to at most T = 2 s. Ungated public rosters are exempted, and the text says honestly that they make no claim against a departed viewer.
- Per-stream DEK separation (5.1, #169): the exporter secret is never wrapped, so a kept wrap on one stream cannot open a peer's unkept stream in the same epoch.
- What happens when a log or founder equivocates: 5.3.1 and 5.3.1.1 cover it. The Registry anchors the consistency proof to the prior root it recorded itself. Fork attempt, equivocation and silence are separate outcomes, and a strict-majority quorum means equivocation names a double-signer. Lineage-head and STH cosign preimages carry distinct domain labels.
- Why the trust root is trusted, and that it is not self-authenticating: 5.3.4 says the bundle is 'tamper-evident, not authentic', requires an out-of-band anchor (a pinned bundle_fingerprint or the accord-holder key fingerprints), signs no response wrapper, and states that the shipped charters have witnessed mode off.
- Fail-secure, non-silent recipient exclusion (5.2): a recipient with no ML-KEM key is excluded and hard_case:recipient_excluded is emitted inside the cohort. There is no fallback to v1 or to plaintext.
- Announce suppression (5.4.6): directed announces inherit the prohibition (#91), and the forwarder's role is reachability only (#132). The section states exactly what the forwarder learns, and explicitly says cover framing 'is not a claim against that inference'.
- Delivery receipts (5.3.3.6) are checked by a JOIN against a published root (witness-cosigned for accountable streams), and the text keeps delivery distinct from consumption.

**Presenter: complete**
- 1.13.3.1 non-goals: complete and well-bounded. Relationship existence, the communication graph, traffic analysis, GPA unobservability and stream PCS are each disclaimed by name, with the mechanism that leaks (family_id on the envelope, announce/path-request, STH cadence at T=2 s, cascade timing). A red-teamer can work from this list.
- 5.1 one construction, one wire identifier (x25519_mlkem768_aes256_gcm_hkdf_sha256). Matching is by exact bytes, and normalising a variant spelling is forbidden. Domain-separation constants form a separate class and are never snake_cased. The rule is closed and testable.
- 5.1 per-stream DEK derivation (#169): HKDF-SHA256 over MLS-Exporter('ciris-stream-dek/v1', empty context), with info = label || stream_id_utf8 || epoch_be8. The exporter secret itself is never wrapped, so one stream's kept wrap cannot open a peer's unkept stream in the same epoch. The construction is fully specified, but its KAT evidence is staged (see partial).
- 5.1 removal coalescing: one rotation per STH window, so exposure is at most T, and the cascade cost is worked at N = 10^6. Also complete: the public-broadcast exemption from rotation, which is honest that an ungated roster makes no confidentiality claim against departed viewers.
- 5.2 structural invisibility as two layers (the unconditional non-emission rule, and at-rest encryption as defence in depth). Fail-secure exclusion of a recipient with no ML-KEM key is never a downgrade to v1 or plaintext, and it is never silent: hard_case:recipient_excluded is emitted inside the cohort. CLM-structural-invisibility is established on a conformance test (test_570).
- 5.3.1 / 5.3.1.1 STH cosign and lineage-head cosign. The canonical preimages carry distinct domain labels (no cross-replay). Ed25519 signs over canonical and ML-DSA-65 over canonical || ed25519_sig. The Registry anchors the consistency proof to the prior root it recorded itself, not one the requester claims. Fork attempt, equivocation and silence are three separate outcomes. CLM-consistency-proof is established.
- 5.3.2.4.3 / 5.3.2.4.3.1 tier invariant tier = federation implies a verified hybrid signature. It applies at every federation ingest, trace store included. The argument against relying on content addressing (a CRQC forger hashes their own forgery) is correct and stated. CLM-two and CLM-admission-pqc are established on tier_ingest.rs.
- 5.3.2.5 the size check runs before the full-SHA check, and the rule is explicit about where size lives (descriptor, not the frozen envelope). 5.3.2.6: the render decision comes from verified, sniffed bytes and receiver policy, never from the sender's declared format. Note that CLM-blob-size-checked-first and CLM-render-tier-receiver-policy are staged (see partial).
- 5.3.3.1 STREAM nonce (prefix[7] || counter_be[4] || last_flag[1]): byte-exact info, an argument that the info parse is unambiguous, cross-epoch counter reset shown safe by key change, the single-sender-per-(stream_id, epoch) invariant, and truncation/append resistance from last_flag. CLM-stream is established.
- 5.3.3.2.5 one chunk seal (#140): the inner seal is the 5.3.3.1 seal, the rc6 second inner nonce is retired, and the outer per-link nonce is pinned.
- 5.3.3.6 delivery receipts: verification is a JOIN against a published (for accountable streams, witness-cosigned) STH root, not a bare signature check, and a receipt proves delivery, not consumption. CLM-delivery is established.
- 5.4.1 deterministic record_id: CBOR major types pinned, three normative vectors exercising the u8, u16 and u32 epc headers, HKDF-Expand taken over the raw exporter_secret (not RFC 9420 MLS-Exporter). CLM-record-id is established on test_500.
- 5.4.6 announce suppression, including the #91 ruling that a directed announce inherits the prohibition, and the #132 ruling that a forwarder's only role is reachability. The section states what the forwarder learns and gives the bar for any multi-hop amendment. CLM-announce-suppress, CLM-scope-destination and CLM-scoped-body-identity-link are established.

**Presenter: partial (and what it leans on)**
- 1.13.3.3 equivocating founder, marked 'mitigated': the mechanism (witnessed lineage heads, 5.3.1) is specified, but the shipped roots are minted with witnessed mode off (witness_quorum: 0, no directory, 5.3.4 'Shipped charter defaults'). Today a founder-set fork is caught only by the pinned bundle_fingerprint at attach time, not by witnesses. CLM-lineage-head-cosign is staged on CIRISServer#693. The row should say 'mitigated when witnessed mode is on; off at the 0.5.219 mint'.
- 1.13.3.3 equivocating producer, marked 'mitigated': per 5.3.3.3 the witness cosign is OPTIONAL. Best-effort (open-media) streams have a producer-signed root only. The accountable tier is described as 'impl-pending the streaming substrate AND STH consistency-proof enforcement'. The mitigation holds for accountable streams only. The 5.3.3 bifurcation table still marks media multicast 'spec-now, impl substrate-pending' (the RC1-era label).
- 5.1 epoch keying: CLM-epoch-keying is staged on CIRISServer#536, and CLM-stream-dek-per-stream on CIRISVerify#308 (KATs not yet landed). Discrepancy to check: the CHANGELOG re-pin 17 entry (2026-10-14) says 'CIRISServer#536 ... leave the staging tokens', yet constitution/claims.tsv still has 21 rows staging on CIRISServer#536, among them CLM-epoch-keying and CLM-multi-steward in this slice. Either the register is stale or the CHANGELOG sentence is too broad.
- 5.1 tree vs flat cascade: the choice depends on 'whether the RET mesh offers efficient multicast', which the text calls 'the open transport question (the bandwidth model's one free parameter)'. The cost claims rest on a bandwidth model that is not in this book. 5.1 also says that adding the stream/epoch axis needs a Persist CHECK-arm migration, and nothing in this book tracks that migration.
- 5.1 / 5.3.3.5 E1 transit KEX binding (#171, rc8): this book has one sentence. The binding, its four properties and key separation live at CC 3.3.6.2 (P-other). CLM-transit-kex-binding is staged on CIRISVerify#309 and CIRISEdge#805. Labels, KDF layout and KATs are 'Verify's', so the hop KEX is not byte-pinned in this book.
- 5.3.3.2.5 one chunk seal: CLM-one-chunk-seal has its persist half established (re-pin 16), and the edge half is still staged on CIRISEdge#805.
- 5.3.4 trust-root discovery: CLM-multi-steward is staged on CIRISConformance#90, CIRISServer#536 and #537, and CLM-trust-root-bundle-route on CIRISServer#537. 'CIRISServer serves it ... once the fold's phase 2a lands'. The anchor model is sound, but the route is not evidenced in this book. Revocation (T4) and attach freshness (T4a, T6) are defined in CC 3.2, outside this book.
- 5.3.2.5 / 5.3.2.6: CLM-blob-size-checked-first and CLM-render-tier-receiver-policy are both staged on CIRISServer#614. The node ingest pipeline is 'CIRISServer's to build against this slot'.
- 5.4.6 device roster: CLM-device-roster-announced is staged on CIRISServer#691.
- 5.4.3 fixed 1.4 KB envelopes and cover: these lean on a 'companion Poisson-cover emission discipline' and a 'companion per-community archive-policy section', neither of which appears in this book or in part_5 (grep finds no Poisson definition). The cover budget (~10–60 KB/s/peer) is asserted without derivation.
- 1.13.3.1 / 1.13.3.4 GPA unobservability depends on the 'CIRISNodeCore Anonymous Tier — Sphinx onion routing', which is outside this book and has no claim row here.
- 1.13.3.3 coordinated reviewer capture leans on Part 4 (4.4.2 aggregation defaults, 4.4.3.8 Policy A), Part 6 (6.2.3.1 Signal_eff, 6.2.2 k_eff) and Part 8 (8.6.1 grades, 8.3.1 R14). R14 is present at part_8 line 180. The 8.6.1 grades match the CHANGELOG (Kish: identity; clique_neutralization: identity / theorem-given-model on the newer pin only; k_eff saturation: measured, 6 substrates).

**Presenter: missing**
- The question 'what happens when it is wrong' is not answered for the trust anchor itself. 5.3.4 roots everything in a bundle_fingerprint 'baked into conformant builds'. This book has no case for a compromised build or distribution channel, a compromised accord holder (the recovery path is in CC 3.2 T4, outside the book), or a wrong pin. 1.13.3.3 has no row for 'compromised trust anchor / accord holder' or 'compromised build pipeline'.
- A row for at-rest-encryption-off. 5.2 says at-rest encryption 'MAY default off as a v1 migration posture'. Yet 1.13.3.2 claims end-to-end confidentiality is 'additionally provided by the per-epoch DEK', and the 1.13.3.3 'compromised substrate node' row says 'cannot decrypt self/family content (no DEK)'. With at-rest off, a substrate node holding self/family bytes holds plaintext. The adversary table never states that condition.
- A single primitives-and-assumptions table: each primitive, its security level and the assumption it rests on (IND-CCA2 of the ML-KEM/X25519 combiner, SUF-CMA of the bound hybrid signature, PRF security of HMAC-SHA3, collision resistance of SHA-256). It should also say what breaks if one half of a hybrid falls. The hybrid AND-verify rule covers signatures (5.3.2.4.3.1), but the KEM combiner's security when one component breaks is never argued.
- Any composed-protocol security argument or adversarial run. formal/mesh_net checks invariants (Inv_Invisible, Inv_KnownHold and others) over an abstract model, not cryptographic games. 5.4.2 and 5.4.3 cite reductions ('by reduction to XChaCha20-Poly1305 IND-CCA + HKDF-SHA3') with no proof or citation of one. No red-team or adversarial test results are in this book (8.8.7 cadence is named elsewhere, not exercised here).
- 1.13.3.3 coordinated reviewer capture names the `signed`-mean residual (R14) but not a stronger one. 4.4.2 makes `positive-only` dimensions aggregate by Max ('any positive is conclusive'), so a single captured trusted attester settles them outright. The reviewer-capture row omits this, and it is worse than the plain mean.
- Side channels and implementation attacks: timing of the hybrid KEM and signature code, RNG failure (the deterministic nonces help but are not discussed as such), key-storage compromise of an occurrence, and fault attacks. Only the render tier (5.3.2.6) treats implementation risk.
- MLS Welcome (5.4.4): the inviter's ML-DSA-65 signature covers only the encapsulation bytes. It has no domain-separation label and no binding to the group id, epoch or invitee key id. A cryptographer will ask why this is free of cross-context reuse; the text does not say.
- Phone-class 'relay-blinded path' resolution (5.4.6): the blinding mechanism is not specified, so what the L1-relay parent learns about the phone's group lookups is not bounded.
- No adversary row for a malicious relay or forwarder distinct from the 'compromised substrate node' row, despite #132 giving forwarders a sanctioned role. Also no row for the 5.4.5 privacy-witness operator misbehaving beyond 'deviation is slashing'.
- Downgrade and version-negotiation attacks: the CEG-Version header MINOR mismatch is 'compatible (clients MAY warn)'. The transitional accept-only alias flag (5.1) and the CIR1/CIR2 transport negotiation (5.3.3.5 E1) are not analysed as downgrade surfaces in 1.13.3.

**Presenter: jargon**
- Stale and legacy anchors that a reader cannot follow: §8.1.12.4, §5.6.8.4, §5.6.8.8.2, §10.1.4, §7.7 (5.2 pseudocode), §10.5.8.2 (5.3.3.2.3), 'CEG §11', '§19.3', '§19.4' (5.4.1, 5.4.2), 'tracked §N.3' (5.3.3.2.1), and fragment links such as #1053-…, #103-…, #10582-… throughout 5.x. They break the precise-noun budget because the nouns do not resolve.
- 5.3.6 says 'currently 1.0-rc27' for the CEG-Version header, which is stale against VERSION 1.0-rc8.
- 1.13.3 uses CEG/RET before RET is expanded in this book. Also undefined here: L1 peer, Leviculum (5.4.6), LensCore knob (5.1 P4), CEWP / cewp (5.2, 5.4), D2/D3/D4/D6, E1–E4, V1/V2/V3 locks, P4, AV-59/60/61, F-AV-FRONTRUN.
- 'Option A' (1.13.3.1, 5.1) points to 4.5.12.1, which is outside the book.
- 'Witness' carries three meanings: STH cosigner (5.3.1), privacy witness chain (5.4.5) and WholenessWitness (6.1.1). The 5.4.5 box warns about this, but 1.13.3.3 says 'witness' without qualifying it.
- 5.4.5 says cover leaves are 'cryptographically indistinguishable from real Merkle roots under the IND of HMAC-SHA3'. HMAC is a PRF, so the precise statement is PRF-security. 'Commits to count mod target_rate' is not an obviously meaningful commitment and needs stating precisely.
- 5.4.2 says 'no cover marker' while 5.4.3 requires type=cover inside the AEAD header. These agree only if 'marker' means 'cleartext marker'; say so.
- Byte-order inconsistency a reader will trip on: epoch is u64 big-endian in the 5.1 DEK info, the 5.3.3.1 nonce and the 5.3.3.2.3 header, but u64 little-endian in the 5.3.3.6 receipt_signing_bytes (with no stated reason).
- '1.4 KB, one MTU' (5.4.3) gives no transport, and the default MTU on the Reticulum path this book otherwise uses is smaller. Name the link the MTU refers to (low confidence; check against the Edge implementation).
- Comparative framing against the house rule: 1.13.3.4 says 'What CEG adds beyond systems that ship default privacy with no in-network governance'; the 5.4.6 Position paragraph says 'surveyed zero-emission systems accept linear fan-out ... systems that kept coordinator-efficient fan-out uniformly declare group metadata out of scope'; and 5.3.2.6 names Signal, WhatsApp, Element and others (that one is prior art for a recommended set, which is defensible).

**Presenter: rc8 changes**
- 1.13.3.3 gains two adversary rows from Xiong et al. (arXiv:2606.26028), drafted 2026-10-14 (commit 7bf0af9). Placeholder identity: 'addressed by construction', because no registry entry constitutes agent-hood and liveness is never a durable claim (D6). Coordinated reviewer capture: 'mitigated', with the 4.4.2 plain-mean default for `signed` dimensions named as R14 in 8.3.1 and ratchet:flag:* advisory with no evidence row. RATCHET-validated claims are cited at their 8.6.1 grades. Hu & Rong are added to the informational citations.

Kept is now per stream and set by the producer; turning kept on mid-stream wraps current and later epochs, turning it off is forward-only (CLM-kept-mid-stream, normative); 'recorded' is not a field. Erratum: the rekey table row 'Member addition: NO rotation' (the rc6 wrapped-DEK model) is corrected, and an Add now advances the epoch.
- 5.3.3.5 E1 gains the transit KEX binding sentence (steward ruling 2026-10-13, #171, raised via CIRISVerify#309). The relay's static transit pair on its signed transport_destination is used against the initiator's ephemeral key, and KeyPackage init keys and ephemeral-only exchanges are refused. CLM-transit-kex-binding is staged on Verify#309 and Edge#805. The companion in-row/cross-row key-separation ruling (CIRISPersist#1078) lands at 3.3.6.2.

- Re-pin 17 server leg (2026-10-14): the CHANGELOG says CIRISServer#536 left the staging tokens, but this slice still shows CLM-epoch-keying and CLM-multi-steward staged on #536. Reconcile before review.

**Presenter: honest limits**
- 1.13.3.3 'Compromised substrate node — cannot decrypt self/family content (no DEK)': true only when at-rest encryption is enabled, and 5.2 lets it default off. Soften to 'cannot discover or fetch it (5.2); cannot decrypt it where at-rest encryption is on (5.2 layer 2)'. Same fix for 1.13.3.2 'End-to-end content confidentiality is additionally provided'.
- 1.13.3.3 'Equivocating founder — mitigated': the shipped charters have witnessed mode off (5.3.4, witness_quorum: 0), so in deployment today it is mitigated only by the out-of-band pinned bundle. Say so in the row.
- 1.13.3.3 'Equivocating producer — mitigated': it applies to accountable streams with witness cosign (5.3.3.3), which is optional and impl-pending. Best-effort streams are producer-signed only.
- 1.13.3.1 says stream PCS is not provided, while 5.1 says PCS is 'AVAILABLE ... OPTIONAL, operator-enabled'. Reconcile: forward-only by default, PCS only where an operator mandates periodic self-updates.
- 5.4.3 says 'an on-path observer cannot distinguish a large publication from idle substrate maintenance', and 5.4.5 claims rate-hiding. Both are traffic-analysis claims, which 1.13.3.1 disclaims at base CEG/RET. The cover discipline they rest on is not in the book. The reviewer's standing is to kill exactly this kind of claim. Either bound it as 'below the cohort boundary, against a passive observer of envelope sizes, given the companion cover budget', or move it under the 1.13.3.1 non-goal as defence in depth. The 5.4.6 #132 paragraph already concedes that cover framing 'is not a claim against that inference', so the book is internally inconsistent here.
- 5.4.2 and 5.4.3 'indistinguishable from random ... by reduction to XChaCha20-Poly1305 IND-CCA + HKDF-SHA3': soften to 'rests on' until a reduction is written or cited.
- 1.13.3.3 coordinated reviewer capture, 'k_eff ceiling is mechanized and measured': per 8.6.1 the measurement is k_eff saturation across 6 substrates (≈ 7 ± 2) and the ceiling is graded identity. Neither measures Sybil resistance in a feedback setting. Say that what is measured is the ceiling, not resistance to coordinated reviewers. Also name the `positive-only` Max default (4.4.2) beside R14.
- 1.13.3.4 'This recovers the spreading-correlated child-safety catches': an empirical effectiveness claim for the perceptual-hash tripwire with no evidence row. Perceptual-hash evasion and hash-list integrity are not addressed.
- 5.2 'Locality dividend ... ~65% of activity stays local' and the 5.1 removal-coalescing bandwidth figures (3.1 Tbps, 18.7 Gbps at N = 10^6) are scaling-model outputs, not measurements. Label them as modelled.
- 5.4.6 Position, 'the corner's two known prices are bought back rather than paid' and 'structural invisibility hides the election': these read as comparative or superiority claims. Soften to a description of the construction.
- 5.2's opening says 'The strongest privacy guarantee in the system is the one the operator cannot switch off', and the cewp quote says 'the wire format can't carry them in the first place'. Both are bounded only by the scope box that follows. Keep the scope box adjacent and cite 1.13.3.1 in the opening sentence.

## P08 — The Mathematics (Part 6 + Lean)

**Reader:** a mathematician and formal verifier  
**Verdict:** needs work  
**One change:** Put the formal statements into the book. For every claim labelled theorem-given-model or identity, print the exact Lean statement of the cited object (hypotheses, conclusion, and which of λ_geo and κ are axioms), with #print axioms output and a no-sorry attestation at the pin. Then rewrite each prose claim to that exact strength. In particular, 6.2.1 becomes an inequality under axiomatised constants with only the remainder order mechanised, 6.2.3's uniqueness becomes cited-not-mechanised, and λ_geo ≈ 2r is dropped. Until a verifier can see the statement, 'mechanized' is a report, not a proof.

**Promise (presenter):** To a mathematician and formal verifier, this book promises two things, and it promises them at different strengths. First (6.2): a small set of exact statements about effective diversity, each labelled at its strength (identity, theorem-given-model, measured, cited-external). These are the Kish identity k_eff = k/(1+ρ̄(k−1)) with its floor of 1 and its ceiling of 1/ρ̄; a source-discounted sustainability integral σ in event-time form that does not depend on polling cadence, with the exponential's semigroup property; the clique-neutralisation bound Signal_eff → 1; and J = F, which is a co-denotation and nothing more. Lean witnesses for these sit upstream in coherence-ratchet at pin 0c4edfb. The book also promises negative discipline. The collapse geometry is symmetric between the honest and the deceptive region and so justifies nothing (6.2 status note, 6.2.1 scope). No operating corridor is stated (6.2.2). The k → k_eff splice is named as unproven (6.2.1). Second (6.1): byte-exact, integer-only, deterministic wire constructions. These are a Merkle scheme with a stated malleability argument, signed aggregation metadata with dominance and multiplicity gates whose constants are pinned, a procedure-relative "noise floor" predicate against a named adversary A₀, and fountain-storage reliability numbers stated as exact binomial arithmetic on a stated placement model. All of it is pinned by cross-implementation conformance vectors rather than by proof. The book does not promise that any of this is mechanised beyond the named Lean objects, and 6.2.5 says plainly that no operational instrument for k_eff, σ or diversity exists.

**Reviewer: kills**
- 6.2.1, 'V(k) = V(0) · exp(-λ_geo · k_eff) + O(r² · k_eff)' labelled '(theorem-given-model; this section is the statement of record …)' 'under the stated preconditions'. The only mechanised object is an upper bound (≤) with κ·r²·k_eff explicit, and λ_geo and κ are axiomatised. The decay law itself is assumed: the claim row says 'ASSUMED decay law' and 6.2.5 says 'the decay law is assumed and only its remainder order is mechanized'. An equality presented as a theorem under geometric hypotheses is stronger than an inequality about axiomatised constants, and the chapter contradicts itself between 6.2.1 and 6.2.5. Restate it as: 'Model (assumed): V(k) ≤ V(0)e^{−λ_geo k_eff} + κ r² k_eff; mechanised: the remainder's count factor is k_eff'.
- 6.2.1, 'its radius r < 0.5 (the theorem's decay rate λ_geo ≈ 2r)' and the nomenclature note's 'λ_geo ≈ 2r … the radius of the convex deceptive region'. No theorem relates λ_geo to r, because Lean takes λ_geo as a free positive constant. The note also calls a rate 'the radius', and it calls the region 'deceptive' two paragraphs after proving that the bound cannot tell honest from deceptive. Kill 'the theorem's'.
- 6.2.3, 'Among positive, measurable decay functions it is the only one satisfying the semigroup law (theorem-given-model; lean:…sigma_decay_semigroup)'. The cited lemma proves that exp satisfies the law, which is existence. Uniqueness is the measurable Cauchy functional equation, and it holds only up to the rate parameter. Uniqueness is not mechanised and not cited. Split the sentence: 'satisfies (mechanised); uniqueness is classical (cited, not mechanised)'.
- 6.1.2, 'Steady-state storage to remember all of history is O(log T) … the N→1 fan-in makes forever-memory sublinear'. Keeping every level of a fan-in-N pyramid costs T·(1 + 1/N + 1/N² + …) = Θ(T). O(log T) needs bounded retention per level, which is a hypothesis the text never states. As written the result is false.
- 6.1.1, 'the CVE-2012-2459 odd-node-duplication malleability is not exploitable in this construction's uses', with the reasons '(1) … mandatorily hybrid-signed' and '(2) … verified by recomputation from the full source-id list … (malleability moot)'. Neither reason addresses the attack. With odd-node duplication, the leaf lists [a,b,c] and [a,b,c,c] produce the same root. Both are lexicographically sorted, and neither 6.1.1 nor 6.1.2.1.1 requires deduplication: the 2.6.1.1.1 set-semantics rule sorts but does not dedup, whereas StorageBudgetV1 explicitly says 'sorted … and deduplicated'. A signature binds the root, not the list, and full-list recomputation accepts the duplicated list too. A descend output with a repeated last member therefore 're-derives the parent's member_commitment byte-for-byte'. The security argument is stated stronger than shown. It is repaired by requiring strictly increasing leaves, or by adopting the 6962 prefix with lone-node promotion.
- 6.1 opening, 6.1.5.1 and 6.1.2.3, 'RaptorQ decode is all-or-nothing at block level … below the decode threshold a layer yields nothing'. RaptorQ is systematic, so received source symbols are plaintext fragments of the layer. 'Nothing' is true of the block decode and false of the information held. Because the noise-floor privacy predicate leans on this sentence ('residual fidelity there is exactly 0'), the claim is true only relative to R. It should say so, not state it as a property of the code.
- 6.1.2.1.2, 'since v3 a lying n_eff is mechanically provable against mass_commitment from held evidence'. n_eff = round((Σm)²/Σm²) is not pinned to integer arithmetic, and neither is the tie rule for round() at .5. Meanwhile mass_commitment commits round(m×1e6), not m. The similarity metric explicitly bans IEEE-754, but n_eff and mass_to_fixed get no such pinning. Recomputing from committed fixed-point masses can therefore differ from an honest aggregator's n_eff near a rounding boundary. 'Provable lying' could convict an honest producer, so this is stated stronger than the spec supports.
- 6.1.2.3, 'Conformance — proven cross-impl.' What was reproduced cross-implementation is the v2 family. The same section makes version < 3 inadmissible ('a verifier MUST reject a tier at version < 3') and says v3 needs a re-cut. The proven artefact is a version the spec now rejects, so the heading should read 'proven at v2; v3 unproven'.
- 6.2.2, 'Symmetric noise mints even-order structure from four slots up, so the rigidity pole carries a positive floor at k ≥ 4'. This is a mathematical assertion stated as fact, with no theorem, no citation and no claim row. Either cite it or mark it as conjecture.
- claims.tsv, CLM-sigma-semigroup, 'established'. The summary asserts the whole event-time group-share σ rule and the non-conformance of the per-signal and linear forms. The only evidence is the semigroup lemma (plus nonneg). Narrow the row to what the lemma proves, or demote it until the CIRISConformance#57 vectors exist.
- Vendored evidence for CLM-math-collapse. The witness set still includes Core.Corridor.corridor_keff_range_asymptotic ('rests on the corridorBounds axiom') and the Core.Dynamics corridor-exit theorems, but 6.2.2 withdraws the corridor. A collapse claim should not be backed by objects whose axiom the book voids.

**Reviewer: unanswered**
- Are the cited Lean objects actually proved? The packet has 'Formal witnesses: none'. The repo vendors only a TSV inventory filtered on status==mechanized. There is no Lean source, no lake build recipe, no #print axioms output and no no-sorry statement for coherence-ratchet@0c4edfb. 'mechanized' is an upstream self-report that I cannot check.
- What is the exact Lean statement of collapseUpperBound / remainder_scales_with_k_eff, and with which hypotheses? The inventory note says it 'axiomatizes framework primitives λ_geo, κ' and states 'V(k) ≤ …'. If the bound is a defined function of k_eff, the remainder theorem may hold by unfolding a definition and say nothing about any volume. Convexity, r<0.5, Grassmannian-uniform orientations and k≥3 are not shown to appear as Lean hypotheses.

- The v3 AggregationMetaV1 vector family: the cross-implementation proof covers v2 only, and v3 needs a re-cut (6.1.2.3).
