- The 'positive floor at k ≥ 4' for the rigidity pole (6.2.2) has no proof and no citation.

**Reviewer: confusions**
- 'theorem-given-model' is defined as 'machine-checked under stated hypotheses', but the book never shows which hypotheses the machine checked. The label is unverifiable from this book.
- 6.2.1 gives the hypotheses as 'Grassmannian-uniform' orientations; 6.2.5 gives them as 'near-orthogonal constraints'. These are different conditions, and neither is shown in Lean.
- 6.2.1 says 'codimensions add' for 'independent, non-redundant (diverse)' manifolds. The needed condition is transversality / general position, and it is unnamed.
- 6.2.1 uses 'rationale space ℝ of dimension D'. ℝ is the reals.
- 6.2.2 says 'the variance identity itself holds for the average off-diagonal correlation without requiring full exchangeability'. It also needs equal variances, which are not stated.
- 6.2.2 says 'J is strictly decreasing in ρ̄'. That holds only for k>1 and λ_op·σ>0. k_eff_monotone_rho is noted as 'monotone decreasing … for k>1', and strictness of J is not mechanised.
- Two distinct objects are both called 'Kish': the mass-weighted participation ratio n_eff = (Σm)²/Σm² in 6.1.2.1.2, and the equicorrelated variance-of-mean k_eff in 6.2.2. The book never distinguishes them.
- 6.1.5.1 calls N=20 the 'lossless threshold' and 'exactly computed' reliability. RaptorQ decode at exactly N symbols succeeds only with high probability (RFC 6330), so the binomial figures are for an idealised deterministic-threshold decoder. Spread-duplicate placement is also a model choice whose source is not given.
- 6.1.2 says that (R, ε) is 'pinned by a CC 6.1.4 conformance vector the same way RC1–RC7 are'. RC1–RC7 are undefined in this book, and no such vector appears in 6.1.4.
- Protocol and house vocabulary is used inside definitions without a glossary: CEG, 1+4, JCS, M-1, PIN-NORMATIVE, CEWP-1.0, BLINKING_DOT, F-3 matrix, AIR-module, 'Conscience sensitivity' (used to define λ_op), Book IX, CCA v5, HE-300, Annex G.
- References to material I was not given: the coherence-ratchet Lean source, bench:RATCHET#8, RATCHET#9/#10/#17, CIRISConformance#57, CIRISEdge#438, FEDERATION_SCALING_MODEL §4.4, and the Part 2/3/5/8 sections cited for definitions.
- 6.1.5.1 restates its constants from 'CIRISEdge src/holonomic/fountain_defaults.rs @ d53c7d4', but the claim row points at CIRISServer/src/holonomic/fountain_defaults.rs. It is unclear which file is authoritative.

**Reviewer: presenter kinder than the text**
- The presenter listed the 6.1.1 Merkle construction as complete, 'with a stated malleability argument'. The argument does not hold. Signing and full-list recomputation do not prevent [a,b,c] and [a,b,c,c] from colliding under odd-node duplication, and the construction does not require deduplicated leaves.
- The presenter described the Lean witnesses as sitting upstream and credited 'a full Tendsto proof'. That phrase is the TSV note's self-description. Nothing in the repo lets a verifier confirm it. The presenter did list the missing source, but still graded CLM-math-kish complete on the inventory alone.
- The presenter put the 6.2.1 collapse bound under 'partial' and proposed relabelling it. The text is worse than partial. It states an equality as a theorem under geometric hypotheses, while its own 6.2.5 and claim row say the decay law is assumed. That is a kill under my standing, not a partial.
- The presenter called 6.2.1/6.2.5 'the most self-limiting writing in the book'. 6.2.5 is. But 6.2.1 still carries '(theorem-given-model)', '= … + O(…)' and 'the theorem's decay rate λ_geo ≈ 2r'. The self-limitation sits in the scope paragraphs, not in the statement of record.
- The presenter said the reliability numbers 'match the text exactly'. The arithmetic does, but 'exactly computed' assumes decode is certain at N symbols, which RaptorQ does not guarantee.
- The presenter said the R9 calibration 'checks out'. It does, but the presenter did not notice that n_eff and mass_to_fixed are left in unpinned real arithmetic. That undercuts the 'mechanically provable' claim the presenter accepted.
- The presenter placed the O(log T) storage claim and the 'yields nothing' RaptorQ claim under missing/honest-limits as softenings. The O(log T) claim is false as stated without a retention hypothesis, and it should be withdrawn, not softened.
- The presenter missed the unsupported 6.2.2 assertion 'positive floor at k ≥ 4'.

**Reviewer: answered**
- Kish identity and ceiling (6.2.2). The statement is true: k_eff = k/(1+ρ̄(k−1)), equal to 1 at ρ̄=1 and to k at ρ̄=0, and bounded above by 1/ρ̄ for ρ̄∈(0,1] because k/(1+ρ̄(k−1)) ≤ 1/ρ̄ iff ρ̄ ≤ 1. The vendored inventory lists matching objects: Core.BaseIdentity.k_eff_bounded, k_eff_bounded_above and k_eff_asymptotic_ceiling. The packet correctly separates the PSD domain [−1/(k−1),1] from the [0,1] clamp and calls the clamp policy.
- J = F (6.2.4). It is stated as rfl co-denotation only (Core.Coherence.J_eq_F, J_eq_F_kish), and the text explicitly disclaims any measurement content. It is stated no stronger than its proof.
- Clique neutralisation (6.2.3.1). Signal_eff = n_src/(1+ρ̄_src(n_src−1)) tends to 1 as ρ̄_src → 1. This is true, and it is the Kish functional applied to sources (Core.SignalSourceDiscount.clique_neutralization, Signal_eff_is_kish). The text correctly notes that the mechanised objects take (n_src, ρ̄_src) as inputs and do not define the grouping.
- Elementary σ analysis (6.2.3) checks by hand. The interior-signal over-credit factor is exp(d(t+Δt−t_i)). The continuous-rate contribution is (w·Signal/d)(1−e^{−dΔt}). The half-life is ln2/0.05 ≈ 13.9 d, and the linear form goes negative past 1/d = 20 d. The event-time form is cadence-free trivially, because it is a function of the event set and t.
- Reliability arithmetic (6.1.5.1). I recomputed P(Bin(22,q)+Bin(4,1−(1−q)²) ≥ 20) and got 0.999926, 0.995039, 0.957288 and 0.845021. The naive P(Bin(30,q) ≥ 20) gives 0.999911 at .90 and 0.997058 at .85. All of these match the text. The text labels them arithmetic on a model, not measurements.
- R9 calibration (6.1.2.1.2). For independent uniform bytes, E|Δ| = (256²−1)/(3·256) ≈ 85.33, so similarity ≈ 0.665; the text's 0.667 is a rounding of 85/255. Cosine ≈ 0.75 is correct. The largest connected component is at least the max clique, so the gate only tightens. The 900-near-duplicate negative passes the mass gate and fails the multiplicity gate, which is correct.
- Negative discipline (6.2.1 scope, 6.2.2, 6.2.5). The collapse bound is invariant under swapping the honest and deceptive regions. No corridor is stated. The k→k_eff splice is named as unproven. The informativeness condition κr²k_eff < V(0)e^{−λ_geo k_eff} is stated. The noise floor is procedure-relative against A₀, and the text says 'aggregation is not anonymization'. These are honest scope statements.

**Presenter: complete**
- 6.2.2 Kish identity and its ceiling: the statement matches the vendored Lean inventory (Core.BaseIdentity: k_eff, k_eff_at_zero/one, k_eff_monotone_rho, k_eff_bounded giving 1 ≤ k_eff ≤ k on ρ∈[0,1], k_eff_asymptotic_ceiling with a full Tendsto proof, k_eff_bounded_above). The domain-of-validity paragraph correctly separates the positive-semidefinite domain ρ̄ ∈ [−1/(k−1),1] from the [0,1] clamp, which it calls policy. It also correctly notes that averaging dilutes block-structured correlation and that the exposed direction is the one that grants. CLM-math-kish.
- 6.2.4 J = F: stated as an identity discharged by rfl (Core.Coherence.J_eq_F, J_eq_F_kish), with the explicit disclaimer that the Lean object certifies co-denotation only and not what the expression measures. The CLM-math-JF summary says the same. Nothing is overstated here.
- 6.2.3.1 clique neutralisation: Signal_eff is literally the Kish functional on sources, and clique_neutralization, Signal_eff_monotone_ρ_src, sigma_step_with_source_discount and sigma_step_nonneg are vendored as mechanized. CLM-sigma-srccorr.
- 6.2.3 elementary analysis checks by hand. The interior-signal over-credit factor exp(d(t+Δt−t_i)) is right. So are the continuous-rate contribution (w·Signal/d)(1−e^{−dΔt}), the half-life ln2/0.05 ≈ 13.9 d, and the linear form going negative past Δt = 1/d = 20 d.
- 6.1.5.1 reliability numbers: I recomputed P(Bin(22,q)+Bin(4,1−(1−q)²) ≥ 20) and got 0.999926, 0.995039, 0.957288 and 0.845021 for q = .95, .90, .85, .80. The naive P(Bin(30,q) ≥ 20) gives 0.999911 at .90 and 0.997058 at .85. Both sets match the text exactly. The text correctly labels them arithmetic on a model, not measurements, and states no design target pending CIRISEdge#438.
- 6.1.2.1.2 R9 constants: the calibration arithmetic checks out. Independent uniform bytes give E|Δ| ≈ 85.3/255, so similarity ≈ 0.665. Cosine on positive byte vectors ≈ 0.75. Largest connected component ≥ max clique, so the gate errs only toward tightening. The 900-near-duplicate negative is correct: n_eff = N passes the mass gate and fails the multiplicity gate.
- 6.1.1 Merkle construction: the scheme is fully specified (SHA-256 leaves, lexicographic order, odd-node duplication, empty sentinel). The decision not to adopt the RFC 6962 prefix is argued from the three uses, all signed or recomputed in full, and carries a normative caveat that binds any future unsigned-root or partial-proof use. CLM-wholeness-witness has a conformance test.
- 6.1.2 noise floor: the predicate is made procedure-relative ((R, ε), adversary A₀). The text explicitly refuses the unbounded side-information limb and says 'aggregation is not anonymization'. As a definition this is stated no stronger than it can bear.
- 6.2.1 and 6.2.5 scope and falsification: they name the symmetry of the geometry, the unproven k→k_eff splice, the informativeness condition κ·r²·k_eff < V(0)e^{−λ_geo·k_eff}, and that the 'selective' direction is not a prediction of the framework. This is the most self-limiting writing in the book.

**Presenter: partial (and what it leans on)**
- 6.2.1 collapse bound (CLM-math-collapse). The register row is honest: 'Remainder bound only … of an ASSUMED decay law with substrate-specific free constants'. The vendored note on collapseUpperBound says it 'axiomatizes framework primitives λ_geo, κ'. It leans on upstream coherence-ratchet@0c4edfb Lean source, which is not in this repository, and on bench:RATCHET#8. Without the source the reader cannot tell whether remainder_scales_with_k_eff is substantive or holds by unfolding a defined bound.
- 6.2.3 σ rule (CLM-sigma-semigroup). The row's summary covers the whole event-time group-share rule: per-signal multiplication non-conformant, recurrence only a consequence, linear decay non-conformant. The only witness is sigma_decay_semigroup (plus nonneg and J_nonneg_under_decay). The cross-implementation conformance points Δt ∈ {1,25,400}, including an interior signal, are an open obligation at CIRISConformance#57 and no test is cited.
- 6.2.3.1 source grouping. The partition of signals into groups g 'is not yet pinned' (RATCHET#17), and the mechanized objects take (n_src, ρ̄_src) as inputs. σ conformance therefore holds only for singleton or declared groupings, as the text itself says.
- 6.2.3.1 attestation requirement (CLM-sigma-attest) is staged on CIRISServer#536, a bulk designation ticket that carries many unrelated rows. The w = 0 rule for free gratitude has no implementation evidence yet.
- 6.1.2 to 6.1.2.3 aggregation tier. The text says the vector family is closed at v2 and that v3 (the R9 constants, mass_commitment, the fail-closed rule for version < 3) 'require a re-cut vector family before CC 6.1.2 is 1.0 at v3'. Yet CLM-noise-*, CLM-aggregationmetav1, CLM-neff-dominance-gate and CLM-multiplicity-gate are all established. The reader should take these as established at test_540/541/542 and impl level, not as cross-implementation-proven at v3.
- 6.1.4: 'Until a second implementation reproduces these byte-for-byte, the CC 6.1 shapes are pinned-but-unproven, RC-grade, not 1.0'. That holds for StorageBudgetV1/CorpusWantV1, the ALM topology, fountain claims and the A/V shapes. Only the 6.1.2 v2 family is claimed as reproduced cross-implementation (6.1.2.3). No operational benchmark exists for any 6.1 mechanism (6.1.4).
- 6.1.5.3 durability at every tier (CLM-durability-every-tier, CLM-audience-claimed-nodes): established on CIRISPersist impl rows, but both rows also carry staged:CIRISEdge#806. That is why the packet lists Edge#806 as an open ticket while the header counts only one staged row.
- 6.1.5.1 constants are restated from CIRISEdge fountain_defaults.rs @ d53c7d4, but CLM-registry-replication points at CIRISServer/src/holonomic/fountain_defaults.rs. The reader would need the repo-consolidation history to see these are the same file. The doc-comment discrepancy is open at CIRISEdge#438.

**Presenter: missing**
- The Lean source itself, or a pointer the reader can build: the repository vendors only a TSV inventory (constitution/vendor/evidence/coherence-ratchet.cc_lean.tsv) filtered on status==mechanized. The packet's 'Formal witnesses: none' is literally true. The reader gets no `lake build` recipe, no `#print axioms` output, and no statement that the cited theorems are free of `sorry`. The rc8 formal/guarantees model states 'no sorry' for itself, but nothing equivalent is said for coherence-ratchet.
- The exact Lean statement of collapseUpperBound and its hypotheses. 6.2.1 lists convexity, r < 0.5, Grassmannian-uniform orientations and k ≥ 3. A verifier needs to see which of these appear as Lean hypotheses and which are absorbed into axioms λ_geo, κ > 0.
- Any theorem behind 'λ_geo ≈ 2r' (6.2.1). Lean treats λ_geo as a free positive constant, so the ≈2r relation is unmechanised and unsourced.
- A uniqueness proof for 'among positive, measurable decay functions [exp] is the only one satisfying the semigroup law' (6.2.3). The cited sigma_decay_semigroup proves only that exp satisfies the law. Uniqueness is the measurable Cauchy functional equation, and no Lean object or citation is given for it.
- A proof or derivation of the O(log T) forever-memory storage claim (6.1.2). Keeping every level of a fan-in pyramid costs a geometric series, which is O(T). O(log T) needs bounded retention per level, and that hypothesis is not stated.
- A σ conformance vector, and a test for the Δt ∈ {1,25,400} plus interior-signal family (CIRISConformance#57).
- A vector that pins (R, ε = 0.05) for the default layered-codec fidelity metric. 6.1.2 says the pair is pinned by a CC 6.1.4 conformance vector, but 6.1.4's list of vector families does not include a noise-floor vector.
- Any proof for the WholenessWitness 'not exploitable in this construction's uses' argument (6.1.1). It is an informal security argument and is presented as a rationale. That is acceptable, but a verifier will note that nothing about it is mechanised.
- The spectral effective-rank instrument that would price block-structured correlation (RATCHET#9/#10), and the LP pair-pinning gate for the side-informed adversary (#45). Both are named as future work only.

**Presenter: jargon**
- 6.1 opening and 6.1.3: CEG, '1+4', JCS, M-1, PQC, ML-DSA-65, 'steward-binding', 'quorum-merge', 'PIN-NORMATIVE' and CEWP-1.0 all appear without definition in the packet. These are protocol terms, outside a mathematician's budget, and the packet gives no glossary (the definitions live in Part 2, Part 5 and 8.1.1).
- 6.1 throughout: RaptorQ/RFC 6330, ChunkLayer, SFU, ALM, anti-entropy, cohort_scope, custody:ack:v1, consent:replication, hard_case:*, CVE-2012-2459. CVE-2012-2459 is explained only by allusion.
- 6.1.2.2: NcmecCsam, GifctCip and legal_basis appear as raw enum names.
- 6.2 intro and 6.2.1: 'Book IX', 'the Accord', PDMA, Wise-Authority, 'AIR-module limits', 'Conscience sensitivity' (used to define λ_op), and the CIRIS Capacity Score 𝒞_CIRIS. These are house vocabulary used inside a mathematical definition.
- 6.2.1: 'rationale space ℝ of dimension D' uses ℝ, the standard symbol for the reals, as a name. A mathematician will trip on this.
- 6.2.1: 'codimensions add' is asserted under 'independent, non-redundant (diverse)'. The precise condition is transversality or general position, and it is not named.
- 6.2.2 and 6.2.3.1: 'F-3 correlated-action matrix', 'Commons-Credits', 'Annex G', 'HE-300', RATCHET#17, CCA v5. These are cross-references a mathematician cannot resolve from this book.
- 6.1.2.1.2: 'Kish / inverse-Simpson participation ratio' is fine for this reader, but the text then calls k_eff a 'Kish identity' over a correlation. The two uses of 'Kish' (a mass-weighted design effect and the equicorrelated variance of a mean) are distinct objects and are not distinguished.

**Presenter: rc8 changes**
- The Part 6 text is unchanged at rc8. `git log v1.0-rc7..HEAD -- constitution/part_6_the_coherence_mathematics.md` is empty. The last edit, 10cbea3 (2026-10-05, the custody:ack:v1 holding claim, #141), landed before the rc7 tag.
- The Part 6 claim rows and evidence pins are unchanged since rc7: there is no diff on the 6.x rows in claims.tsv, and the coherence-ratchet pin is still 0c4edfb.
- New at rc8: the edition is cut into books (#123), and this packet (P08) was generated for the 'maths' volume by tools/build_review_piece.py. Its reviewer and standing line ('kill a result stated stronger than its proof', the original #72 scope) is new framing, not new content.
- Adjacent rc8 changes that touch this book only by reference: CC 3.1.5 run_kind (80e12ae); the [hybrid-sig] reference row added to 2.6.5, which points at 6.1.3 for the bound-hybrid signature (509b3d9); 1.13.3.3 adversary cases citing RATCHET-validated claims at their 8.6.1 grades (7bf0af9); and the rc8 formal/guarantees Lean model ('core only, no sorry'), which does not cover Part 6.

**Presenter: honest limits**
- 6.2.1: the displayed 'V(k) = V(0)·exp(−λ_geo·k_eff) + O(r²·k_eff)' is labelled theorem-given-model. The Lean object is an upper bound (≤ with an explicit κ·r²·k_eff), the decay law is assumed with λ_geo and κ axiomatized, and 6.2.5 itself says 'the decay law is assumed and only its remainder order is mechanized'. I would rewrite the display as an inequality and relabel the decay law as an assumed model, with only the remainder order at theorem-given-model.
- 6.2.1: the hypothesis lists disagree. 6.2.1 says Grassmannian-uniform orientations; 6.2.5 says 'near-orthogonal constraints'. I would align them and say which ones the Lean statement actually carries.
- 6.2.1: 'λ_geo ≈ 2r' should become a heuristic or an upstream estimate, since Lean takes λ_geo as a free positive constant.
- 6.2.3: 'Among positive, measurable decay functions it is the only one satisfying the semigroup law (theorem-given-model; sigma_decay_semigroup)'. The Lean object proves only that exp satisfies the law. I would soften this to 'exp satisfies the semigroup law (mechanized); uniqueness among measurable solutions is the classical Cauchy result (cited, not mechanized)'.
- CLM-sigma-semigroup: the established row's summary is the whole normative σ rule, but its only evidence is the semigroup lemma. I would narrow the summary to the semigroup and nonnegativity, or add the σ conformance test before calling the rule established.
- CLM-math-collapse: the vendored evidence still lists Core.Corridor.* and Core.Dynamics.* theorems under this claim, including corridor_keff_range_asymptotic, which 'rests on the corridorBounds axiom'. 6.2.2 withdraws the corridor, so those rows should leave the collapse claim's witness set.
- 6.1.2: 'Steady-state storage to remember all of history is O(log T)' needs its retention hypothesis (bounded residency per level) stated, or it should be softened to 'sublinear under bounded per-level retention'.
- 6.1 opening and 6.1.5.1: 'RaptorQ decode is all-or-nothing at block level … below the decode threshold a layer yields nothing'. RaptorQ is systematic, so received source symbols are themselves plaintext. 'Nothing' is true of decoding the block, not of what an adversary learns, so I would soften to 'the block does not decode'. That distinction matters for the noise-floor privacy reading.
- 6.1.5.1: 'sits in the RaptorQ O(N²)-decode sweet spot (microsecond scale)' and 'K=6 is RFC 6330's empirical overhead profile' are unsourced performance claims, and no bench exists (the text says so in the same section). I would soften both or cite them.
- 6.1.2.3 'Conformance — proven cross-impl': true at v2 only. The current version is v3 and, by the section's own last sentence, needs a re-cut vector family. I would soften the heading to 'proven cross-impl at v2; v3 pending re-cut'.
- 6.1.2: 'pinned by a CC 6.1.4 conformance vector the same way RC1–RC7 are' should be softened until a noise-floor (R, ε) vector appears in the 6.1.4 list.

## P09 — Appendices: glossary, translation, concerns ledger, interop profiles, references, stubs

**Reader:** an editor and external-review coordinator, with a standards person reading 8.4  
**Verdict:** needs work  
**One change:** Rebuild 8.3.4 so each row can be checked against evidence. Split it into two tables, 'Closed' and 'Mitigated / partial / acknowledged / staged', and move the delivery axis, endpoint schemas, occurrence_id, G2 and G3 out of the closed table. Give every row a claim id and register status column, so 'closed in text' and 'closed in evidence' read differently. Re-point the holds_bytes row to custody:ack:v1. In the same commit, set 8.9 to 'Open stubs: 1' (8.6.4), list WITNESS_KIND_REGISTRY and the 2.6.5 to 8.6.1 informational citations as open references, and remove 'nothing referenced remains undefined' from the 8.8 preamble. That gives a coordinator a ledger to audit instead of a list of section pointers.

**Promise (presenter):** This book is the back matter that should let you check the rest of the edition without taking our word for anything. It promises five things. First, one place where the load-bearing terms are defined (8.1.1), with friendly story names mapped back to the wire names they stand for (8.1.2–8.1.4). Second, a stated discipline for when a claim belongs in the wire and when it should be left untranslated (8.2). Third, a register of concerns that sorts every known weakness as a bet, an exposure, deferred, an overlap or closed, and for each closed gap names the section that closes it (8.3). Fourth, a boundary profile for outside standards, labelled informative: use CEG inside and the shared standard at the edge, with C2PA written out in full, stubs for COSE, RFC 9421, SD-JWT VC and KEYTRANS, and as of rc8 a mapping onto the SCITT roles (8.4). Fifth, references, the grades for reading evidence, the migrated Accord annexes A–J, and a stub registry that says 0 open (8.6, 8.8, 8.9). Read it as a map of where things stand. It is not a certificate that they are true. Several of its own "closed" and "0 open" labels do not hold up, and I point to each one below.

**Reviewer: kills**
- 8.9: kill "Open stubs: 0." 8.6.4 in the same book reads '[source content to migrate ... not present in this snapshot]', and 8.6.3 says WITNESS_KIND_REGISTRY.md is 'cited by name only'. The count is at least 1, and 2 if the by-name-only citation is counted.
- 8.8 preamble: kill "The stub registry at CC 8.9 records that nothing referenced remains undefined." This is false for the same reason, and also because 2.6.5 (part_2 line 592) sends Magnifica Humanitas and the Ubuntu literature to 8.6.1, and neither is listed in 8.6.1.
- 8.3.4 header: kill "These are settled." The table that follows contains MITIGATED (G2, G3), PARTIALLY CLOSED, ACKNOWLEDGED and STAGED-STREAMING rows. A header that calls all of them settled is the 'closed gap still open' my standing exists to catch.
- 8.3.4 delivery-axis row: kill "CLOSED-OBSERVER-SHARE / STAGED-STREAMING" as a row in a closed-gaps table. 8.3.3 says the streaming half is 'impl substrate-pending', with RC1-1b '(Still owed.)', RC1-1c an unmade constraint migration and RC1-7 unratified constants. Half of this gap is open.
- 8.3.4 endpoint response schemas row: kill "PARTIALLY CLOSED in CC 5.3.6 ... full OpenAPI committed." 8.3.6 lists 'Full OpenAPI export for all endpoints' as deferred to Phase E. A commitment is not a closure.
- 8.3.4 occurrence_id row: kill "ACKNOWLEDGED in CC 2.1 + R6 above" in the closed-gaps table. R6 (8.3.1) is an open bet, and 8.3.6 defers the fleet-attestation primitive that would close it to a workshop.
- 8.3.4 holds_bytes row: kill the row label "holds_bytes full-SHA verify + TTL — CLOSED in CC 5.3.2.5 / CC 5.3.2.1". holds_bytes was retired at rc7 (#141). part_5 line 210 now speaks custody:ack:v1 and records that the old consumer-withdraws behaviour was retired. The closure may survive, but the row certifies a primitive that no longer exists and needs re-pointing before it can be called closed.
- 8.3.2: kill "The moderation surface is **complete**". The same sentence says admission enforcement into the safety subsystem is still to be done, and no ticket is named. 'Specified' is the strongest word the text supports.
- 8.4.2 and 8.8.5 Annex C, Art 50 row: kill the Status "Evidence-bearing" on a statutory row, together with "it is what discharges EU AI Act Art. 50(2) at a media boundary". Annex C §3 says statutory rows stay Informative until legal review, and the media limb (CLM-c2pa-emit) is staged and dormant. The row presents an open compliance limb as closed.
- 8.4.2: kill "C2PA has no consent architecture, no revocation, no 1+4". 'No revocation' is likely wrong as stated (C2PA signing certificates are subject to revocation and status checking), and the sentence is a comparative claim against another system, which the house rules forbid. Restate it as what a C2PA manifest does not carry from CEG.

**Reviewer: unanswered**
- Is every term defined once? Not answerable from this book. 8.1.1 has seven rows. Contribution, WA, PDMA, WBD, ST, CIS, RM, SI/CS, MH, HE-300, H3ERE, M-1, HITL/HOTL/HOOTL, Policy E/I/M, T2/T5, GenesisBundle, NodeCore, k_eff, rho-bar and sigma have no row and no pointer to where they are defined. 8.6.1 Known limits (1) says no tool checks terms against 8.1.1. So 'defined once' is neither stated nor verifiable.
- Is every closed gap really closed? 8.3.4 gives only section pointers. It has no claim id and no register status per closure, so 'closed in text' cannot be told apart from 'closed in evidence'. Several rows rest entirely on other books (G2 and G3 on Part 4, the v1.4 T-3 rows on Parts 1 and 3).
- Which version of C2PA? None is named, and there is no informative reference entry for C2PA or for CAWG (which 8.4.2.2 uses without expanding it).
- No IETF or COSE reference list: no informative entries for COSE (RFC 9052/9053), deterministic CBOR (RFC 8949), RFC 9421, SD-JWT VC, OpenID4VP, KEYTRANS or the SCITT agent-execution drafts. RFC 9943 appears only as an inline link, and I could not confirm the number from the packet.
- No conformance artefacts for any profile: no test vector for the C2PA evidence_refs shape, for the COSE re-sign or for the SCITT mapping. 8.4.3 says the COSE export waits on the ML-DSA COSE registrations.
- Is the namespace manifest still deferred? 8.2.1 and 8.3.6 defer FSD/CEG/dimensions.json to the '1.0 lock' and 'Phase E', but manifests/namespace_registry.json ships with CLM-wire-vocabulary established. The book does not say whether this deferral has been discharged.
- Are annexes F–J binding? 8.8 does not say whether they bind a CEWP node or CIRIS agent today. It calls them 'included for completeness', yet they carry MUSTs (the TX-10 attestation, the lexwatcher.py feed list, the CEP export, the hardware interlock at A4) and KPIs that have no evidence row.
- Is the RFC 9421 export post-quantum? The 8.4.3 row signs outbound HTTP with 'existing Ed25519 keys', which drops the ML-DSA half of the interior's bound-hybrid signature at the edge. The text does not say so.

**Reviewer: confusions**
- evidence_refs shape: in 8.1.5 evidence_refs is an array of strings ('sha256:abc123...'). In 8.4.2.1 it is an array of objects {kind, locator, manifest_sha256, claim_generator, validation}. I cannot tell which is the wire form, or whether 'kind' is a field the grammar defines. 8.4.2.1 also cites 'lowercase hex per §0.6', and no §0.6 exists in this book.
- Wrong cross-references in 8.4.1: MLS/TreeKEM is cited as 'CC 3.1.5' linking to part_5, but part_3 3.1.5 is the CIRISAgent accord/DMA namespace and the MLS rekey is CC 5.1 (2.6.5 [RFC-9420] says so). RFC 6962/9162 logs are cited as 'CC 3.1', but the log is at 5.3.1. 'CC 2.4 records this decision' links to part_3. 8.4.2.2 refers to 'the CC 8.4.1 COSE export profile', but COSE is an 8.4.3 stub. 8.4.3 repeats 'the CC 3.1 RFC 6962 abstraction'.
- Normative inside an informative section: 8.4 declares itself 'whole ... informative', while 8.4.2 says 'emit normative' and 8.4.2.2 carries a MUST. The explanation that the obligation lives at 3.4.14 is there, but a standards reader expects the MUST in one place only.
- SCITT mapping: 'Receipt — the cosigned tree head'. In SCITT, a Receipt is a per-statement, COSE-signed proof of inclusion, not a tree head. As written the mapping is loose enough that a SCITT reviewer will reject it unless it says that the inclusion proof plus the cosigned head together form the receipt. 'Transparency Service — not a single custodian' depends on independent witnesses, and the text does not say whether witnessed mode is deployed.
- 8.4.3 COSE row: 'any IETF JOSE/COSE verifier'. JOSE and COSE are different families, and the row is about COSE_Sign1 only.
- Stale staging ticket: 8.4.2 and 8.8.5 say the C2PA emit is staged 'against CIRISConstitution#9', but the register row CLM-c2pa-emit says staged:CIRISServer#536, and its summary still says 'as of rc5'.
- Claim anchors: CLM-conscience-faculty is anchored at 8.8.6 (Annex F), but its content is 8.8.10 (Annex J), whose own text says '(CC 8.8.6)'. CLM-wire-vocabulary is anchored at '8.8', which does not discuss wire-vocabulary governance.
- Annex I §4 errors a standards reader will catch: 'Art 16 Human Oversight' (human oversight is Art 14, and Annex C itself maps Art 16 to provider obligations); 'Art 13 Transparency — Universal' (Art 13 applies to high-risk systems); §4.1 'KPIs F-T-1 through F-T-5 (Annex G)' (the F-T KPIs are Annex H §3).
- Steward sense: 8.1.1 requires every use of 'Steward' to read under exactly one sense, but 8.3.4 G2 '1-of-6 accord/steward sign-off' and 8.3.6 '2-of-3 steward sign-off' do not say which sense they mean.
- Undefined terms used inside definitions or rows: Edge/Reticulum, founder-quorum, cohort_subkind, is_steward_bound(K) (8.1.1); HITL/HOTL, Portal/Verify, LANGUAGE_PRIMER, D26 ext (8.1.3–8.1.4); Policy M/E/I, T2, T5, GenesisBundle, Attestation-Ladder Composition (8.3.3–8.3.4); Sunlight-lineage, resolve_encryption_keys (8.4.3); MH, ST, CIS, RM, M-1, ACCORD §VII Ch2, ACCORD_UPDATE §2 (Annexes F–I).
- 8.3.1 lists 'R5 — Discharged' and 'R9 — Mitigated (shipped)' in a table titled 'named as bets'. It is unclear whether a discharged risk should move to 8.3.4. R8 is also printed after R9.
- Annex J §§0–8 (the HE-300 / 'Ethics Engine Enterprise API' harness, CI YAML and release thresholds) is migrated text with no register row. Annex H gates PATCH releases on 'HE-300 pass'. I cannot tell whether this harness runs, or whether the §4 admissibility standard applies to its numbers.
- Companion documents and primer are hosted in CIRISRegistry (8.2 LANGUAGE_PRIMER, 8.6.3), which the CHANGELOG says folds into CIRISServer. The links may rot.

**Reviewer: presenter kinder than the text**
- The presenter lists 8.4.2.1 and 8.4.2.3 as complete and says a standards reader 'can check this against C2PA directly'. That is not possible: no C2PA specification version is cited, the evidence_refs object shape contradicts the string form in 8.1.5, and '§0.6' is a dangling reference.
- The presenter lists the 8.4.3 SCITT mapping as partial only because it has no conformance vector. The text has a substantive mapping error the presenter did not raise: 'Receipt' is mapped to a cosigned tree head rather than to a per-statement inclusion proof. The JOSE/COSE conflation was not raised either.
- The presenter did not mention that the RFC 9421 export row signs with 'existing Ed25519 keys', which silently drops the post-quantum half of the interior signature at the edge. The algorithm-correspondence paragraph says the opposite for COSE.
- The presenter treats 8.3.4 'Delivery axis', 'occurrence_id' and 'endpoint response schemas' as 'at least the last three are not closed'. The text is weaker than that. The header calls all of them 'settled', the OpenAPI 'commitment' is contradicted by 8.3.6 in the same chapter, and no row carries a claim id. So a closure cannot be checked against evidence anywhere in 8.3.4, not only for three rows.
- The presenter puts the Art 50 'Evidence-bearing' status under honest_limits ('should not read as a compliance claim'). The text is unkinder: it violates Annex C's own §3 graduation rule, and 8.4.2.2 calls the C2PA emit 'what discharges EU AI Act Art. 50(2)' while that limb is staged and dormant.
- The presenter did not catch the Annex I statutory errors (Art 16 labelled as human oversight, Art 13 labelled 'Universal', F-T KPIs attributed to Annex G). A standards or legal reviewer will find these first.
- The presenter lists 8.8.10 §§1–5 as complete. The §4 'normative' admissibility standard sits inside an annex that 8.8 calls 'included for completeness', so its binding force is unclear. The CLM-conscience-faculty row backing §1 is staged, and its contrast is post-hoc.
- The presenter attributes the missing informational citations to 2.6.5 in general terms. Concretely, 2.6.5 names Magnifica Humanitas and the Ubuntu literature as appearing in 8.6.1, and neither does. That makes the 8.8 claim 'nothing referenced remains undefined' false a second way, independent of 8.6.4.
- The presenter lists 8.3.1 as complete. R5 'Discharged' and R9 'Mitigated (shipped)' sit in a table titled 'named as bets' with no rule for when a risk leaves the table. The lifecycle of a concern between 8.3.1 and 8.3.4 is not stated.

**Reviewer: answered**
- Defined once, for one word: 8.1.1 pins 'Steward' to three named senses (substrate steward, owner-steward, co-steward) and sets the rule 'Prose MUST be readable under exactly one sense'. This is the model the rest of the glossary should follow.
- Defined once, for seven terms: 8.1.1 defines CEG, CEWP, fabric node, coherence signal, ciris-canonical, FedCode and infohazard, each in one place, with the section that governs it.
- Narrative names traced back to wire names: 8.1.2–8.1.4 map every friendly leaf a reader meets in a story to its canonical wire form, so the bytes can always be recovered.
- Closed gaps are traceable to sections: 8.3.4 gives each gap a terminal status and a resolution pointer, so I can at least locate where each closure is claimed.
- Open items are named rather than hidden: 8.3.3 lists RC1-1b ('Still owed'), RC1-1c (the V054 constraint migration is 'not a pure index-add') and RC1-7, each with owner and gating. 8.3.1 R14 names the 4.4.2 plain-mean default as an rc9 question and does not claim it is closed.
- Where it touches standards, the principle: 8.4.1 says 'speak CEG inside, standards at the edge' and names four boundary modes (export, import bridge, already interior, not adopted), with the standards placed in each.
- Where it touches C2PA: 8.4.2.1 and 8.4.2.3 import a manifest as an evidence_refs citation. It is checked by a C2PA verifier and never by a CEG verify path, validation is advisory, invalid or absent C2PA is recorded rather than fatal, and no new subject_kind is added. 8.4.2.2 says the generated-media MUST lives at 3.4.14 R3.
- Where it touches IETF and COSE: 8.4.3 gives dispositions for RFC 9421 / Web Bot Auth, COSE_Sign1 / deterministic CBOR, SD-JWT VC / OpenID4VP and tiled logs / KEYTRANS, plus an informative SCITT role mapping (Issuer, Signed Statement, Transparency Service, Receipt, Registration Policy, Relying Party) with lifecycle and algorithm correspondences.
- How to read evidence: 8.6.1 gives four strength grades (identity, theorem-given-model, measured, cited-external), each with what it does not license, and a per-claim reading key. It states that terminology validation against 8.1.1 is not implemented (Known limits (1)).
- 8.8.10 §4 sets a normative admissibility standard for judge-scored numbers: kappa >= 0.7, recall and precision >= 80% on the pivotal class, adversarial-flip sensitivity, and the two-axis form.

**Presenter: complete**
- 8.2 Translation discipline (8.2.1–8.2.4): the decision tree, the five families, the four strict verdicts and the T-1/T-2/T-3 taxonomy are self-contained and reviewable now. The one stale line is noted under missing (dimensions.json).
- 8.3.1 Acknowledged risks R1–R14: each row states the bet and the residual. R5 is marked discharged and gives its residual (TPM vendor anchors deliberately unbaked). R9 states its unverified limb and the strength of the proposed instrument. R10 points to the wager ledger CIRISConstitution#84. R14 (new at rc8) names the plain-mean default in 4.4.2 as an open rc9 question instead of claiming it is closed.
- 8.3.3 Observer-share vs streaming split: the three items still open on the streaming side (RC1-1b, RC1-1c, RC1-7) are listed with owner and gating, and the Persist constraint-migration caveat is said out loud rather than presented as purely additive.
- 8.3.5–8.3.7 First-adopter exposures F1/F2, the deferred-workshop table and overlaps O1–O4 are stated plainly. The deferred table keeps the discharged hardware-attestation row so the change from the 1.0 surface stays visible.
- 8.4.1 Governing principle and the four boundary modes (export, import bridge, already interior, not adopted), plus the disposition rule for legally recognised records (in-grammar and bridged, never one or the other).
- 8.4.2.1 and 8.4.2.3 C2PA import as an evidence_refs citation: the C2PA signature is checked by a C2PA verifier and never by a CEG verify path, a validation result is advisory, invalid or absent C2PA is recorded and not fatal, and no new subject_kind or primitive is added. A standards reader can check this against C2PA directly.
- 8.6.1 Strength grades (identity, theorem-given-model, measured, cited-external) and the per-claim reading key: each row says what the artifact does not license; the collapse bound is graded at the weaker of two disagreeing pins; the operating corridor carries no grade because none is claimed. The CCA authority note is explicit.
- 8.6.2 Lineage lives in CHANGELOG, VERSION and the tags, and is not restated in this book.
- 8.8.10 Annex J research evidence, sections 1–5: the tuned-domain result is reported with its CIs, what it does not establish is stated first, the transfer reading that cuts against us is reported, and a normative admissibility standard for judge-scored numbers is set (kappa >= 0.7, recall and precision >= 80% on the pivotal class, adversarial-flip sensitivity). The testimonial-class defect rule is set at 8.8.10 §5.
- corpus: CLM-migration-fidelity is established on validation/results.csv (0 REJECT under the skeptical rubric). That certifies that the A–J annex migration in 8.8 is faithful to its source. It does not certify the annexes' content.

**Presenter: partial (and what it leans on)**

WA, ST, CIS, RM, SI/CS, MH, HE-300, H3ERE, k_eff, rho-bar, sigma, Contribution and HUMANITY_ACCORD have no glossary row. So 'every term defined once' is not true of 8.1. **Presenter: honest limits**
- 8.3.2: 'categorically stronger (a genuine first)', 'CEG is the only model that fails secure', 'All 9 surveyed networks ... fail open'. These are comparative claims against named systems, which breaks the house rule. Soften to what CEG does (4.5.4 named-moderator invariant: a group cannot exist without an accountable moderator) without ranking it.
- 8.3.5 F1 'No prior system separates earned standing from purchasable token at scale' and 8.3.1 R11–R13 ('Secure Scuttlebutt hit exactly this wall', 'Spamhaus', 'No surveyed system survived its first emergency use'). These are comparative or survey claims with no citation in 8.6.1. Either cite the survey or restate each as our own bet.
- 8.4.2: 'C2PA has no consent architecture, no revocation, no 1+4'. This is comparative, and 'no revocation' is likely wrong as stated: C2PA signing certificates are subject to revocation and status checking. Restate as 'CEG's consent and revocation semantics are not carried by a C2PA manifest'.
- 8.4.2 / 8.8.5 Art 50(2) posture: 'the marking is by construction ... cannot be silently stripped inside the federation'. Keep the qualifier 'inside the federation' prominent. Once text leaves the federation, no marking travels with it, and that limb (CLM-c2pa-emit) is staged and dormant. Rows are 'Informative' pending legal review (8.8.5 §3), so 'Evidence-bearing' on the Art 50 row should not read as a compliance claim.
- 8.3.4: 'These are settled' over a table containing MITIGATED, PARTIALLY CLOSED, ACKNOWLEDGED and STAGED-STREAMING rows. Retitle it, or split the closed rows from the rest.
- 8.9: 'Open stubs: 0' and 'nothing referenced remains undefined' (8.8 preamble). This is false while 8.6.4 is a placeholder and WITNESS_KIND_REGISTRY is cited by name only (8.6.3). Should read 1 open, or 8.6.4 should be filled.
- 8.3.2: 'The moderation surface is complete'. Soften to 'specified', since admission enforcement into the safety subsystem is still implementation work.
- 8.8.10 §1: 'The machinery carries the bulk of the effect'. This is one battery, one agent version and one provider, the content contrast is post-hoc, and the row is staged (CLM-conscience-faculty on #88). The text already says most of this. Keep that wording, and do not quote the -31.6 headline without it.
- 8.6.1 grades table: the collapse-bound row is graded at the weaker of two disagreeing pins, which is honest. The register still has no strength column (8.6.1 'Known limits' (2)), so an external reader sees 'established' on rows whose grade is identity only.
- 8.8.6 / 8.8.7: the 30-odd 'established' CLM-await-* rows can be misread as evidencing Annex F/G. They certify agent code symbols only. Annex G's red team is 'owed' (§3.1) and its KPIs have no evidence row. Say so at the head of 8.8.7.
- 8.4.3 SCITT mapping: 'Transparency Service — not a single custodian' depends on independent witnesses. The rc7 cut notes the shipped roots run with witnessed mode off and an empty witness directory. The mapping should say the multi-witness property is specified but not yet deployed.
- 8.4.3: RFC 9943 is cited as the SCITT architecture RFC number. A standards reader should confirm the number before any external submission (a draft-ietf-scitt-architecture citation is the safe fallback). The same applies to the 2.6.5 [SFrame] row, which still cites the draft although SFrame is published as RFC 9605.

## P10 — The Register (claims.tsv, pins, EVIDENCE)

**Reader:** an auditor or regulator, and a component author  
**Verdict:** needs work  
**One change:** Make each established row carry a resolution record that an auditor can check without the CIRIS team: the repository and commit where the cited symbol actually lives (not only the manifest's host repository), the SYMBOL or DECIMAL grade, and the checker's verdict. Add a check that the cited path#symbol exists in the source tree at that commit, not only in a manifest row. Under that check, 3.1.9.1 CLM-contributions fails today (its symbol was removed in CIRISPersist ca6bdf60). The 44 rows that rest only on impl:CIRISServer/ paths absent at c70f96bb would have to be re-pointed to the repository that owns them at its pinned commit. CIRISClient would need a pin, or CLM-notify would drop to staged.

**Promise (presenter):** For every claim the Constitution makes, this book tells an auditor which section it lives in, which status it holds, and which artefact backs it. "Established" means at least one code symbol, test, proof or benchmark resolves at a recorded commit. "Staged" means a named open ticket is to supply the artefact and none resolves yet. "Normative" means the text settles the rule and no outside artefact exists. The pins table gives the commit of each of the eight component repositories that the artefacts were checked against. In short, the book promises that the auditor can follow any claim to a specific artefact at a specific commit, and that a status the artefact does not support is this book's defect and not the text's. The promise is real and checked by machine (tools/check_claims.py). However, what "established" proves is narrower than the word suggests: for 293 of the 341 established rows, it means the component's own manifest at the pinned commit publishes the named symbol at that section. It does not mean an independent test of behaviour passed.

**Reviewer: kills**
- 3.1.9.1 CLM-contributions, status 'established' on `impl:CIRISServer/src/federation/blobs.rs#holds_bytes_attestation_envelope`. Kill: the pin no longer resolves. The path does not exist in CIRISServer at c70f96bb. The server manifest attributes it to CIRISPersist at ciris-persist@v17.0.1. CIRISPersist at its own pin ca6bdf60 lists that symbol under 'Removed (clean break, no aliases)' in its CHANGELOG, and blobs.rs:4618 calls it 'the retired holds_bytes_attestation_envelope'. The register's own 2.4 CLM-row-types-closed says holds_bytes was retired to custody:ack. This row is established on a symbol that the pinned code deleted.
- 4.2.1.2 CLM-notify, status 'established' on `impl:CIRISServer/client/shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/.../AccordScreen.kt#invocationBadge`. Kill: the path does not exist in CIRISServer at c70f96bb. The manifest row assigns it to CIRISClient at ciris-client@0.5.228, and CIRISClient has no pin. A status cannot rest on a repository the pins table does not pin.
- The 44 established rows whose only evidence is an impl:CIRISServer/ path that is absent at c70f96bb (list from the register: CLM-subject_kind 3.3.2, CLM-subject_kind-subject, CLM-family-subject, CLM-consent-subject, CLM-identity, CLM-encryption_pubkeys, CLM-accord-reservation, CLM-system, CLM-family-self, CLM-detector-only, CLM-witness-emitter, CLM-identity-set, CLM-consent-counter, CLM-concurrent-write, CLM-community-three, CLM-family-policy, CLM-cascade, CLM-multi-subject, CLM-axis, CLM-takedown, CLM-envelope-multi, CLM-holder, CLM-merge, CLM-attestation-tier, CLM-local, CLM-two, CLM-admission-pqc, CLM-stream, the five realtime_av rows, CLM-delivery, CLM-normative, CLM-welcome-wrap, CLM-announce-suppress, CLM-registry-replication, CLM-deterministic-alm, among others). I would not kill the claims. I would kill the phrase 'an artefact in a pinned commit backs it' (packet line 27) as applied to them. The artefact sits in a different repository at a version that is not pinned, and in some cases (ciris-server@main) at a branch, not a commit. Spot check: the symbols I tried (check_reserved_prefix_admission, precedence_winner, process_takedown_admission) do exist in CIRISPersist at ca6bdf60, so the fault is mostly in the pointer, not in the code. That fault is still mine to kill.
- 8.8 CLM-wire-vocabulary, status 'established' on `test:@manifests/WIRE_VOCABULARY.md`. Kill the 'test' tag. It is a Markdown document whose existence is checked, not a test that ran.
- corpus CLM-migration-fidelity, status 'established' on `test:@validation/results.csv`. Kill the 'test' grade. It is a model-graded rubric result whose file existence is checked, at no stated commit.
- 8.8.9 CLM-discrimination-gate, 'established' with the summary 'a request without one bypasses the gate'. The status overstates the claim unless the claim is restated as 'for requests carrying a capability'.
- 3.4.13 CLM-child-protection, 'established' with the summary 'conformance vectors superseded ... awaiting the substrate age-p…'. The row says itself that the evidence that was established is gone and it is waiting. The single symbol age.rs#is_minor does not carry 'seven child-protection rulings'. Demote to staged, or narrow the summary.
- 3.4.14 CLM-synthesis-disclosure, 'Machine-generation marking is mandatory ... universal', 'established' on `edge_runtime.py#initialize_edge_runtime` alone. A runtime initialiser does not evidence a universal marking duty. The agent manifest comment says it 'registers THIS engine's own signer'. Narrow the claim to the signer registration, or add the marking artefact.
- 4.2.1.1 CLM-halt-one-row, 'established' with the clause 'agents check the latch before every effectful act', while its own evidence cell carries `staged:CIRISAgent#1231`. The status claims the agent half. Split the row, or mark it established in part.
- 4.5.8.3 CLM-settled 'Settled in CIRISAgent, carried as-is' staged on CIRISServer#536, and 4.5.10.4 CLM-documents-what-2 'What this documents' staged on CIRISServer#536. These are not claims, and the staging ticket does not match the text. Remove them, or write them as claims.
- 3.1.2 CLM-nsproc-cosign-transparency-log-cosigned-tree-size, 'established' on `operational.rs#check_skew_bound`. A clock-skew check is not evidence about cosigned tree size. This looks like a DECIMAL-grade match presented as an artefact.

**Reviewer: unanswered**
- 'At which commit' for the code itself. tools/check_claims.py (lines 5-7, 155) resolves pointers only against the vendored manifest, never against the source tree. The CIRISServer manifest at c70f96bb lists rows owned by other repositories at other versions: 40 rows CIRISPersist at ciris-persist@v17.0.1 (the persist pin is v55), 22 ciris-verify-core@v10.1.1, 15 at the branch name 'ciris-server@main', and 1 in CIRISClient at ciris-client@0.5.228. A pointer written impl:CIRISServer/... therefore points at code whose commit appears nowhere in the pins table.
- Of the 92 claim ids with an impl:CIRISServer/ pointer, 76 name a file path that does not exist in CIRISServer at c70f96bb (for example src/federation/admission.rs, src/transport/realtime_av.rs, src/ciris-verify-core/src/humanity_accord.rs, src/ciris-crypto/src/scope_privacy.rs). 44 established rows rest only on such pointers (for example 3.3.3, 3.4.1, 3.4.7, 3.5.1, 4.2.1.1 CLM-invocation, 4.4.3.4, 5.3.3.1, 5.4.4, 6.1.2.2, 6.1.8).
- 4.2.1.2 CLM-notify is backed by a Kotlin file the CIRISServer manifest attributes to CIRISClient. CIRISClient has no row in evidence_pins.tsv, so this artefact has no pinned commit at all.
- The resolution grade (SYMBOL or DECIMAL) of each row. The checker computes it (check_claims.py lines 9-19, 814), but the packet does not show it.
- The checker's result at this commit: errors, warnings, pending rows, whether the ticket-state gate ran, and whether pin staleness ran (it needs gh and is only a warning, lines 553-589).
- The full evidence cell. The packet truncates it, so the staged half of 4.2.1.1 CLM-halt-one-row (staged:CIRISAgent#1231) and the second and third pointers on many rows cannot be seen.
- The commit of in-repo '@' artefacts (formal/authority_tree, validation/results.csv, manifests/). No CIRISConstitution commit or tag is given.
- The definition of status and tags. EVIDENCE.md is not in the packet, and the cover still mentions an 'open' status that no row uses.
- When each pin was taken, and the CI state at the pin. The packet does not mention that Agent, Verify and Edge date from re-pin 14, or that the CIRISServer c70f96bb Windows lane was red.

**Reviewer: confusions**
- 'Pinned commit'. I was told the artefact is in a pinned commit. What is pinned is the commit of a manifest file, and that manifest can declare code in another repository at another version, or at 'main'. Neither the packet nor the pins table says so. The pins table in the packet also leaves out the 'vendored' column, so I could not tell that the check reads a local copy.
- 'backed when' predicates ('impl (repo!=— and crate!=open)', 'status==green') are code, not definitions, and they do not say that crate@version may differ from the pinned commit.
- The 'impl' tag is applied to test files (the CLM-await-* rows point at tests/.../test_*.py) and to a JSON registry (3.1.7 CLM-registry-of-record impl:@manifests/namespace_registry.json). So 'impl' and 'test' are not reliable as kinds of artefact.
- The packet's cover lists 'open' as a status, and EVIDENCE.md says open mirrors CC 8.3.1, but no row uses it, and the R-bets (including R14) have no rows.
- 'designated in bulk, see designation issue' is repeated 51 times and never gives an issue number. '#62' and 'CC#67' are named without saying what was verified at designation.
- The 55 CLM-nsproc-* rows have slug summaries ('kind kind discriminator hard case kind'). I cannot tell what claim the artefact is supposed to support, so I cannot judge whether it supports it.
- One symbol backs many unrelated claims: check_trace_dimension_admission backs 5 rows, and BlobBackedSchemaResolver backs 3 (including 4.5.1.1 axis discipline). That is consistent with a section-level (DECIMAL) match, but no grade is shown.
- 1.13.3.3 now says 'addressed by construction' and 'mitigated' for two adversary classes, while its only row, CLM-adversary-classes, is normative-only. A statement that a mitigation exists is not normative, and it has no artefact.

**Reviewer: presenter kinder than the text**
- The presenter said 'established' means a symbol 'resolves at a recorded commit' and that 'the auditor can follow any claim to a specific artefact at a specific commit'. That is kinder than the facts. Resolution is against the manifest at that commit, and the CIRISServer manifest attributes most of its rows to Persist v17.0.1, Verify v10.1.1, 'ciris-server@main' and unpinned CIRISClient. 76 of 92 server-pointer claim ids name paths that do not exist at c70f96bb.
- The presenter listed 'Pins table: eight repositories, each with a commit' as complete. It is not complete: CIRISClient carries an established artefact (4.2.1.2 CLM-notify) and has no pin.
- The presenter's honest-limits list did not include any row whose artefact has been removed. 3.1.9.1 CLM-contributions is established on a symbol that CIRISPersist ca6bdf60 records as removed ('clean break, no aliases'). That is a dead pin, not a softening.
- The presenter framed the narrowness of 'established' as manifest-published versus behaviour-tested. The bigger gap is that the published entry can refer to an older version of different code than the one pinned.

**Presenter: missing**
- The resolution grade of each row. check_claims.py separates SYMBOL (the named artefact is published) from DECIMAL (only that the manifest backs something at this section), but the packet shows neither, so an auditor cannot tell which established rows were checked against a specific symbol.
- The full evidence cell. The packet truncates it at about 110 characters, which hides staged halves (for example halt-one-row's CIRISAgent#1231) and second and third artefacts.
- The commit for each row. The reader has to join the repository named in each pointer to evidence_pins.tsv by hand. In-repo '@' pointers (formal/, validation/, manifests/) have no CIRISConstitution commit or tag stated anywhere in the packet.
- When each pin was taken, and whether it is current. Agent 73d6fde, Verify 44d9dbf and Edge 7057fc2 date from re-pin 14 (2026-10-07), while Persist and Server date from re-pins 16 and 17. The pin-staleness check exists but needs gh, and it is a warning only.
- The checker's result at this commit: pass or fail, warnings, pending-manifest rows, whether the open-ticket check ran, and pin staleness. The packet presents the table but not the verification of it.
- CI status of the pinned commits. CHANGELOG records that the Windows lane was red on CIRISServer c70f96bb (a path-handling fault in the scan itself, fixed in CIRISServer#768 for the next pin). The packet's pins table shows nothing about this.
- EVIDENCE.md, which defines the statuses and tags, is not included as an extra file. The packet's cover paraphrases it ('staged (an open ticket will)'), and its own header still says 'CC 1.0-rc4' with 'Until those land' language.
- What 'backed' means for each kind of artefact, in plain words. For impl it means the component's own manifest lists the symbol at the section. It does not mean a behavioural test passed, and the reader is not told so.
- A cross-reference from claims to the P00 guarantees (G-rows) and their grades, for example G7 'witnessed' against an 'established' halt-one-row.
- The designation issue for the 51 CLM-await-* rows ('designated in bulk, see designation issue' never gives a number), and what CC#67 designation and #62 'namespace-processor' minting each verified.
- A list of staged tickets with owner and state. Ticket concentration (CIRISServer#536 on 21 rows, CIRISAgent#1250 on 8, CIRISPersist#803 on 6) is visible only by counting.
- An 'open' status row for any acknowledged gap or R-bet: EVIDENCE.md says open mirrors CC 8.3.1, yet no row uses it, and R14 from the rc8 threat model is not mirrored.

**Presenter: honest limits**
- Throughout the register, 'established' should read 'implemented: symbol published in the component's own manifest at the pinned commit' for the 293 impl-first rows. That is a statement about where the code is, not an independent conformance result. Only the 43 test-first and 5 lean-first rows carry a check result.
- 4.2.1.1 CLM-halt-one-row: present it as 'established for Verify and Server; agent half staged (CIRISAgent#1231)'. G7 is still graded witnessed in the CHANGELOG, and an auditor reading 'established' will take it that the halt reaches agents.
- The eight established rows that still carry a staged token (4.4.3.8, 6.1.5.3 x2, 4.2.1.1 x2, 3.1.2, 3.2 x2): soften these to 'established in part' and name the staged half.
- corpus CLM-migration-fidelity is 'established' on test:@validation/results.csv. That file is a model-graded skeptical-rubric result, and the checker only confirms that an in-repo file exists. Soften to 'validated by rubric review'.
- 8.8 CLM-wire-vocabulary uses 'test' evidence that is a Markdown file (@manifests/WIRE_VOCABULARY.md), checked only for existence, not by a test.
- 8.8.9 CLM-discrimination-gate is 'established' while its summary says a request without a capability bypasses the gate. The status should state that scope.
- 3.4.13 CLM-child-protection is 'established' while its summary says the conformance vectors were superseded and it is 'awaiting the substrate age-p[lane]'. Re-check the status against what the age.rs#is_minor symbol carries.
- 4.2.2.1 CLM-hardware-class-hardware establishes an 'acknowledged gap'. The status certifies the code that admits a self-asserted hardware class, not that the gap is closed. Say so.
- The 51 CLM-await-* and 55 CLM-nsproc-* rows (106 of 341 established) were designated in bulk with slug summaries. Present them as 'symbol located at section', not as reviewed claims, until each has a human-readable summary.
- 1.13.3.3 'coordinated reviewer capture: mitigated' rests on 6.2.3.1 clique_neutralization, which holds as a theorem given the model. Do not let the register's 'established' on CLM-sigma-srccorr read as evidence that capture is mitigated in operation. R14 has no row.
- The packet's evidence-kind line (impl 293, lean 5, normative-only 26, staged 79, test 43) counts only each row's first token. It hides mixed rows and the single bench pointer (6.2.1 RATCHET#8). Say so beside the counts.
- Pins: present the pin table as 'artefacts resolved against these commits at re-pins 14 to 17', not as 'current'. Three repositories are a week older, and CIRISServer c70f96bb carried a red Windows lane at pin time (the scan's own path fault, CIRISServer#768).
